# What changed

Newest first. One version number covers the desktop app, the phone app and
the backend files (the `VERSION` file). Builds made by GitHub add a build
number as the last part - `0.2.57` is a build of 0.2.

## Not in a numbered version yet

- **A home-screen Jarvis widget keeps showing the widget its slot was given (2026-10-30).** On the release
  APK the shrinker merged the seven widget classes into two, and Glance tells the cards apart by class
  name - so "Jarvis widget 2" kept reading "No widget chosen" after the owner picked a widget for it, and
  the approval, quick-link and calendar cards could each repaint with another's content. The build now
  keeps every widget class distinct (app/proguard-rules.pro carries the dexdump evidence and the
  field-test symptom), and a unit test fails if a keep rule is ever removed.
- **A widget you built by chatting is one tap from your home screen (2026-10-30).** When a home-screen slot has a saved widget, Brain → Widgets now offers **Put it on my home screen**: the phone asks the launcher to place that slot's widget itself, instead of only telling you the touch-and-hold route. It always says which of three things happened - that widget is already there; the home screen was asked (a launcher may still show its own confirm or refuse); or this launcher cannot add widgets on request, in which case it gives the touch-and-hold route in words. It never claims the widget was placed when Android only accepted the request. Nothing is sent to the PC, no approval card is raised, and which widget a slot shows is still chosen in the slot picker. The app compiles and the JVM tests pin every sentence and all three receivers; the pin itself was not driven on a phone at this desk (none here), so that row stays NOT-TESTABLE until the field test.
- **A described widget can now be arranged four ways, and the phone stops offering two home-screen slots that could never draw (2026-10-30).** "Put my reminders at the top" or "make the count big" now picks one of four fixed arrangements - `stack` (the default, and what every existing widget already gets, so nothing on a home screen changes unless asked), `big` (the first number drawn large), `list` (the heading, then the lists, then the counts and bars) and `compact` (one heading, one thing and the buttons) - checked on the PC and drawn the same way by the desktop widget window and by "Jarvis widget" 1-3 on the phone. The list is CLOSED and always will be: Android fixes a widget's layout when the APK is built, so no server can add one, and the docs now say that plainly instead of promising a look Jarvis cannot draw. Nothing new is approved and nothing is sent anywhere new: same blocks, same sources, same five tile buttons, same App-lock hiding, same stale-link gate, no new route. Two real defects found while building it are fixed too: the phone's slot picker offered five slots while the app shipped three board receivers, so slots 4 and 5 could be chosen on that screen and never drawn (it offers three now, and a JVM test counts the manifest to keep the two equal), and `jarvis_widgets.py`'s own docstring named a `widgets.patch` that has never existed (the module ships whole and its routes are switched on by `jarvis_brain_reads.install()`). The arrangements are proved as rules by both apps' tests against the shared fixture; no widget has been seen drawn on a real launcher or an emulator yet.
- **Updating Jarvis now downloads the signed release instead of silently building from source, and no longer rebuilds after an update (2026-10-30).** The desktop update was taking the slow route for two measured reasons, and both are fixed. GitHub serves the release page's small manifest as a download rather than a web page, so Windows handed the script its raw bytes; the script piped those bytes straight into its JSON reader, which read one number per byte and found no version - so it concluded no release existed and built the app from source instead, silently, on every run. The ancestry check had the other half of the same trouble: it asked git whether the released commit was already in the folder's history, and that answer is yes BOTH when the folder is exactly the release and when it has moved past it, so a folder sitting on the released commit rebuilt instead of installing the signed release that is that same code. The reply is now decoded as text before it is parsed, and the comparison asks "equal" before it asks "older": a folder standing on the released commit installs the signed release, a folder with genuinely newer local commits still builds and is never downgraded, and an unzipped folder or an unrelated history still installs the published one and says it cannot tell. Every safety rule is unchanged - nothing unsigned is installed, nothing is approved for you, a download that does not verify is refused, and the backend keeps its backup and its rehearsal. Proved by `scripts\test-update-jarvis.ps1` in Windows PowerShell 5.1 (the owner's version), which lifts the functions out of the script as written and runs them against synthetic replies and a throwaway repository: 15 of its checks fail against the old script and all 21 pass against this one. What no desk can prove is one real round trip to the release page, which is the Lead's call.
- **A card for an action Jarvis has no risk line for can be approved again, with the strongest warning and the human check (2026-10-30).** On the owner's own PC that morning the app builder's build card appeared, he pressed Approve, and nothing happened: a risky card needs a Windows Hello prompt, a prompt may only ask about something Jarvis can name, and an action with no risk row and no written words was refused outright - a card that looked alive and could not be approved by anybody. Gemini's Q2 ruling made it a dual rule, and both halves are now built. **At runtime** an action whose `risk.classified` is false is given the worst case the gate has (permanent and outbound, so no swipe and no one-tap gesture on any screen), is forced to tier "ask" so it can never run with nobody asked, and its card carries one plain warning line - "Unclassified action - treated as permanent and able to leave this PC" - in `risk.why` and at the start of `notice.body`, so the desktop, the HUD and the phone all show it with no client change. The owner can then approve it, and only through the human check every risky card already needs (Windows Hello on this PC, the phone's own lock and signature from another device); a yes recorded without that check is refused by the gate itself, so nothing auto-approves and nothing approves in bulk. "What asks first" refuses to loosen such an action (409, nothing written) and offers no switch for it. **In CI** a shipped action that can run unattended and has no explicit risk row still fails, by name (`backend/test_gate_unknown_risk.py`), and `test_gate_risk_rows.py` is run unchanged beside it. The forced "ask" changes nothing the owner uses today because the EIGHT actions that had an `auto` line and no row - `calendar_read`, `email_read`, `notes_search`, `home_read`, `news_read`, `page_read`, `github_read`, `docker_service_stop` - were given their own rows in the same patch (the ninth of that shape, `lower_api_limit`, already has one from `chatbot-limits.patch`, so a second row would have been dead code and `test_gate_risk_words.py` caught exactly that); the tool literals the gate maps straight to a tier ("calculator" -> auto, "memory_store" -> notify) are exempt by name, from the gate's own table. Files: `backend/unknown-risk-approvable.patch` (`jarvis_gate.py`), and the SHIPPED `jarvis_owner_check.py`, `jarvis_asks_first.py` and `jarvis_card_words.py` (copied to their `jarvis-backend/` twins). Honest limit: nineteen actions that already only run on an "ask" line still have no risk row, so their cards keep the warning rather than a proper sentence - giving them rows is a separate, per-action job. Tests: `backend/test_gate_unknown_risk.py` (101 checks), including that a yes without the human check is refused and cannot run the action, and that a classified action with no written words is still refused.
- **The live deploy tool can now carry `no-deadline-cards.patch`'s hunk 9, the one hunk of that patch the owner's gate cannot replay (2026-10-30).** The patch was cut against a gate that still had the six-line "`raised` is stored WHOLE" comment and stored `json.dumps(raised)` uncapped; the live `jarvis_gate.py` holds neither of those - the `[:1000]` cap is back and the `target_digest` column is there - so hunk 9's twelve-line pre-image appears **0 times** in it (and once in the published base), and `apply-patches.ps1` fails the same hunk on that folder. The entry gains `adapted_hunks`: an explicit OLD -> NEW pair in `tools/hud-deltas/` (`gate-nodeadline-h9-old.txt` / `gate-nodeadline-h9-new.txt`) that adds ONLY the `waited_until` column and its value, keeping live's cap rather than quietly undoing a choice the live file already made. Each block must appear exactly once or the whole file is refused, and the new block's column line is the hunk's own probe, so a second deploy skips it instead of replacing twice. Two chain fixes ride with it, both measured on the staged gate: `no-deadline-cards.patch` rewrites TEN of `no-expiry-cards.patch`'s twelve regions and the two lines the `gate-digest-*` entries probed, so without a second proof the NEXT dry run would refuse patches the deploy had just applied - `no-expiry` now carries the later patch's complete six-line post-image as `or_probes`, and the two gate-digest probes moved onto lines no later patch touches. Measured on the 288-file refresh tree: **1 refusal -> 0** (exit 2 -> 0), `212 already present, 1 to insert (16 hunk(s))`, rehearsal OK; a copy of the gate the deploy writes then reports **213 already present, 0 to insert, 0 refusals** on the next run. `backend/test_reconcile_live_backend.py` proves the insert once, the second pass, a half-applied gate finished, a differently-edited or duplicated block REFUSED, and that the old entry refuses the same fixture (428 checks, all green). **Not deployed:** nothing was run with `--apply`; the live folder was only read.
- **The README says what shipped on 2026-10-30:** the GitHub front page now lists the history door with archive, the Jarvis Calendar (phone, HUD and widget), chat attachments that are read on the PC and never uploaded, the parking cost estimate, the phone town field, the Android app builder's two cards, custom widgets with "Put it on my home screen", custom notification styles, and how to add any feature from Edit HUD. It also carries the measured context (16,384 deployed; 32K and 64K measured worse), the signed 0.2.368 desktop installer and the 0.2.3261 phone APK, and an honest-limits note for each new ability.
- **The "it tried to rush you" warning now works on the main chat path, and tool text is cleaned of invisible characters before the model reads it (2026-10-30).** The security audit of 2026-10-30 (findings C1, C2 and C5) found the rush rule written, tested and never switched on for the HUD chat: a web page or an email saying "just approve this now" could not raise the warning or the swipe-lock, and a word split by a zero-width joiner reached the model raw. Every tool result now goes through the project's one outside-text pipeline before the model reads it: zero-width characters, bidi controls, Unicode Tag characters and look-alike letters are removed, and a rushing phrase arms the gate's existing latch - so the next action that would have run on its own stops and asks, and its card carries the unswipeable warning. It is the same latch the gate already reads: no second switch, no new control, and the standing rule that nothing may clear a rush latch is untouched. The schedule read (`coming_up`) is labelled as stored data too, so words typed into a reminder cannot reach the model as if the owner had just said them - and, like every other read in a turn, a note write after it asks first and a timer or reminder requested in that same turn is refused so the owner can type it themselves. An ordinary email still arms nothing; the tests that prove the invisible characters are gone, the latch arms and an ordinary message does not are in `backend/test_injection_cases.py`, and the module now has its own suite in `backend/test_content_risk.py`. Honest limit, unchanged by this fix: there is still no mechanical check on the model's own answer prose (audit finding P1) - the card remains the boundary for anything that acts. **Four more gates follow the same rule, and the document store now draws its own line (same day, second commit).** In a turn shaped by outside text - this turn read email, a file, a page or another tool's answer, or the conversation is tainted, or your newest message was pasted - a page fetch at an address you did not type says so on its card and cannot be skipped by a loosened setting, `run_data_script` asks even with the data sandbox switched on, and a calendar write waits for a person exactly as a note write does (that guard is in place before there is a chat calendar tool). A long result is scanned at BOTH ends now, because the model is sent a long text's first and last part while the instruction scanners only look at a start. `jarvis_documents` labels, folds and scans the words it returns itself, instead of leaving that to the agent one layer up - and the project's chain checker is 7 probes with 0 blocked, where 4 rows used to read BLOCKED; the three that were about a hop that does not exist in this tree now assert exactly that, and go red the day one appears. Proved by `backend/test_tainted_tool_gates.py` and the mutation suite behind the chain checker.
- **The required `backend` job is green again: a personality switch rings `manner` once and `voices` once.** #969 made a preset carry its own voice and *apply* it into the one PC-wide voice setting, so a personality switch now moves the sound as well as the preset and must tell the Voices screens to re-read. #971's `test_presets_consistency.py` was written before that and still demanded `["manner"]` alone, so from the morning of 2026-10-30 `origin/main` failed its own required `backend` suite and every open pull request failed a ~50-minute job on a test that could never pass. The check now asserts one `manner` ring, one `voices` ring, no kind twice, and reads the `manner` frame by name instead of assuming it is the last frame; a refused switch and humour still ring exactly what they always did.
- **Your town can be typed on the phone now, not only on the PC.** Appearance → "Sun, moon and weather" used to say "Your town is typed on the PC, in Settings, Animal options" and show nothing to type in. The PC now accepts a town from either app and says so in its own words, and the phone has a "Your town" field with a "Save town" action beside the town it already shows and "Forget my town". The PC looks the words up in the town list it already carries (about 16,000 towns, nothing fetched online) and keeps only the position rounded to about 11 km; typing a town sends those words only to your own PC over the pairing link, and never a name or position to any service. A town the PC cannot find is refused there, in its own words, and the field stays so you can correct it. Moving the town while Open-Meteo weather is on still switches Open-Meteo off, exactly as the PC's own field always did, because its ONE card named the old position. The desktop is unchanged; the Android compile is checked by CI, and the field itself still needs a run on the phone.
- **The phone's History is one tap from Home, and a chat can be archived and brought back (2026-10-29 request).** The owner asked for chat history "one click from the android jarvis app" and for archiving next to deleting. Home's always-on status line now carries a **History** control, so the kept chats open without going through Brain or the More menu; the Brain entry, the More menu's "Earlier chats" row and the pinned-message way in all stay. History's row menu gains **Archive**, which reads **Unarchive** on an archived chat, and a visible **Archived** chip beside the "Show" chips opens the archived view - the same list read with `archived=1`, so the live list never mixes archived chats into it. Archiving is reversible, so it asks nothing first and raises no approval card; only the chat's id and the wanted state are sent, and a row moves only after the PC says yes. Delete is untouched and keeps its confirm, word for word. This is the Android half: the backend route (`POST /api/history/archive`, `?archived=1` on `GET /api/history`, an `archived` field on every row) and the desktop's History control come from the other halves of the same batch. `tools/check_parity.py` records the route as `desktop-todo` until the desktop's History calls it.
- **The phone's History and More menus are simplified: one filter row and one Filters button, and a More sheet of five doors (2026-10-30).** The owner sent screenshots of the latest app and said *"these both screenshots need further simplification, too many options"*, then chose *"Tuck the rare ones behind one Filters button."* History used to draw three chip rows - the conversation kinds, the tags, and the same tags wrapping onto a second line with Untagged - under a "CONVERSATIONS / Tags / Forget a time frame…" line. It now draws ONE row: the owner's tags as quick chips ("All", "Work", "Learning", "Personal", …) that scroll sideways instead of wrapping, with a **Filters** button at the end whose dot (and TalkBack count) says when one of its filters is on. That button opens ONE flat sheet - the same quiet Dialog shape as Home's More menu - holding everything that was there before, still combining exactly as it did: the conversation-type filters (All chats, Just chats, Live only, Support chats, Chats with other AIs, Comparisons) and the Archived view; the full tag list with Untagged (so nothing depends on a sideways swipe); the **Tags** editor; and **Forget a time frame…**. Applying updates the list immediately, and the sheet follows the same "hide memory lists and chat history" guard the tag names do. The More sheet is short on purpose: **New chat, Photo, Attach a file, Modes, Quick note**, then Close (plus the conditional Notifications row, kept direct because it is the only door to attaching captured notifications and one sheet deeper would make it three taps). Think, Coach this and Incognito moved one tap deeper, behind the one **Modes** row in a small sub-sheet, each behaving exactly as before with the same offer gates. Earlier chats left More because it opened History and History IS the list (Home's status line opens it in one tap); Pinned messages moved into History's own header as one compact **Pinned** action that opens the same PinnedScreen; Jarvis Presets left More for Home's existing one-tap "Presets →" door beside the Galaxy. NOTHING was dropped - only relocated, and every capability stays reachable within two taps. The desktop app is deliberately unchanged: this is a phone-only simplification, on a phone that does not show the desktop's HUD/menus, so no parity row or one-sided note is needed. Proven by the full local JVM suite (2,955 tests, 0 failures, including the updated source-pin tests: ChatMenuTest, AuditPlumbingTest, HistoryArchiveTest and PresetsLandingTest now assert the NEW contract rather than the old rows) and `tools/check_parity.py`; **not proven on a phone** - no APK was built from this branch, so the field-test step is the CI-built APK on the owner's phone: open History and More, open Filters, pick a tag and a kind, open Modes and turn Incognito on.
- **The phone's Work pane can start a background job and answer the one thing it is waiting for (2026-10-30).** Same loop as the desktop's, in the phone's own words: a "Start a background job" form offering only the job kinds this PC registered, and under a parked job the single permission it wants with exactly **No** and **Allow once**. It starts with no permissions frozen, so a job parks at its first need and asks there; one answer covers one use and a No stops that job without remembering it. An older PC keeps its read-only job list and says which patch it needs.
- **The phone asks for its fingerprint on exactly the cards the PC asks Windows Hello for - and says so, in words, before you press (2026-10-30).** The owner's ruling: *"if there is anything that needs approval for Jarvis on desktop with the Windows Hello PIN as well, make the Android app require a fingerprint to approve the same card - but I can approve the same level of card on Android as well."* Reading the code first was the whole answer to the first half: the phone already required its check on those cards - `SecurityRules.riskyByToday` is the same rule as `jarvis_owner_check.is_risky`, held to the PC's own answers by the shared `contract/risky-approval-cases.json`, and `MainActivity.approveItem` runs `BiometricGate` (a strong fingerprint, or the phone's PIN, the same check App lock uses) on both of its paths before anything is sent. What was missing was the words: the card said **"Fingerprint follows"** - which named neither what the check is with nor when it happens - and said it even on a PC-only card, whose Approve is not drawn at all. It now says **"Approve asks for your fingerprint or PIN."**, or "Approve asks for your fingerprint." under "Fingerprint only", on its own line under the buttons, from `SecurityRules.approveCheckWords` - which reads the same two things the check reads (`approvalNeedsCheck` and `Security.method`), so it cannot promise a check that will not run, and `ApproveAsksFingerprintTest` (in the required `phone-unit-tests` job) holds the sentence and the gate together over every shared risky case. No backend change: the signed-approval contract (JARVIS-API §91) already accepts a phone's signature and already refuses a risky card from a device with its own key that carries none, and `backend/test_approval_sign.py` already proves both. The buttons keep the shared "Deny"/"Approve" words on both apps, and Deny, a cancelled check and a failed one still send nothing. One difference is written down rather than half-built: an action with no written words is refused on the PC before any prompt (`owner_check: "unlabelled"`) and the phone cannot know that, because the row carries no field for it - JARVIS-API §3, ARCHITECTURE §8. Not verified on a phone - a real `BiometricPrompt` needs a device, which this desk does not have, so that row stays PARTIAL. What IS proven here, not just in CI: `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest --offline` on this PC compiled the change and ran **2,960 JVM tests with 0 failures**, including the six new ones.
- **Parking, priced minute by minute from public data (2026-10-30).** "find cheap parking near Pike Place Market tomorrow from 4:15 PM to 9:00 PM" is now answered on this PC with no model and no tool slot: a new shipped module, `backend/jarvis_parking.py`, walks the stay one minute at a time across the sign's own time windows, so a 4h 45m stay on a 2-hour block that turns free at 6 PM reads as **legal, $3.50** instead of being hidden, and a three-tier block sums to **$30.50** instead of one rate for the whole stay. Prices come from Seattle's public Blockface layer and OpenStreetMap's Overpass - no key, no account, standard library only, so `requirements.lock` does not move. What leaves the PC is a latitude, a longitude and a radius; the only request that carries words is the map lookup of the place the owner himself named. A place whose price is not published is counted and named, never quoted as free, and every answer ends by saying it is a cost estimate and not a booking. The PC's screen gets the full breakdown; a phone gets two options with a `geo:` navigation link and not one desktop-only phrase. `backend/test_parking.py` proves all of it offline (140 checks, every socket made to fail), and "parking for my next meeting" reads the next calendar event that has a place - quoting the venue back locally and **not** sending the calendar's own words to a map service.
- **The phone's notifications can be styled, and Jarvis builds the style from your words (2026-10-30).** Say "make my Jarvis notifications look like a warm orange pulse" and Jarvis makes a preview from a closed menu - a colour name, a figure, an animation (still, pulse, flip, spinner) and a speed - which you tap Add to apply to your approval cards and reminders. The animation is one Android itself performs inside the notification (`ViewFlipper` frames and an indeterminate spinner), so it keeps running when the app's process is dead and costs no wakeups. It changes only how the words are drawn: Deny is still the only button on an approval card, nothing new is shown, and while the phone is locked or App lock is on the plain notification is used instead. The desktop deliberately has none: a Windows toast is drawn from Windows' own template, so there is no layout to style (see `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §8 and JARVIS-API §167). Not verified on a device: no emulator or phone at this desk, so what is proven is that the app compiles, the 160 backend checks and the JVM tests pass, and every View used is on `RemoteViews`' allow-list.
- **A fourth live repair carries the unclassified-action gate change to a PC whose patch stack cannot be applied:** `tools/repair_live_backend_gaps.py` now also puts in `unknown-risk-approvable.patch`'s five pieces (the eight `_RISK` rows, the worst-case warning, the risk and notice wording, and the forced "ask" in `check()`) as one anchored write — one backup of the file as it was before the run, one compile, rollback on failure — because the install's own earlier repair put the app-builder rows where that patch's first hunk expected its pre-image. `tools/reconcile_live_backend.py` gains the matching `DELTA_TABLE` rule so a fresh folder is carried by reconcile itself; its probes are one added line per hunk, because the report's proposed full warning sentence is not in the source at all (it is split across two literals). Both tools' suites prove the repaired gate equals the published one byte for byte.
- **A repair tool for a PC whose patch stack cannot be applied:** two base-file pieces the deploy could not carry - the app builder's two risk rows in the approval gate (without them its cards could not be approved) and the chat pin/archive routes in the HUD (without them they answered 404) - are now inserted by `tools/repair_live_backend_gaps.py`, anchored, idempotent, backed up and compiled before the swap sticks. Found by the 2026-10-30 field test; it reports "already present" on an install where the patcher works.
- **A live install whose HUD cannot take the new patch now receives the background-jobs routes:** `tools/reconcile_live_backend.py` gains one `DELTA_TABLE` rule for `jobs-routes.patch` (four hunks, with one adapted pair for hunk 3 whose pre-image the earlier live repairs had already reshaped), so a folder carried forward by the installer or a drifted install gets `GET /api/jobs` with grants and ticking, and `POST /api/jobs` plus `POST /api/jobs/answer`, instead of reporting the patch "already present" because one imported line happens to exist elsewhere in the HUD of an install that never received the routes. Two block files under `tools/hud-deltas/` hold the exact old and new text, and a hermetic test in `backend/test_reconcile_live_backend.py` proves the carried result equals the patch's own application byte for byte and that the adapted pair is refused-free on both the published base and the live-shaped HUD.
- **A reseal tool that puts a drifted live install back on the bytes this repository ships, and repairs the installer's record it can prove (2026-10-30, Gemini's Q3 `(b')`).** The owner's install still reports **93 FAIL** rows from `selftest.py --preflight`: `scripts/apply-patches.ps1` cannot replay its stack there, so the five patched base files sit on an older intermediate state, and `_jarvis-state.json` - the installer's own record beside the backend - still holds the hashes of that older state. `tools/reconcile_live_backend.py` can move the `$SHIPPED` files but must never write the record, so this is the smaller, separate tool Q3 asked for. Dry-run by default: it reads `$SHIPPED` and every module's bytes from `--ref` (`git show`), never the working tree, compares each entry with the live folder **by bytes**, and prints what it would write, which record rows it would re-hash, and the preflight rows it counts now and after - through `backend/selftest.py`'s own `patch_states` for the patch rows, and through the same four rules `pf_modules` uses for the module rows. `--apply` copies the repository's exact bytes, keeps every replaced file as `<name>.before-reseal-<stamp>` beside it, compiles the file that is **now in the folder** (bytecode to a temporary file, so none is left behind), purges stale `__pycache__/*.pyc`, and re-runs the same comparison as its convergence proof; a write or compile failure restores every file it wrote and removes every file it created. It then rewrites **only** the record rows it can prove: a row whose file this repository ships gets the repository's hash, while `written`, `patches`, `patchList` and every untouched row stay exactly as the installer left them, and a `resealed` key records the tool, the commit and the row names. A row for a file this repository does **not** ship (the five patched base files) is never re-hashed - it is printed with both hashes and the run exits 1 - and `--rebaseline-unprovable` records the folder's own bytes for exactly those rows, the patcher's own first-run baseline rule, off by default. **It cannot reach 0 FAIL on its own and says so:** the preflight's *patch* check is about `.patch` text in the five base files, and only the patcher (or #997's targeted repair, `tools/repair_live_backend_gaps.py`) moves those rows. `backend/test_reseal_live_install.py` proves 92 checks on synthetic live folders - dry run writes nothing, apply keeps a backup and copies the bytes exactly, a stale row is corrected only for a file the tool owns, an ambiguous or absent file is refused, an unshipped file is never touched, a second apply changes nothing, a corrupted live file is replaced and compiles, and the real `pf_modules` goes from three FAIL rows to one (the file the repository does not ship) with the tool's own mirror agreeing on both numbers. Also green: `test_reconcile_live_backend.py` (428), `test_base_matches_repo.py` (135), `test_base_regeneration.py` (39), `check_stale_twins.py`, both pin ratchets, `roll_changelog --check`, `test_pictures.py` (258) and `test_api_doc.py` (77). `docs/DEPLOY.md` says how to run it. **Not run against the owner's live folder:** the dry run is the first thing to read, and it writes nothing.
- **The card timer can reach a PC whose patch stack cannot run:** the live-install repair tool now carries a third piece - the `expires_in` countdown `show-card-timer.patch` adds to `pending()` - so a drifted install shows every card's remaining time instead of letting it run out silently (the field test hit exactly that with the app-builder card). Same rules as the other two pieces: anchored, idempotent, backed up, compile-or-rollback, and it says "already present" on an install where the patcher works.
- **Long Fuse: start a background job and answer the one thing it is waiting for (2026-10-30).** The job queue had a list and a cancel and no way in or out: a job could only be created by code, and a job that needed a capability waited for ever. The Brain's Work tab now starts ONE job from your own words - it begins with no permissions and parks the moment it needs one - and answers a parked job's single grant with **Allow once** or **No**: one job, one use, no standing permission, and a No stops that job without remembering it. The phone's Work pane gets the same loop in its next slice.
- **The calendar HUD test no longer fails at one particular hour of the day:** its `soon` event fixture was `now + 90 minutes` with its 45-minute end on today's date too, so from 21:45Z the drawn card (rightly) read the event as already ended and dropped it, and from 22:30Z the start itself was on tomorrow. The fixture now clamps the start to today with a minute to spare, gives the event's end the END's own date, and proves the clamp over every hour of a fake day, so no PR inherits a red `frontend` at one particular time of the clock.
- **The HUD's History is one visible click in every layout, and both desktop windows can archive a chat.** The 2026-10-27 History panel lived behind an icon in the left rail, and in three of the six HUD layouts that rail is an off-canvas drawer, so the owner had to know the icon was there and then open the drawer; the owner asked on 2026-10-29 for chat history "accessible directly from the jarvis hud". A **History** button now sits on the page's main strip in every shell (same clock glyph, opens the same one panel through the same code the rail icon uses; the rail icon stays), and both desktop windows gained **Archive** and **Unarchive** beside Delete and a visible **Archived** view: the HUD sidebar gets "Your chats" / "Archived" chips over the same list, and the Brain's History tab gets a `history-archived` switch with "Back to your chats". Archiving is reversible and asks nothing; **Delete still asks its triple-sentence confirm, in both windows**. An archived chat cannot be opened or continued from the archived view (the row says so and points back); archiving the chat a window is currently in starts a fresh conversation there instead of filing into a hidden one. It reuses the PC's new `/api/history/archive` and the existing archived-list flag, so nothing leaves the PC and no approval card is raised; the phone's half of the same request shipped in its own change. Not verified here: the installed WebView2 app (the page was driven in Playwright across all six shells) and the live Python route end to end.
- **Chats can be archived, and put back (2026-10-30).** The owner asked on 2026-10-29 that chats can be archived and deleted with a confirm step. Delete already existed and is unchanged; this adds the missing half. A chat can now be put away without being deleted: `POST /api/history/archive {"id": "<conversation id>", "archived": true}` archives one chat, and `archived: false` brings it back exactly where it was - reversible, metadata only, and no approval card or confirm step, because it stores no words, opens no chat and sends nothing (delete keeps its own confirm). `GET /api/history?archived=1` lists the archived chats, the ordinary History list never shows them, and every conversation row carries `archived: true|false`, so the desktop HUD/Brain and the phone draw the same two lists. The flag lives in the PC's own encrypted history file as a plain `archived_at` column, so a chat archived on the phone is archived on the PC and survives a restart. Tested by `backend/test_chat_log.py`.
- **The FAQ and tutorials cover the 2026-10-30 batch:** eleven new questions (history door and the hide-lists gate, archive/unarchive, the Jarvis Calendar, widgets and one-tap home-screen placement, notification styles, Edit HUD, attachments and PDF privacy, parking, widget privacy, the measured 16,384 context, and the Android app builder) plus two short walkthroughs - putting a chat away and getting it back, and attaching a file and asking about it. The text lives in `docs/TUTORIALS-DESIGN.md` for the catalogue module to carry, and states honestly where an ability is not reachable yet.
- **The Jarvis face takes the Galaxy's right-hand half when the galaxy is hidden (2026-10-30).** In the Studio layout, hiding the Galaxy used to collapse the stage to one column and centre a square face above the conversation. Now the conversation keeps the left half and the face sits in the right half - the same 45% the Galaxy picture used - 240px base, so 300px on Medium and 360px on Large. Everything else is unchanged: what "hidden" means (the picture, its dots, its legend and its trace are still gone), the Show the galaxy chip in the bar, the other Inspector tabs (Approvals, Reminders, Memory, Telemetry) and every other layout.
- **The calendar and app-builder section numbers were swapped in the docs, and the app builder has no entry point yet (2026-10-30).** `docs/JARVIS-API.md` pointed the desktop Jarvis Calendar at §166 and the app builder at §168 in its own notes, but the headings say the calendar is §168 and the app builder is §166; the calendar references in §164, §166's template answer (its status object is §166.5), `plugins/ready/android-builder/README.md` and the two 2026-10-30 changelog fragments are corrected. §166's title no longer says "from chat": its four routes exist and answer, but no chat sentence, model tool or button in either app calls them yet - only the module's own tests do, so an entry point still has to be written. `docs/README.md`'s row for `docs/TUTORIALS-DESIGN.md` now gives that document's real status instead of "Everything below is implemented".
- **The stale `JARVIS-API.md` section pointers in code comments are fixed, and `CLAUDE.md` no longer denies the local Android build (2026-10-30).** Follow-up to #1002, which corrected the swapped section numbers in the docs proper: the same swap was still in the comments that cite `docs/JARVIS-API.md` from code and in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §8. The calendar is **§168** and the app builder **§166**, so sixteen places that pointed the calendar at §166 now point at §168 - `backend/jarvis_open.py`, `jarvis_quick.py` and `jarvis_settings_registry.py` with their `jarvis-backend/` twins, the desktop's `build.rs`, `permissions/surfaces.toml`, `calendar_settings.rs`, `lib.rs` (twice), `calendar-settings.js` and `settings.html`, and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §8's home-screen-widget row; the three comments that quote "Open my calendar still opens it" now cite §21.13, the section that sentence is actually in. The app builder's two `jarvis-framework.toml` comments (`backend/rebuilt/` and its `jarvis-backend/` twin) now say §166, not §168. Separately, `CLAUDE.md`'s "How the Android apps get built" no longer says "There is no local Android build in this container" and "GitHub Actions is the only compiler": the owner's PC has the JDK, the Android SDK and a warm Gradle cache, so `.\gradlew.bat testDebugUnitTest --offline` compiles the app and runs its JVM/contract unit tests locally in about two minutes (verified on this PC on 2026-10-14, `docs/FIELD-TEST-PLAN-2026-10-14.md`, and again on 2026-10-27, `docs/DECISIONS.md`), while CI remains what builds, signs and publishes the APK and runs the emulator smoke job; `jarvis-client/README.md` and `.claude/agents/phone-playtester.md` carried the same stale claim and now say the corrected version too. No code changed - comments and docs only.
- **The desktop's floating face (the picture-in-picture face) can be made bigger or smaller from the tray, opens on the screen you are looking at, and can no longer be left half off-screen (2026-10-30).** The owner asked to improve the PC version after the phone work, and to look at picture-in-picture behaviour on the desktop. Three changes, all in `jarvis-desktop/src-tauri/src/windows.rs` plus two tray rows: (1) **a remembered face size** — 160, 200, 240 or 300 logical pixels, stepped with the tray's new "Floating face: bigger" / "Floating face: smaller" rows under More…, applied live when the face is open, persisted in `floating.json`, and defaulting to the same 200 so nothing changes for anyone who never touches it (an older or hand-edited prefs file falls back to 200 rather than a 0-pixel window); (2) **opens on the monitor under the cursor** instead of always centred on the primary display, which is the wrong screen as soon as a second monitor is in use; (3) **a full on-screen clamp** — a saved position is pulled entirely inside the monitor it overlaps most (or the nearest monitor when that one is gone), so unplugging a monitor or moving a window between mixed-DPI screens cannot leave the face unreachable. The existing promises are untouched: always-on-top by default, never takes the keyboard, no text box and no button, clicks pass through where the animal is not drawn (the hit grid and its 32×32 measurement are size-independent because the page fills the window), no new command, route, permission or approval card, and the same two local-only Tauri commands (`get_floating` now also reports `size`; `set_floating` accepts optional `clickThrough`/`size`). Proven at the desk against the Windows target: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo check`/`cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --all-targets -D warnings` and `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` all green (1,126 tests, 0 failed; four new pure tests for the size cycle, the four-size sanitiser, per-monitor clamping and seam/straddle monitor choice), and the tray source-pin suite `tests/tray-rows.mjs` passes (27 ids, 27 dispatched, every id handled). **Not proven here:** no desktop build was installed or opened in this session — the session cannot host an interactive GUI app (five launch attempts of the installed app died `0xC0000409`; `notepad.exe` died with the same code), so the on-screen resize/placement has not been seen; the CI/`desktop-latest` build and one owner look are the remaining check. The browser-driven suites (`ia.mjs`, the Playwright half of `floating-click-through.mjs`) cannot run in this session (named pipes/Playwright absent) — the source-only halves passed.
- **The deployed context is 16,384 tokens and measured, not the 4,096 the documents claimed (2026-10-30).** The 2026-10-30 competitiveness audit calls the 4,096-token context "the single biggest capability gap" and its work order asks for "8K-16K via a Modelfile", and the measurement found the premise out of date before it found anything else: `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=16384` is already set for the owner's Windows user, both of this PC's Ollama servers report `context_length: 16384`, the everyday model's runner carries `-c 16384`, and the live chat's model is `qwen3.5:9b` (`model-state.json`; the last recorded turn, 2026-10-29 18:21, ran it at 100% GPU and 39.01 tokens/s). Nothing in the 8K-16K range was left to raise, so no live setting was touched. Measured the same night on the RTX 2060 12 GB: at 4,096 / 8,192 / 16,384 the model loaded every time at 100% GPU, reported 5.54 / 5.62 / 5.80 GB, left 4,055 / 3,983 / 3,815 MiB free, and decoded at 38-43 tokens/s while reading the prompt at about 1,200 tokens/s; 16,384 costs only 240 MiB more than 4,096 because `qwen3.5:9b` caches only its 8 full-attention layers. What 4,096 did is measured too: a ~5,019-token prompt reached the runner as 2,050 tokens and lost its start, while 16,384 read all 15,409 tokens of a 15,000-token prompt; and the 1,024-token answer allowance still comes out of the same window (981 of a requested 1,024 at 16,384, 984 at 4,096, both stopping at the window edge). `docs/MODEL-TOPOLOGY.md` now states the measured 16,384 and names the environment variable in Setup, `backend/jarvis-primary.Modelfile`'s comments stop claiming 4,096, and `docs/MEASURED-2026-10-30-CONTEXT.md` carries the numbers, the procedure and the undo. **Not measured, and not claimed:** 32,768 and 65,536, sustained multi-turn use, and the second card's lanes.
- **A card with a limited time now shows its timer, on every screen (2026-10-30).** The owner's ruling, verbatim: "if there is a card with a limited time of any kind, be sure to put the timer on it." `backend/show-card-timer.patch` (applied after `no-deadline-cards.patch`) sends `expires_in` again on `/api/pending` for a **routine** card whose request is still waiting: whole seconds left until the row's own `waited_until`, never 0 or negative, rounded up, and absent for a ceremony card (pairing, an approval key, a restore) and for a request that has already ended. The 60-second grace is deliberately not counted down, because no request can act on the card in it. **It is display only:** the 2026-10-28 no-deadline ruling is not reversed - no card carries a deadline of its own, nothing is refused because a clock reached zero, nothing auto-approves, a Deny always works, and the read-time sweep, `still_waiting()` and the 409 are untouched. The desktop words it once (`countdownWords` in `card-words.js`): "14m 59s left", then "42s left — under a minute to decide." with a warning colour, shared by the bar, the widget and the HUD page's own copy (held together by `tests/expiry-parity.mjs`). The phone's deadline readout - "EXPIRES IN 00:43", "EXPIRING SOON" in the last minute, and the thin bar, all dormant since 2026-10-28 - lights up again, with the last-minute rule now one pure function (`countdownReadout`) a JVM test can hold. **Honest limits:** it needs a live deploy (`apply-patches.ps1` on the owner's PC plus a backend restart, or `tools/reconcile_live_backend.py` once it has a `show-card-timer.patch` delta rule); the phone's new Kotlin is compiled by CI only (`phone-unit-tests`), not at this desk; and the two apps word the clock differently on purpose - plain words on the PC, the instrument readout the phone has had since DEF-010. Tests: `backend/test_gate_card_timer.py` (69 checks, plus the bite proof that the two-patch stack sends no timer at all), `jarvis-desktop/tests/expiry-parity.mjs` and `approvals-contract.mjs`, and the phone's `CardTimerTest` + `PendingRowsContractTest`. Docs: `docs/JARVIS-API.md` §pending, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2, `backend/README.md`.
- **The Jarvis Calendar can now be switched from the desktop's own Settings, and the calendar's read fails closed (2026-10-30).** Two halves were still missing after the HUD card landed. The first the owner could see: the phone has had the calendar's ON/OFF row since its own slice, but the desktop's calendar lives in the HUD - a different window with no Settings page - so on his PC there was no control for it anywhere except asking out loud, and the feature's own row said so. Settings now has a "Jarvis Calendar" card whose one switch reaches the same value the phone's row and the typed sentence reach (`POST /api/calendar/settings`, one field), through a new `calendar_settings` command that drops every event, title, place and count in Rust - the Settings page draws one switch and cannot read his day. No card either way, and turning it OFF never deletes an event; the sentence under the switch says both. The second was a bug found while wiring the tests: the HUD's calendar read treated ANY object as a calendar unless it said `available: false`, so the single-event answer `{"ok": true, "event": {...}}`, a bare event and an empty body all became "an available calendar with no events" and were drawn as *"Nothing in the next 7 days"* - a reassuring empty day invented from an answer nobody understood. The read now requires a real boolean `available` and, when true, an `events` array, and refuses anything else in words; the two write handlers had the same shape of bug (`reply.ok === false` accepted an answer with no `ok` at all as "Event added.") and now require `ok: true` positively. All of it is pinned by two new checks in `tests/hud-calendar.mjs` (33 in all), and documented as `docs/JARVIS-API.md` section 168.
- **Attached PDFs and Office files are readable again: the first 16 KiB of every one was being lost in transit (2026-10-30).** MarkItDown, the part of Jarvis that turns a PDF, Word, Excel or PowerPoint file into text, guesses a file's type by reading its first 16 KiB and then rewinding. A file attached to a chat reaches it on a Windows pipe, and a pipe reports that it can rewind and then silently does not - so the parser was handed the file from byte 16,384 on. Every PDF tested (22 of 22) lost text, three came back with no words at all and were refused with "it has no words in it to read" about a file that had plenty, and every Word, Excel and PowerPoint file failed outright as "Jarvis could not read it (FileConversionException)"; a 25 KB Word file reproduced that exact sentence. The child now copies the pipe into memory before the converter sees it - never written to disk - so both reading paths convert the same complete bytes. The suite's one check on the real reading path asserted only that the answer HAD an `"ok"` field, on a 32-byte fixture, so it passed on a guaranteed failure; it now asserts the answer IS ok, on a real PDF and a real `.docx` bigger than 16 KiB, read through both paths.
- **The app builder's two cards can be raised again:** asking Jarvis to build an Android app (or put it on the phone) now raises its approval card instead of answering *"TypeError"*. The drop-in loader only hands a module the server's three checks, so the module now builds the card plumbing (the same `jarvis_gate.check` every other card uses) and rings the `approval` doorbell both apps re-read. Found on the owner's own PC during the 2026-10-30 field test, where `POST .../android/build` answered 503.
- **Approving an app build now actually builds it:** the app builder's card plumbing returned a plain yes/no where the rest of Jarvis hands back the approval gate's full verdict, so an approved card still ended with "the gate answered at tier 'unknown'" and nothing ran (found on the owner's own PC on 2026-10-30: the build card was approved on the desktop and the build refused). The gate's verdict is now passed through unchanged, and a regression test drives the decision path with both shapes to prove which one runs the build.
- **The phone keeps its shared debug signing key, and rotating it is now one command (2026-10-30).** The owner accepted the sideload boundary Gemini's Q4 ruling named: the app is signed with one shared debug key, sideloaded onto his own phone only, and updated only from this repository's release over https with the APK's certificate pinned in the app - so the key stays for now, and the record says exactly when that has to change (public distribution, the APK handed to anyone else, or the repository made public with release assets). Replacing the key costs one `adb uninstall` and a re-pair, because that is what Android requires when the certificate changes. `scripts/rotate-android-signing-key.ps1 -Rotate` does the key half of that ceremony: it makes a new keystore outside the repository, refuses without the flag and refuses to overwrite, reads the alias and password from the build file rather than carrying them, and prints the new fingerprint with the remaining steps. It changes nothing else. **Honest limit:** only the owner's phone can prove the new APK installs and pairs again - this was run against a throwaway key in a temporary folder, never against the real one.
- **A small Jarvis window can stay on screen while you use other apps (2026-10-30).** Settings -> This app -> Picture-in-picture is Android's own picture-in-picture: the window shows the face and the first line of the newest answer (the default), the last few messages, or Jarvis Live, and it switches to the Live view by itself while a Live session runs. It never approves anything, never shows hidden or locked content (it says "Hidden while Jarvis is locked." instead) and cannot change a setting; its only action is Expand, which brings Jarvis back. Floating Jarvis and this can both be on - the floating face hides while the window is up and returns when it ends. Phone only, saved on that phone, and not yet driven on real hardware.
- **Jarvis builds an Android app and sideloads it onto the phone, with one approval card for the build and one for the install (2026-10-30).** The owner asked *"can jarvis build android apps? that i can sideload into my phone? if not add them"*; the honest answer before this was no, because milestones C (run a command), F (native Android projects) and G (install the debug APK) of `docs/APP-BUILDER-DESIGN.md` were all unbuilt. `backend/jarvis_android_builder.py` now writes a FIXED minimal Android project into one app task's copy - the Gradle files, a manifest with **no permission at all**, one WebView activity, and the owner's own `index.html` / `app.js` / `app.css` - then runs exactly `<task copy>\gradlew.bat assembleDebug` and exactly `<sdk>\platform-tools\adb.exe install -r <the apk that build made>`, each behind its own risky `ask` card naming the exact command, folder and file (`app_build_android`, `app_install_android`). A model may only supply the three web files plus a validated app name and two `#rrggbb` colours: it writes no Gradle file, no Kotlin and no manifest, so it cannot add a permission, a dependency or a network address. The environment is `jarvis_child_env`'s allowlist plus three toolchain paths (a test proves a token, key or password in the parent's environment does not reach Gradle or adb), the build's output goes to a gitignored log with only its end kept, and no other adb subcommand exists in the module - a source scan fails the suite if one is added. Measured on the owner's PC on 2026-10-30: **one real build of exactly this template, 53.6 s, a 2,469,448-byte debug APK, `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`**, `aapt2 dump badging` showing no `uses-permission` line and `apksigner` showing the Android debug key. That was a WARM build - Gradle 9.6.0 and the Android Gradle plugin were already cached on this PC - so a truly cold first build (about 130 MB of Gradle plus the plugin) is **not** measured, and the card says a first build downloads them. Honest limits, on the card and in the docs: debug key only, sideload only and never the Play Store, no per-app icon yet, no test ever runs Gradle or adb (a CI runner has no Android SDK and no phone), and the routes are PC only because the SDK and the cable are on this PC - the two CARDS still reach both apps, so the owner can approve from the phone. Routes are JARVIS-API section 166; wiring is the drop-in folder `plugins/ready/android-builder` (so **no patch**), the gate rows in `jarvis-backend/jarvis_gate.py`, two tier lines in `backend/rebuilt/jarvis-framework.toml`, and Gradle's own two wrapper files carried as beside-files (Apache-2.0, credited). Tests: `backend/test_android_builder.py`.
- **Settings words that named the other app are fixed, and two records now say what both apps really draw (2026-10-29).** The 2026-10-29 settings wrong-app audit found the two Settings screens clean but the words and the records wrong. 17 rows the PC alone has told the phone "Settings, Accounts" and "Settings, Shortcuts" with nothing saying these are the PC's, so the phone's own "All features and abilities" dead-ended; the phone's directory now puts "On your PC: " in front of those words, and a Kotlin test pins it. The desktop's directory named "All features and abilities" - the directory card itself - for Talk-to-type and the calendar instead of the Settings card the owner must open; it now names Shortcuts and Accounts. Floating Jarvis is recorded as both apps' (the PC's is the Floating face toggle inside Appearance), and Notifications is too: the phone really does change the PC's seven kinds in its "Notifications from Jarvis" row, so "open notifications" now lands there instead of answering "only in Jarvis on your PC". The connect answer says the card is in the HUD, not "the Jarvis window", and the main Settings search stops announcing a stale "3 settings match." beside "Nothing here matches". Already fixed on main: the tray-footer sentence #974 qualified, and the Google/Gmail answer copy is the quick-path branch's.
- **The phone's field-test bugs are fixed: true counts, readable cards, separate taps and honest words (2026-10-29).** The 2026-10-29 real-device test found eight phone bugs; this fixes six here and points the other two at the work already doing them. The Inbox header now counts the approval cards it lists ("1 card waiting", never "0 waiting" above a waiting card). An approval card whose PC sent no summary never draws raw JSON - it starts folded with a readable line and an "Explain more" control that opens plain words. Home's status words are their own button, so a tap near "Connected" can no longer turn automatic model restarting off. A device row says "last active", never the unsupported "last used" (the PC writes that number on any request, including the app's own 90-second poll). "What can you do?" no longer offers a phone a tray-icon right-click without saying it is on the PC. "Change your voice to Ashby" when Ashby is not made says so in the Voices screen's own words instead of listing 22 voices. The calendar answer names the PC's Settings, Accounts rather than sending a phone to a screen it does not have. Settings search relevance (B5) is PR #970's, and the made-Ashby case (part of B3) is #962's.
- **Jarvis has a calendar of its own (2026-10-29).** The owner asked for "a full fledged easy to use calendar built into Jarvis" that is "disabled but on by default", local-first and shown beside Google Calendar and iCal. S1 is the store and the API, with no screen yet: `backend/jarvis_local_calendar.py` (and its `jarvis-backend/` twin, a `SHIPPED` entry and a `plugins/ready/jarvis-calendar/` folder) keeps events in `calendar.db` beside `schedule.db`, opened with the shared closing connection, and answers `GET /api/calendar?days=7` (or `?id=`), `POST /api/calendar/add`, `POST /api/calendar/act` and `POST /api/calendar/settings` from its own `install()` - so there is no `jarvis_hud.py` patch, no gate entry and no network door. **The date and time are stored as they read on the clock, never as an epoch**, so a 09:00 event stays 09:00 across a daylight-saving change; the epoch is computed on read and follows the PC's own jump (a time that never happened goes at the jump, a time that happened twice takes the first). Recurrence v1 is `none`/`daily`/`weekly`/`monthly` with an optional count or until date, and a monthly 31st is clamped to the short months (28 February in a common year). The feature is **ON with no settings file**, OFF hides the list and refuses writes while keeping every event, and a damaged settings file is OFF with a reason. One event per write, no delete-all and no bulk form; the owner's 2026-10-29 ruling records the cardless decision and the ids-only audit in `decisions.d/` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. `calendar.db` is on the backup list (a missing name there silently drops every event). **"Turn off the Jarvis calendar" works by typing from the day the store lands** - through `CHANGE_BY_ASKING` and the registry's `BoolSetting("jarvis_calendar", ...)`, which calls the same `handle_settings` the route calls, so the switch cannot disagree with itself. "calendar" alone is deliberately not one of its names: a bare calendar alias would also catch "turn off Google calendar" and flip the wrong switch, so "turn off Google calendar" still resolves to nothing. `py -3 jarvis_local_calendar.py check` proves the store, routes, settings, recurrence, DST and backup line in a throwaway folder (17 checks); `backend/test_local_calendar.py` adds 88 checks (all four routes through a stand-in Handler with a cross-origin refusal and a missing-token refusal on each, the owner's own phrase driven through the real chat grammar, damaged store, one-day window, no bulk). **Honest limits:** no HUD card, phone plate or widget yet - those are the next slices and the contract they build against is in `docs/JARVIS-API.md` §164; reminders are stored and never rung; Google and iCal stay exactly as they were and say `not_on_a_screen` until their read is wired to a screen.
- **The desktop HUD has the owner's own calendar: month, week and day views, one Add/Edit form, and a card he can place from Edit HUD (2026-10-29).** The owner asked for "a full fledged easy to use calendar built into Jarvis", local only and disableable. This slice is the desktop half of it: a Calendar card in the HUD, a sheet with Month (a six-week Sunday-first grid), Week (seven columns and one band for all-day and spanning events) and Day views, and ONE form that adds or edits an event - title, date, all day, start and end, last day, a reminder saved with the event (nothing rings yet), place, a daily/weekly/monthly repeat, and notes. It reads the one local store through `GET /api/calendar?days=90`, and writes through two commands of its own, `hud_calendar_add` and `hud_calendar_act`, so the page still holds no pairing token; neither write raises a card (one row on this PC, no list form, no delete-all), and both are held on a stale link. Titles, places and notes are blanked while App lock or "Hide memory lists" is on, and the days, times and counts stay. EVERY STATE IS SAID IN WORDS: the switch turned off ("The Jarvis Calendar is off. Nothing was deleted..."), an empty week, a day with nothing on it, and a read that failed - never a blank card, and no countdown anywhere. The card is a real card the owner can place: it is in the HUD's layout registry and in Edit HUD, every built-in preset carries it, `contract/hud-presets.json` mirrors them, and a card added after the owner last saved a layout is put back into that layout rather than hidden from Edit HUD for ever (his rule that every easily-accessible feature must be addable to the main screen from Edit HUD). Also fixed, found by the new test: an event's moment was computed from a NOON anchor, so every event read as "upcoming" and the card listed events that had already ended. The store, the four routes and the feature's own switch are the backend slice's; the phone's plate and widget are its own slice. `tests/hud-calendar.mjs` (31 checks) runs the page's own calendar block against a tiny fake document.
- **The phone gets Jarvis's own calendar: a month grid, a day list and a home-screen widget (2026-10-29).** The owner asked for a full calendar of Jarvis's own - local only, on by default, disableable - and this is the Android half of it. A new `CALENDAR` screen (last in `Screen`, so a saved back stack still restores by name) opens from a row beside "Coming up" on Brain and from the widget's tap: a 7-column month grid with today ringed, a dot on every day with an event, Previous / Today / Next, and the selected day's list underneath (all-day first, then the times from earliest). One event is added, changed or deleted at a time from a sheet - there is no delete-all and no bulk form, the same rule `/api/schedule/act` keeps - and every write is held while the link is down or catching up. Titles read "(hidden)" under App lock or "Hide memory lists and chat history", while the times stay. The sixth home-screen widget (`CalendarWidget.kt`, 4x2) shows today's date and the next three events, "Nothing today" when that is the truth, one sentence and no title while the phone is locked, "Not up to date" on a stale link, and - when the calendar is switched off - it KEEPS its place and says "Jarvis Calendar is off · tap to turn it on" with a tap into Settings, because a vanishing widget or an empty square both read as a fault. On by default: the PC's switch is the only one both apps read, and turning it off hides the calendar everywhere without deleting one event. Two things the PC does not send, the phone does not pretend to know: the read window starts at the PC's today and runs 90 days forward, so a day outside it says "Not read" instead of "Nothing on this day", and the Repeat choices are the PC's own four (no "Yearly", which the PC refuses). The Add sheet's "Remind me" box says under itself that nothing rings yet - the field is stored for the slice that makes it ring.
- **The four wrong answers the HUD field test caught are answered from this PC now (2026-10-29).** Asked whether it could connect the owner's calendar and email, Jarvis told him connecting Google Calendar "requires a refresh token ... generate an OAuth token, then share the resulting code with me". That was the local model's invention, because no fast path owned the sentence - and every word of it is wrong about this PC: the two-way Google setup is the one-time CLI steps in `docs/GOOGLE-CALENDAR-SETUP.md`, the sign-in comes back to a loopback listener on this PC, and a token, auth code or password is never typed into a chat. "Can you connect my calendar and email", "what about connecting to a gmail account or google calendar?", "connect my google calendar" and "connect my email" are now one deterministic answer naming the four real paths - Google read/write (the CLI setup), the private iCal link on its secure card, GitHub on the same kind of card, and email not built yet with its IMAP details in Settings, Accounts - with no model and no card. "Change your voice to Ashby" no longer dumps every built-in voice id: Ashby and Clara are the two voices made for Jarvis, the optional pack is not made on this PC, and the settings grammar answers the sentence with `settings_choice_bad` and the Voices screen's own note - that they are not made yet, and where the one line that makes them is - instead of listing ids. "What voice are you using" reads the real speaker choice instead of reaching the model, "how do I pair my phone" names Settings, Devices and "Pair a phone" on the PC and "Pair with your PC" on the phone, the "Not connected yet" calendar answer no longer names a Google sign-in row that Settings does not have and keeps the `py -3 jarvis_google_calendar.py mode ical` step that chooses the read-only way, and the Appearance card no longer says the HUD always stays dark (it follows the theme, Daylight too). A made blend still reaches the same cardless voice setter, "connect my calendar" still raises the iCal card, and the model still answers everything none of these own. Proved in `backend/test_hud_fieldtest_answers.py` (521 checks), with the calendar, connect-account, Google and settings suites updated for the sentences that changed hands.
- **The HUD's face is bigger, the hidden Galaxy's space is the face's, and two field bugs are fixed (2026-10-29).** The owner's four sentences on installed 0.2.356, each measured at 1280x650 in the Studio shell before and after. **(1) "the Jarvis face models are too small on the HUD"**: the steps went from 0.75/1/1.25 to **1/1.25/1.5** (Small is the size Medium used to draw, Medium is what Large used to draw), and Studio's own base went from 48px to **120px** - his face was 48px (60px with Large chosen) beside a 671px conversation column, and is now **150px** (180px at Large). Cockpit 220px becomes 275px at Medium; Ambient keeps its 320px base in a tall window (400px at Medium) and uses a 240px base under 800px tall, because at 650px tall the raised steps pushed the composer off the bottom (measured: composer at y=691 in a 650px window). Every rule that sizes the face still multiplies its own base, so all twelve keep their sizes and the three-step control still remembers this device's choice. **(2) "when the Galaxy is hidden, the face model should take up that space instead of showing the description + Show the galaxy button"**: in Studio the freed 45% Inspector column is gone - the stage is one column, the face is the centrepiece at **200px x the device's step (250px at Medium)**, and the "The galaxy is hidden..." paragraph is no longer drawn at all (it stays as the screen-reader sentence). The way back is ONE small chip, "Show the galaxy", in the stage bar beside [Galaxy options] and always inside the window; the Galaxy options -> Hide toggle is unchanged and is the second way. What hiding MEANS did not change: the picture, its dots, its legend and its trace still go. **(3) "the faces panel is clipped on its first open (top 497/height 468 in a 650px window; Close off-screen; reopens fine)"**: the panel was placed when it opened, and the 25 face rows land afterwards, so it was measured while one "Reading the face list..." line tall and then grew past the bottom (measured top 495, bottom 963, Close at 928-952 of 650). A render that lands while the panel is open re-places it. **(4) "the header chip can read NO AI MODEL right after a quick-path turn while the model is up"**: a quick-path answer really does send `"lane": "no AI model"` in `X-Jarvis-Route` (`backend/jarvis_quick.py`'s `LANE`), and the HUD wrote it into the model chip - the Current Models door, which boot paints from `/api/status` and nothing repainted afterwards. The chip now keeps the real model on a quick turn; the telemetry rail's own row still says which lane answered that turn. **Not proven on the owner's own desktop:** his installed app is not rebuilt, so every number above is a browser drawing this branch's page at his window size, not his app.
- **Edit HUD can put any feature on the main screen (2026-10-29).** The owner asked (2026-10-29) that the features they want within one click be addable to the main screen from Edit HUD, with an equivalent edit screen on the phone. This slice is the desktop half: a catalogue of 24 feature cards keyed by the feature's own id in `features/features.json`, a "Main screen cards" panel inside Edit HUD (Add, Remove, move earlier/later, fold away, reset), and a strip at the top of the HUD's stage that draws exactly the cards chosen, in the order chosen. A card whose feature is off says so in a whole sentence ("Calendar is turned off. Turn it on in Settings."), never a blank box and never JSON. The safety surfaces - the approvals queue, Stop everything, app lock, What asks first and the stale link - are deliberately not in the catalogue, so no layout can hide or move them. `tests/home-cards.mjs` (25 checks) covers add/remove/reorder and proves a brand-new feature key can be added by one catalogue row and appears; it also proves a card folded away actually leaves the strip (the 2026-10-30 fix: `homeCardsFor` had ignored `on`, so the note said "folded away" while the card stayed drawn). The phone's equivalent edit screen is the next slice; `docs/HUD-MAIN-SCREEN-CARDS-2026-10-29.md` carries its work order.
- **A card answered anywhere leaves every live surface within 30 seconds, a killed phone's widget within 30 minutes, and a decision now says which surface made it (2026-10-29; the widget bound corrected and the beat hardened after review, 2026-10-30).** The owner's report: *"when a decision/approval card has already been addressed (approved or denied) on either the Android app or the desktop program, make sure the Android widgets (and every other surface that shows a card/notification) update to reflect that - no stale card left showing, no dead Approve/Deny on a resolved card."* The queue was already one list - `GET /api/pending`, with a decided row gone from the next read and a second answer refused - so what was missing was the second half of "one source": the `approval` event is a **doorbell that can be missed** (the backend's pump publishes only on change and swallows its own exceptions, and a pump that is not running rings nothing at all), and the phone had no clock of its own to fall back on. The desktop's HUD page has had a 30-second safety-net read since long before, and its stream re-reads the queue on every connect; the phone re-read it only when an event arrived. Now both platforms ask the same question on the same 30 seconds while a card is waiting on a **connected** link - `QueueBeat.due` (phone, re-reading through `refreshPending`, the one assignment `pending` gets, so the home-screen widget, the notification drawer, Home and the Inbox plate all clear in one beat) and `queue_beat_due` (`stream.rs`, re-reading through `refresh_pending`, the one read the bar, the widget, the tray and the HUD follow). The rule is **two conditions on both sides**, and `stale` is deliberately not one of them: a failed read is what makes a link stale, and the next successful read is what clears it, so suppressing the beat while stale switches off the only retry that can recover - one transient `/api/pending` failure held Approve until the hourly reconnect while Deny still worked. Neither reads anything when the queue is empty, so this is not a poll of an idle PC, and neither can approve or deny anything. Two review rounds hardened the halves that could fail silently: the PC's beat can no longer move the stream's 70-second silence deadline (it is absolute - `last_chunk_at + SILENCE_TIMEOUT` - so `"Jarvis went quiet"` still fires on a dead socket), and a phone response is applied only if no newer queue read has started, so a beat's slow pre-decision GET cannot re-add a card the owner just answered. Two more halves: the approvals widget now carries `updatePeriodMillis="1800000"` (the board widget's own period), because a RemoteViews drawn by a killed process is otherwise held by the launcher for ever - a cold redraw says "Not checked yet" instead of a card it cannot vouch for - which is also why the bound is not one number for every surface: **30 seconds while the app is running and the link is live; up to 30 minutes for a killed phone's widget; and while the desktop's stream is down there is no beat at all, so its bound is the next event or reconnect** (a stale-but-connected link recovers on the next beat). And the phone finally says **who** answered - it sent only `{"id"}` on `/api/approve` and `/api/deny`, so the route's `"hud"` default recorded every phone decision as the browser HUD's (the owner's field evidence: a phone deny stored as `decided_by:"hud"`); it now sends `"by": "phone"` from one constant used by both bodies. Proven by `backend/test_decided_by_attribution.py` (13 checks: the route reads `by` and defaults to hud, a decision is stored with the word it was sent, the card leaves the next read, a second answer is refused), `QueueBeatTest` (6: the rule's cases, the stale matrix, and a source check that the runtime runs the beat and does not stack a second), `PendingRefreshOrderTest` (4: a slow pre-decision response cannot overwrite the post-decision empty list, and a superseded failure cannot mark a fresh success stale), `WidgetStatesTest` (the widget's period, read from the XML a launcher reads), `SignedApprovalTest` (both bodies carry the same word), `ApiContractTest`'s instrumentation check of the posted body, and `stream.rs`'s `the_queue_beat_runs_only_for_a_waiting_card_on_a_connected_link`, `silence_wins_at_the_absolute_deadline_while_the_beat_keeps_firing` (behavioural, plus a control that the old timeout-rebuild shape never reaches silence) and `a_failed_queue_beat_is_recovered_by_the_next_read`. **Not proven:** any of it on a phone - no APK from this branch has been installed, so the field step is a CI APK, a card raised, denied on the desktop, and the home-screen widget clearing within 30 seconds; the 30-minute launcher period was not observed on a device either.
- **A file in a chat: drop it on the desktop HUD, or pick it on the phone, and this PC reads it (2026-10-29).** The file rides in the newest message of the chat request that already exists - no new route, no upload, nothing written to disk - and `jarvis_attached_file.py` turns it into at most 12,000 characters of text before any model sees it: PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, Markdown, text and CSV up to 2.5 MB, validated by kind and magic bytes, with `.env` and key names refused, zip bombs stopped by the existing guard, chat markers stripped, and the words labelled outside text. `jarvis_router.choose(has_file=True)` answers gate "file" before the budget, the complexity check and the offer, and the conversation latches private, so a later web search in that chat still asks. No card of its own (the existing reading-files switch decides), no new Settings row, no new permission, and pictures stay on the picture path. Honest limits: a second question about the same file needs it attached again, and a file bigger than 2.5 MB is a "put it in a folder Jarvis may look in" job.
- **The phone can attach a file to a question, and the PC reads it (2026-10-29).** "Put this thing in front of Jarvis" is the commonest thing the owner does with a file, and on the phone it now takes one control instead of three: **More → Attach a file** opens Android's own file picker (`ActivityResultContracts.OpenDocument()`, the same door the custom-voice WAV already uses) filtered to the seven kinds `my_files` already reads - PDF, Word `.docx`, Excel `.xlsx`, PowerPoint `.pptx`, Markdown, text and CSV. The file rides inside the **newest user message** as one `attachment` object (`name`, `mime`, `size`, raw base64 `data`) with `"has_file": true` beside `has_image`, in the same `/api/chat` body, over the same private link, under the same token - **no new route, no upload, no new permission, no new switch**. The PC turns the bytes into text itself (at most 12,000 characters, with a sentence saying how much was left out) and keeps the turn on the local model; the router's `has_file` gate refuses any cloud lane, and the chat counts as having read outside text, so a later web search or note still asks first - the chip says so. The cap is **2,500,000 raw bytes** (base64 grows it by a third; the largest allowed file plus the most conversation the phone sends is about 3.55 MB against the PC's 4 MiB body cap), refused with the real size and the one thing that works for a bigger file ("put it in a folder Jarvis may look in"). **Nothing is written anywhere on the phone:** the bytes are read straight into memory, never to storage, a preference, a Bundle or the log, and they are dropped the moment the question is sent - the chip goes with them, because the file is not kept and a later question does not carry it. No preview and no text extraction on the phone: the PC is the only place a file's words are read. Pictures keep their own path, untouched. Tests: `AttachFileTest.kt` (the cap to the byte, the refusals' exact words, the names, base64 correctness, the four wire fields exactly as the backend fixture has them, `has_file` beside `has_image`, and that a later question carries neither) and a new case in `ApiContractTest` read off the wire through the real `ChatSession`. **Not proven on a phone:** no APK from this branch has been installed - GitHub Actions is the only compiler for this app, and the first real attach is one CI APK and one picked PDF away.
- **The home-screen approval widget's Deny really denies, even with the phone locked (2026-10-28).** The owner tapped **Deny** on CI APK v0.2.2979 with the phone in App lock / private view and it only **opened the app**, because `ApprovalWidgetRules.denyActsDirectly(hidden, denyOk) = !hidden && denyOk` treated a hidden card as a reason to route the refusal through the app. Deny now acts directly whenever the card allows an unread deny (`notice.denyOk != false`), hidden or not - refusing is the safe direction, and hiding the card's words is a display rule. The one card that still opens the app is the one the server marks `deny_ok: false` (a denial that needs a confirmation): it draws **one** app-opening action, "Deny in app" unhidden and "Review" while locked, and never a button labelled Deny that only opens the app. PC-only cards are unchanged - their deny already works from the phone, so only `deny_ok: false` moves Deny into the app. The locked wording ("Needs your OK / A decision is waiting"), the hidden title, and a locked card's real Deny are pinned in `ApprovalWidgetRulesTest`, `DenyNotHeldTest` and `WidgetStatesTest`. **Not proven on a phone:** no APK from this branch has been installed; the retest is one CI APK and one tap on Deny with the phone locked.
