- **Wave 4: the Galaxy gets its frame budget on a machine with no usable GPU, and an open History group's chevron points down instead of reading as collapsed (2026-10-28).** Two pieces of work were still sitting on two stale HUD pull requests (#910, #914) and had never reached `main`, so they are recovered here against `origin/main` rather than by merging either branch. **The frame budget** is the pair of rules `#910`'s `perf(hud)` commit was the second half of: a slow renderer first loses PIXELS - `nextRenderScale` steps the 3D canvas's device pixel ratio down towards `RENDER_SCALE_MIN` (0.25) while the 2D label canvas, the CSS size, the camera and the projection never move, so text stays crisp and the picture never shrinks on screen - and only a machine still slower than `SLOW_FRAME_MS` (a 34ms median frame, about 30 a second) after that draws every OTHER frame, advancing the clock and the animation on the cheap frame so the motion steps rather than the page freezing. Both halves are now in `galaxy-cosmos.js` and its two vendored copies, and `info()` reports `scale`, `ratio` and `halfRate` so a test can read the budget instead of trusting it. This is genuinely new to `main`: the pickaxe finds no commit on `main` that ever contained `nextRenderScale` (the branch that had it merged `main` *into itself* and the resolution rule arrived in that merge's conflict resolution, which is why squashing lost it). **The chevron** is `#914`'s smaller fix: the History panel's caret is one right-pointing chevron (`chevron: "M9 6l6 6-6 6"` in `hud-history.js`), so the CSS rotation is the whole reading - and the rule on `main` rotated the CLOSED case by -90 degrees, which left an OPEN group showing an unrotated right-pointing caret beside its own rows, i.e. looking collapsed. The rule is now keyed on `data-open="1"` and rotates 90 degrees, so open points down and collapsed points right. Tests: `tests/galaxy-cosmos.mjs` 35 -> 42 checks (7 new, pure Node, no GPU: the thresholds are ordered, a fast renderer never exceeds the display's ratio, a slow one steps down and stops at the floor, a very slow one jumps straight there, a cheap picture earns pixels back one step at a time, a steady frame holds, nonsense input is never a broken picture, and the rule is monotone - worse frames never give more pixels) and `tests/hud-history.mjs` 37 -> 38 (1 new: the open/collapsed caret rules in all three page copies). The browser suites `tests/hud-galaxy-looks.mjs` and `tests/hud.mjs` also pass, so the engine still draws all eight looks and the page still passes its own wiring checks. **What was deliberately NOT taken, and why:** `HALO_LIMIT` (drop the glow haloes past 120 names) is also absent from `main` and was left out - it changes what the picture looks like with no test and no measurement on `main`'s newer per-role node builder, so it is a follow-up with its own evidence rather than a tag-along. The branch's instanced-link renderer (`linkMesh`/`LineSegments`) was left out because `main` superseded it with a newer, tested link-plan cache (`state.plans`, `a38ba8cbd`), and the branch's literal-invoke rework of `hud-history.js` was left out because `main` solved the same `check_invoke_grants` problem its own way (`eb2d3321b`). Everything else the two branches carry is already on `main` and newer there - their `jarvis_hud.html`, `hud-galaxy-looks.mjs`, `hud.mjs`, `docs/JARVIS-API.md`, `capabilities/hud.json` and changelog fragments are all strict subsets of what `main` now holds. **Honest limits:** the perf numbers quoted on the commit this came from (134 -> 209 rAF callbacks in ten seconds at 240 names on a software renderer) are that author's measurement, not re-measured here - what is proved here is the rule's own behaviour as pure maths plus the browser suites still passing; no real WebView2 painting was driven on this PC, so a rebuild and a look at the HUD is what confirms the feel.
- **Asking Jarvis to change his voice now works without the model (2026-10-28).** "Change your voice to Ashby" matched no phrase, so it went to the local model, which has no voice control - while "set the built-in voice to heart" already changed the same setting. "Your voice" and "his voice" are now names of the one `voice_speaker` choice, and "use the Clara voice" / "switch to the Ashby voice" are one closed-world sentence that only fires on a voice the pack really offers, so the natural sentence reaches `jarvis_settings_registry.set_voice_speaker` - the same `jarvis_voices.set_speaker` the Settings picker and `POST /api/voice/voices/speaker` call. It is the built-in voice only and **no card is raised either way**, because this change is cosmetic and PC-wide, the same rule the speed and the manner already follow; it is one `voices/state.json` write and one `voices` event, so the desktop and the phone both follow. Ashby and Clara also answer to the short name the owner says, not just their picker label ("Ashby (made for Jarvis)") - the label alone never matched the sentence. A name this PC's voice pack does not offer is refused with the real voice names and changes nothing; "use the passive voice" and "change the volume" are untouched and still reach the model. A recorded voice is a different, carded feature (`custom_voice`, Voices screen) and no chat sentence can select one. Proved in `backend/test_settings_registry.py` and `backend/test_settings_tool.py`; **not field-tested on the owner's PC** - the v1.0 pack that offers Ashby is not the pack on this dev machine, and the test supplies that pack's own ids rather than typing a voice name.
- **The HUD now offers to start the local model when its own line says the model is not running (2026-10-28).** The owner opened Jarvis from scratch, saw the model was down, and asked for "a connect button next to these notifications saying its offline". Both offline lines in the HUD now carry one: the standing "The local model isn't running on your PC." row and the "Model not running" banner. It sends the Jarvis bar's own two commands (`read_model_lane` and `start_model_lane`, under a new narrow `hud-lane` grant), so it runs the owner's own Windows logon task for the lane the chat is on and raises no approval card - the same action the bar, the widget and the phone already offer, and it cannot turn the automatic restart on. Starting is not arriving: the button says "Starting…" and the warning clears only when the lane really answers; a refusal, or a lane that never comes up, is shown in the PC's own words with the warning left standing. The button is hidden when the PC has not said, and when the HUD cannot reach the PC at all. The second half of the same day's report - "why does a powershell open automatically?" - turned out not to be the app: its Python backend is already spawned with no window, and the visible window is the owner's own Ollama lane task, which Task Scheduler draws in his session. `scripts/hide-lane-window.ps1` is the one-command fix for that (it checks by default, backs the task up, adds `-WindowStyle Hidden` only when `-Apply` is given, and reads the task back); the lane's own output still goes to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\JarvisOllama\logs\main.log`, so nothing becomes undiagnosable. Tests: `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud-connect.mjs` (27 browser-free checks, including the HUD's start loop and `lane-start.js`'s agreeing scenario for scenario, and a regression pin for the browser probe's finding that the live stream's state must not hide the button), `backend/test_hide_lane_window.py` (33 checks) and a Rust source pin in `proctree.rs` for the app's no-console facts. Not field-tested: no press has been made on the owner's PC from this branch, and the lane-window fix has been run with `-Check` only - `-Apply` is the owner's one command.
- **Six silent failures in the desktop and the backend now say what really happened (2026-10-27 audit).** The preset apply's rollback checks all three restores (model, chat lane, chosen preset), names only the ones it saw work, lists any it could not put back in `not_restored`, and no longer answers a blanket "were rolled back" (`backend/jarvis_presets.py`, `backend/test_presets_apply.py`). A connect **timeout** now says "Your PC isn't answering. It may be asleep or switched off, or Tailscale or NordVPN Meshnet may be off at one end" instead of "Jarvis isn't running on your PC", which sent the owner to press Start on a service that may never have stopped (`plain_errors.rs`, the page's own `pc_unreachable` words, `tests/plain-errors.mjs`). The HUD's Presets menu now applies through the shared invoke budget, so `apply_focus_preset`'s 1,250 s budget is reachable from the one surface that applies presets (`hud-presets.js`, `tests/hud-shell.mjs`, `tests/invoke-budget.mjs`); it is a served page module, so `invoke-budget.js` is shipped beside the server (`backend/static/`, `jarvis-backend/`, both `$SHIPPED` lists, the `BESIDE` table, and a `STATIC_ASSETS` key) or the browser HUD would 404 the import. The direct-invoke inventory is 46 files / 97 sites in `src/*.js` plus the 12 in `src/*.html` - 109 across `src`, down from 115 (`hud-presets.js` 5 -> 0, `browser-engine.js` 3 -> 0; the Learning card's two calls and one file left this list in the 2026-10-28 audit follow-up); the rest are counted by `tests/invoke-budget.mjs`, not converted. A Today board that could not be read is no longer drawn as a clear day: Rust keeps "read", "this PC has no board" and "the read failed" apart and the page says "Jarvis could not read today's board from your PC. That does not mean nothing is due" with a Retry (`brain/schedule.rs`, `coming-up.js`, `today.js`, `brain.js`, `tests/today-board-unread.mjs`, `tests/today.mjs`). The browser-engine card says "Saved." only after reading the mode back, and "Asked your PC." otherwise (`browser-engine.js`), and a dropped HUD push, quickbar event or broadcast now goes to the log FILE rather than an `eprintln!` a release build cannot see (`lib.rs`, `notifications.rs`). **Limits, stated:** the HUD's gap detector for a missed event push is not in this change, the Playwright and live-backend suites were not run at this desk, and the Android rows of the same audit belong to the sibling lane.
- **The Settings menu gets the two learning switches, and the audit's wrong Settings claims are corrected (2026-10-28).** The 2026-10-27 settings audit found that `background_learning` and `learn_sensitive_topics` were real switches with a route, a spoken name and an approval card but no Settings row on either app; they now have a **Learning** card on both (desktop `settings.html#learning` + `learning-settings.js`, phone `SettingsScreen.kt` item `learning` + `LearningSettingsPlate.kt`), drawn from the Brain's own `brain_memory_learning`, `brain_memory_learning_sensitive` and `brain_memory_learning_status` commands through one deliberately narrow permission set (`learning-settings`: the switch state and the two switch writes, never the fact list, the saved ids, Forget or export). The feature list's read-aloud row no longer sends the owner to a Voice switch that does not exist: it now names the two Voice choices that really decide whether memory-based answers are read aloud and the HUD's "Read-aloud buttons on answers" row, and says plainly that the HUD row does not turn read-aloud on or off. Two switches that a bad 2026-10-11 merge had put in the wrong cards are back where the registry always said they were - "Let Jarvis Desktop start and stop Jarvis" in Starting Jarvis for you (it was hidden inside the Pictures body), and "Making pictures" in Pictures (it was in the unrelated "Startup and logs" card) - and the settings tests now prove a row is drawn INSIDE the card its section names, not merely that it exists somewhere on the page. The stale `SETTINGS_GAPS["history"]` record is gone: the chat-history switch and its retention have been in Settings, Security since 2026-10-22, so `chat_history.section` is `security` and the gap table is empty for the first time. A real bug the fix uncovered is fixed with it: because "Lock Jarvis" (app-lock) and the chat-history switch share the Security section, an Always-On safety title would have found one switch in its section and turned chat history on; `switches_for()` now refuses to link any switch to an Always-On row, and `tools/check_feature_controls.py`'s own guard 2 passes. Proven by `python -B tools/gen_settings_cases.py --check`, `tools/check_feature_controls.py` (31/31 switches, 34/34 rows, 53/53 sections), `tools/check_feature_list.py`, `tools/check_parity.py`, `tools/check_stale_twins.py`, `backend/test_settings_rows.py` (21), `backend/test_settings_registry.py` (775), `backend/test_feature_guide.py` (1815), `backend/test_feature_controls.py` (16), `backend/test_feature_list.py` (41), `backend/test_menu_visibility.py` (450), the base twin suites (123 + 39), and the desktop settings tests including a new `learning-settings.mjs` and a new per-row card-placement check in `settings-catalogue.mjs`; the phone's own compile is CI's first Android build of this branch, and no Rust source changed - only the permission set and the settings capability that a node security test parses.
- **Settings, Devices can pair the phone again, and the card no longer calls an unreachable PC an old one:** The owner reported that his Pixel 9's Jarvis app was not pairing and that the app's Settings had nothing for "pair"/"reconnect to the PC". Both halves were true, and the second one is why the first could not be fixed from the phone: the pairing flow itself was complete (`net/Pairing.kt`, `PairingScreen.kt`, the QR code and the typed code), but the pairing screen is drawn only while `!paired || repairing` (`MainActivity.kt:2150`), so a phone that still holds a token - exactly the state a rotated key, a reinstalled PC or a moved address leaves it in - never saw it again. Settings, Devices, the one card that talks about pairing, offered exactly one pairing-adjacent control: "Unpair & clear the token", which takes the pairing AWAY and then says "Pair again to use Jarvis" with no way to do it. The two non-destructive ways back existed but could not be found by name: the Home status line, which looks like text rather than a button, and Platform checks -> "Change desktop or token". Settings, Devices now draws "Pair with your PC" as a Primary action above "Unpair & clear the token", in BOTH branches of that card, and it runs the same two lines Platform checks already runs, so there is one definition of how re-pairing starts. The card also stopped making a claim it could not support: it drew "Your PC's Jarvis does not have a device list yet" whenever the phone had not read the PC's version, which is also the state of a PC that is simply off, asleep or logged out of Tailscale - so a paired phone was told its PC was too old when the truth was that it could not be reached. `Devices.notShown` now chooses between that sentence and "This phone cannot reach your PC right now, so its list of devices is not shown here." `PairFromSettingsTest` adds 6 JVM tests: the sentence choice both ways, the button drawn above Unpair in both branches, the handler really setting `repairing`, and the label staying plain words. **Not proven here:** anything on a phone - the Kotlin is CI-compiled only, and no APK from this branch was installed, so the field-test step is the CI-built APK, open Settings, Devices, and expect "Pair with your PC" above "Unpair & clear the token".
- **The live-backend reconcile tool can no longer "succeed" over a patch it silently skipped, and the four 2026-10-27 patches now have exact rules (2026-10-28).** The live-drift audit measured the bug: `tools/reconcile_live_backend.py` decided a patch was `ALREADY_PRESENT` from "any one distinctive added line", and two of `cloud-escalate.patch`'s *added* lines are lines its own hunks show in the old text (`_says = _api.CloudAnswer(`, `def _lane_names() -> list:`) - so a live HUD with none of its three hunks read as present, and `--apply` would have finished `RESULT: PASS` with the cloud-escalation security and behaviour changes absent. A probe is now only an added line that appears NOWHERE in the patch's own pre-image (the `-` and context lines of its hunks), plus a `DELTA_TABLE` entry's explicit probes (every one required); a patch that can be proved neither present nor absent is REFUSED by name with what it needs and how to apply it by hand (`scripts/apply-patches.ps1 -BackendPath <live>`), and the live `_jarvis-state.json` patch list is no longer accepted as proof - it records a past run, not the bytes now in the file. The brief's first reference, the published base `jarvis-backend/<file>`, was measured and rejected as the filter: that folder is a snapshot of the owner's LIVE folder with the whole stack already in it, so 132 of the 160 patches have every added line there (e.g. `_PUMP = _ev.Pump(engine=_ENGINE)` from `events-pump.patch` at `jarvis-backend/jarvis_hud.py:6307`), and "absent from the base" would have refused 132 patches on any live file. The four patches are carried as their own hunks: `cloud-escalate.patch` and `f01-manual-lane-gate.patch` on `jarvis_hud.py`, `reminder-actions.patch` (`schedule_action` in both gate places) and `update-in-chat.patch` (`desktop_update` in the never-a-standing-rule set) on `jarvis_gate.py`. The dry run now prints every refusal in full instead of "... and N more", and the ref-guard refusal prints the exact `git worktree add` command, because the tool still refuses to run from a checkout that is not at `--ref`. `backend/test_reconcile_live_backend.py` proves the trap on the published base itself (the removed rule called it present), that cloud-escalate absent is refused rather than skipped even when the patch list records it, that each rule's staged text is byte-for-byte what `git apply` makes of the same patch, that a second run reports `ALREADY_PRESENT`, and that no test writes to a live folder (375 checks). **Not measured:** the tool was not run against the owner's live folder, so a dry run there may now name patches the old state-file fallback used to wave through; each is either genuinely absent (apply-patches.ps1) or present-but-rewritten (add a rule). A patch with no `DELTA_TABLE` entry is still judged by "any one pre-image-absent added line is present", which rejects a re-written line but is not a byte-exact proof - `docs/DEPLOY.md` says so in its limits.
- **The deploy tool now carries three live-folder delta rules it needed on this PC: `briefing.patch`, `learning-extract-reason.patch` and `model-recommendations.patch` (2026-10-28).** The owner's backend deploy (`tools/reconcile_live_backend.py`, dry-run then `--apply`) refused two patches by name on 2026-10-28 because their live files are newer than the patch pre-images and `DELTA_TABLE` had no rule for them - the tool refused rather than guessed, exactly as designed, but that left the morning briefing's routes and the learning fix's error reason undelivered. The three entries add the rules: briefing is five HUD hunks plus one gate hunk (its gate hunk probes the live wording, which already names the morning briefing, so a re-deploy reports it present instead of re-inserting it); the learning fix is several hunks in `jarvis_extract.py` with post-image probes from different regions; and the advisor's install block is one hunk immediately before the main socket, with its own probes. Every rule is evidenced (pre-image exactly once or refused; post-image probes so a second deploy skips instead of duplicating) and the dry run on the owner's folder goes from 2 refusals to 0. Proven by `backend/test_reconcile_live_backend.py` (all 375 checks) and three real dry runs against the live folder on 2026-10-28, the last of which wrote nothing and reported **0 refusals, 226 planned changes**.
- **The first page now says what actually ships (2026-10-28).** `README.md` gained this wave's user-facing work where it already lists features - the **Anti-Slop filter** (off by default, cardless both ways, the phone carrying the same switch and em-dash box), the **model and preset advisor** (Check for new models, Benchmark on this PC, a preset recommendation you approve through the existing cards, PC-only), the HUD's **live theme follow**, its **faces list** with the **Face size** row and the **Start the local model** button, the two-way **Google Calendar** sign-in beside the private iCal link, and **connecting a calendar or GitHub by talking** into Windows Credential Manager - plus the sentence that changes the built-in voice by asking, the Android **Settings → Devices → Pair with your PC** way back in, and the card lifecycle (15 minutes by default, four cards with no deadline, a lapsed card refused rather than approved and cleared off the phone by the next read). It corrects the Settings directory's stale figures (141 features / 41 switchable / 11 enforced / 30 planned became 142 / 42 / 14 / 28, with the reason on the page), says the rolling release tags are where a version number lives (0.2.355 for the desktop and 0.2.3116 for the phone on 2026-10-28), names the seven required checks that gate a merge, and adds the honest limits this wave needs: the HUD pieces are page-tested rather than seen in the installed window, the voice sentence and the pair-again button are proved by tests and a compile rather than by saying or tapping them, and the Google sign-in is still a command-line setup with no Settings row and nothing on the phone. No rule, owner quote, licence or launch-video text changed; `tools/check_readme_truth.py` and `tools/check_docs_map.py` are green.
- **The HUD's memory graph now forgets when the private lists hide, and the hidden-lists setting blocks desktop captures on its own (2026-10-28).** The seven findings of the 2026-10-27 privacy audit (`.dsh-scratch/AUDITS-2026-10-27/PRIVACY.md`). P-1 (High): `emit_private_hidden` broadcasts `private-hidden` to every window instead of two, and the HUD page listens for it and for `security-changed`, drops every word it holds (the galaxy's nodes and labels, the inspector, the search box, the recall trace, the trail of the owner's own questions, the node buttons) and then re-reads `/api/graph`, which comes back redacted - so a Show that ended can no longer be read off a map that was already drawn, and the counts line says "Memory lists are hidden until you unlock." in `hud-safety.js`'s own words instead of claiming "no saved facts on this PC yet". P-2 (the owner's decision, 2026-10-28): `capture_blocked_from` checks `private_hidden` BEFORE the owner-is-here answer, so "Hide memory lists and chat history" alone keeps Jarvis's windows out of a screenshot on the PC as it already did on the phone - it could never block anything before, because `app_locked` is false whenever App lock is off. P-3: the phone's one-time backup recovery code now raises `FLAG_SECURE` while it is drawn (`Backup.codeShown` -> `SecurityRules.blockScreenCapture`'s new `codeShown`), so a screenshot, a cast or the recent-apps picture cannot take the one key to a backup. P-5: the two toasts that carried the PC's own words (a reminder that would not open, a Snooze answer) go through `notify_guarded`, with fixed sentences that keep the meaning. P-6: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` no longer says the desktop has no screenshot block, and the HUD's Reminders comments now match the code (the panel is re-read on the same event). P-7: Live's and "Live ended - Resume"'s notifications are redacted on the lock screen by the shared `notificationsLocked` rule. **P-4 was not a bug and no code changed for it:** the report's mechanism does not exist - in Tauri 2 a page's global `listen()` registers with the target `Any`, and `match_any_or_filter` lets an `Any` listener through every filter, so the widget's `private-hidden` listener already fired before this PR (`tauri-2.12.5/src/event/listener.rs:346`); the broadcast keeps it true by construction. **The limits, plainly:** Live's foreground notification still tells a stranger that Jarvis is on this phone - a foreground service must show a notification, and `VISIBILITY_SECRET` is not honoured for one - so what was removed is the session's own state line (minutes left, Mic off/Mic on, the PC's why); and while "Hide memory lists" is on, desktop captures are blocked nearly all of the time, which is the cost the owner chose with P-2. No route, command or token changed.
- **The presets are one list on both screens, and a personality switched on the PC shows on the phone without reopening it (2026-10-28).** The owner's ask was "make sure the presets for jarvis personality and other presets are consistent too", after a voice that disagreed with every screen showing it. The phone's "How Jarvis talks" card now draws the PC's own `GET /api/manner` personality presets - the same ten names, descriptions and order the desktop's Settings window draws - marks the PC's `personality` and nothing else, and switches with the same one field (`{"personality": "<id>"}`); before this it offered only Warm/Plain and marked "Plain" as in use whenever the PC was on any other preset. A new `manner` event re-reads that card when a personality is switched elsewhere, and the desktop's older-PC fallback list is now held word-for-word to `contract/personalities.json` (it was one title behind: "Socratic Coach" against the PC's "Socratic Coach & Tutor"). The four dials and the "My personalities" editor stay the desktop's, and the phone says where they are. Honest limit: nothing was driven on a real phone - the phone half is JVM tests plus a successful compile.
- **The plug-in catalogue accounts for all 37 drop-in folders (2026-10-28).** Ten drop-ins that replace no patch - receipts, skill-hub, vault, voiceprint, notion-obsidian, media-index, sandbox, self-improve, auto-router and vault-organizer - were in neither `plugins/registry.json` nor `plugins/README.md`, which said 27 drop-in modules while `plugins/ready/` held 37. The generator now lists them from their own `plugin.json` and refuses a folder it cannot account for; `backend/test_plugin_catalogue.py` pins both.
- **The phone's "Needs your OK" line no longer covers "Unpair & clear the token", and typing "pair" now finds Devices (2026-10-28).** Two defects on one screen, both measured on the owner's phone and both Gemini's `F4`. **The covered control:** the fixed approvals line is drawn by `MainActivity` in a `Box` the size of the screen, aligned to the bottom, so it takes no room from the screen underneath, and Settings' list padded its bottom by 16 dp - at max scroll the last card's last control sat under the line. On the installed APK `uiautomator` put the Devices card's Unpair button at `[90,2058][990,2202]` while the line's own top was measured at `y=2064`, a 6 px sliver of a 144 px button. The line is now **measured as it is drawn** (`onSizeChanged`, because it wraps the PC's own card title) and Settings keeps that height plus the line's 12 dp edge gap and 10 dp of air under its content (`BottomReserve`, `SettingsScreen(bottomReserve = ...)`), so the last card scrolls clear of it; a quiet screen reserves nothing. **The search order:** typing "pair" answered "3 settings match." in screen order - VOICE, then APPEARANCE, then DEVICES - because Voice and Appearance both print the conditional line "Pair with your desktop first - these settings live on it.", every hit was the same weakest tier, and the phone's box filters in the screen's own draw order rather than ranking (the desktop sorts by rank; `FindAnything` already does too). A section's heading, the names of the controls drawn on it and the search index's own synonyms are now the words it NAMES, the sentences it prints are not, and a mention-only match answers **only while nothing names what was typed** (`SettingsSearch.namesIt`, the rule in `matching`) - so "pair" answers Devices alone, the same for "pair with your pc" and "unpair", while "pair with your desktop" still finds the two sections that print it. Devices' card also indexes its own two buttons ("Pair with your PC", "Unpair & clear the token"), so both are findable by the words printed on them. The four shared tiers and `rankPart` are untouched, so the cross-app fixture still holds both engines to one rule set. **Tests:** the phone's JVM suite, 2802 tests in 258 classes, all passing - 6 new in `BottomReserveTest` (the arithmetic, the 16 dp overlap kept as the failing-on-old-code case, and source pins that the screen adds the reserve and `MainActivity` measures the line) and 2 new in `SettingsSearchTest` (the word "pair" names Devices and not the two mentions, plus the negative: prose still answers when nothing names it). **Honest limits:** no approval card could be raised on the phone without writing to it or to the live backend, so the line's own top is Gemini's measured `y=2064` and the button's bounds are this branch's dump - the overlap is that arithmetic, not a fresh screenshot; and no APK from this branch has been installed, so the fixed padding is proved by the unit suite and the layout maths, not yet by a look at the phone.
- **The phone's approval card and the desktop's are the same card wherever a written rule allows it (2026-10-28, the owner's ruling: *"the cards on android should match the cards on desktop"*).** The audit found one of them was a defect rather than a difference: the phone has refused an expired card since DEF-010 (2026-10-11) - no buttons, and the sentence saying why - while all three desktop surfaces went on drawing live **Approve** and **Deny** on the same card, so the same request that had timed out could be pressed on the PC and not on the phone. The rule is now one function (`cardHasExpired`, `jarvis-desktop/src/jarvis-link.js`), used by the Jarvis bar, the widget and the HUD page, and `decideApproval`/`decide`/`apprDecide` refuse an expired card as well, so hiding the buttons is a drawing that matches the rule rather than the rule itself. The sentence is one sentence from `backend/jarvis_card_words.py` (`TIMED_OUT`) drawn by both apps, with the phone's own advice (`TIMED_OUT_NEXT`, "Ask the desktop to raise this again.") as its second half only there, because on the PC the owner is already in front of the window that raises cards. The other difference was a stale copy: `desktop_update` joined `jarvis_owner_check.PC_ONLY_ACTIONS` on 2026-10-26 and the phone's `PcOnlyActions` never heard, so it offered an Approve the PC refuses - the set is now written into `contract/card-words-cases.json` by `tools/gen_card_words_cases.py`, the phone's JVM test reads it, and a future PC-only action fails the required `phone-unit-tests` job instead of a tap. Tests: `jarvis-desktop/tests/expiry-parity.mjs` (new, seven checks over the rule, the sentence, the HUD page's own copy and both desktop scripts), `card-words.mjs` + `approvals-contract.mjs`, the phone's `CardWordsContractTest`/`ExpiredCardShowsNoButtonsTest`/`AuditPlumbingTest`, and `backend/test_card_words.py`'s two new checks. **Not aligned, on purpose or not yet:** the desktop still has no "start short / Explain more" fold (`/api/card-prefs` is `desktop-todo` in `tools/check_parity.py`, with `docs/DESKTOP-APPROVAL-CARD-PREF.md` naming the work), the desktop still asks Windows Hello at the moment of approval rather than saying so beforehand the way the phone's "Fingerprint follows" does, and the phone's risk line is drawn as pills plus a swipe sentence where the desktop uses one sentence - all three are listed in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §8.
- **The phone's History keeps a pin where a pin means, and the dead second section rule is gone (2026-10-28).** The phone read `pinned` from `GET /api/history` but threw `pinned_at` away, so the Pinned group was whatever the Newest/Oldest/Title sort made of the pinned rows: the newest pin did not go to the top of Pinned, and the sort control could reorder a group whose order the contract fixes. `ChatLog.Summary` now carries `pinned_at` (read from the list and from a word search), `HistoryView.pinnedRows` orders the Pinned group `pinned_at DESC, updated_at DESC` - the rule in `docs/JARVIS-API.md` section 18.3 and the desktop HUD's own order - whatever the sort says, and a successful pin moves the row using the PC's `pinned_at` from the pin answer (the phone's clock stands in when an older PC echoes none). Two more honesty fixes ride along: the Pinned header's TalkBack label says "1 chat" for one, and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 8 gets the missing blank line between the faces paragraph and the pinned-messages one, with "Pins are phone-local" now reading "Message pins", so a message pin is never confused with a chat pin. `ChatTags.group` - a second section algorithm that no production code called, only its own tests - is deleted; the one arranger is `HistoryView.sections`, and the shared `tag_group_cases` fixture is still checked against the desktop's `groupRows` in `jarvis-desktop/tests/tags.mjs`. `tools/check_parity.py`'s `/api/history/pin` row no longer claims the phone reads the flag from an opened conversation: it reads the list and search rows, with the pin's moment, and an opened conversation carries no pin control on the phone. Measured: `gradlew testDebugUnitTest --offline` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, 2,771 tests, 0 failures (2,776 before, less six tests of the deleted `ChatTags.group` and one new pin-order test); `check_parity` and `check_docs_map` clean at 405 documents. **Not fixed here:** with the PC's History recording off, a pin can still record a chat id the PC never kept, and its Pinned row still offers Open chat - the PC's history switch is not available to the app at pin time (only HistoryScreen reads `GET /api/history`'s status, and no other route or saved setting carries it), so an honest fix needs a new read or rework rather than a guessed one.
- **A route no app calls is now named by `tools/check_parity.py` instead of passing silently (2026-10-28).** The 2026-10-28 wiring audit found the blind spot: the checker built its route sets ONLY from the desktop's and the phone's own source, so a documented backend route neither app called was in neither set, failed nothing, and "No undecided drift." printed over a feature no app could reach. The checker now also parses `docs/JARVIS-API.md` - its fenced route lines, route tables and method-bearing headings, skipping the doc's own section 8 "draft, proposed, or not actually wired" - and every documented route must be called by an app, already classified, or in a new `BACKEND_ONLY` register with a written reason. An unclassified documented route now fails with the route, the doc line and both ways out. The register carries 29 routes, not the 8 the audit named: the same sweep found 21 more documented routes no app calls (the read-only diagnostics, the blind vault, the uncensored opt-ins, the phone's switched-off Live stream, and others), each with a reason from its own doc section. The audit's eight are the receipts list and Undo, the skill hub's four doors, and the Notion preview and import; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 8 now records them and the register, and `backend/test_parity_callgraph.py` proves an unclassified documented route fails, a classified one passes, and the register fails in both stale directions. The existing counts are unchanged: 295 desktop routes, 259 phone, 241 ported, 50 not porting, 3 still to port, 1 not the backend's, 2 planned. **Honest limits:** the doc universe is a line parser, so a route mentioned only inside a prose sentence is not in it; and 21 of the 29 register entries are "no screen yet", not permanent decisions - the moment an app calls one, the check fails and says to remove the entry.
- **One PC-wide voice: change it in either app and Jarvis sounds like it - in Live too (2026-10-28).** A voice picked in the Android app or the desktop app is now the voice you actually hear, on both, including Jarvis Live. The cause of "I change the voice and Live sounds the same": a chosen personality was quietly speaking in its own voice over the top of the one both apps were showing. A personality now *writes* its voice into that one setting instead of overriding it, so the screen, the event and the sound can no longer disagree. The phone's Live screen also says which voice it is speaking in, and says plainly when your PC has no voice engine and Live is using the phone's own voice - the one case a PC setting cannot change.
- **"Can you process all of my Notion notes into Obsidian?" gets the real steps instead of "I cannot access Notion" (2026-10-28).** The owner asked exactly that on 2026-10-27 at 20:57:32, and the model - which is offered no Notion tool, whose feature guide mentions the folder import only inside the "Folders Jarvis may look in" row, and which answers "I do not have a feature called ..." when asked for a "notion export" - answered that it could not access or process files from Notion, while this PC has had both for weeks: `jarvis_documents.import_notion` (the folder import behind Settings, Folders Jarvis may look in; PC only; no card) and `jarvis_notion_obsidian.py` (the full Notion-export-to-Obsidian-vault converter at `POST /api/notion/import`; PC only; one card). `jarvis_quick.py` now answers that sentence and its close phrasings without the model (`quick: "notion_export"`, read-only, no card): the export is made in Notion (Settings, Export all workspace content, as Markdown & CSV), the .zip is picked under Bring in a Notion export, the notes land in a **NEW** folder Jarvis can read and search, nothing on Notion's side is changed, and the answer says plainly that the vault converter has no button in either app yet. "Open Notion" still opens the app and a reminder that mentions Notion is still a reminder; a sentence with one more condition still goes to the model. **Not proven here:** live model behaviour for the phrasings this grammar does not match - those still reach a model that has no Notion tool.
- **Jarvis can now recommend a local model or preset change - and only the owner's existing cards download or apply anything (2026-10-28).** The backend half of the owner's request: one click checks the public Hugging Face / Ollama catalogues through the model manager's existing keyless pass and caches the shaped candidates (`POST /api/models/check`, `GET /api/models/recommendations`); a bounded local benchmark measures an **installed** model's tokens a second, video memory and context through the existing Ollama path and speed record (`POST /api/models/benchmark`); and preset changes are computed from those measurements and the measured GPU sizes alone (`POST /api/presets/recommend`), with the owner's own note refining them through deterministic keep / lower / raise / quality rules (`POST /api/presets/recommend/opinion`). Nothing downloads and nothing applies: a download is still the existing `/api/models/install` card and a preset change is still the existing `/api/work/apply` card. All five routes are PC-only (403 from another device before the body is read), because the phone must not grow a model catalogue. **Honest limit:** the live Hugging Face lookup was not exercised from this checkout (no network - the suite injects the catalogue pass), the catalogue pass sorts by trending so no "newer than yours" date is claimed, and no model's quality is measured.
- **The Brain can look for newer models, measure one here, and suggest preset changes you approve (2026-10-28).** The Brain's Model card has a "Check for new models" button: it asks the PC to look on Hugging Face, shows each candidate's repository ref, size, quantization and licence (or says the catalogue did not report one), offers a Download that goes through the existing approval card, a "Benchmark on this PC" action that shows tokens a second, graphics memory and context as measured here, and a preset recommendation with its evidence - approve it through the existing preset card, or tell Jarvis what you think and the recommendation comes back revised before you approve. Nothing downloads or applies without a card, and no model is called better until it has been measured on this PC.
- **A learning pass is no longer lost to the notes-table migration (2026-10-28).** `jarvis_auto_learn._init_notes()` migrated `auto_learn_notes` with three bare `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`s, and a new notes table was created WITHOUT `topic_ask`, so every first install ran one of those ALTERs on its first card as well. Two writers exist on one database: the learner's `after_pass` (through `_note_card`) and any thread serving `/api/memory/pending` (through `jarvis_intake.annotate()` -> `card_notes()`), and both can read "no column" before either ALTERs. The loser raised `sqlite3.OperationalError: duplicate column name`, which escaped `_note_card` -> `after_pass`, was recorded by `note_failure` as `after_pass: OperationalError`, and saved or carded nothing - the live HUD's `passes` 0 and `failed` 8. The CREATE now carries `topic_ask`, so a first install runs no migration at all, and the upgrade path's ALTERs are idempotent under the same narrow rule `jarvis_memory._init()` already uses for `facts.retired_at`: only "duplicate column name" is treated as "already there", and `database is locked`, `no such table` and everything else still raise. `backend/test_learning_after_pass.py` (11 checks) fails on the old code with `{'saved': [], 'cards': {}, 'error': 'OperationalError'}` and passes after it; `jarvis-backend/jarvis_auto_learn.py` is byte-identical. **The other half of the silence, in the same release: a failed model call now says WHY.** `jarvis_extract._local_llm()` swallowed every failure into `None`, so the learner could only print "reached no model ... Is it pulled?" - while on 2026-10-28 the model was loaded (9.86 GB VRAM) and answered a one-token POST in 1.969 s with an EMPTY answer (`done_reason` "length"). It now records a CLOSED-WORLD reason - the exception class or the kind of empty answer, the HTTP status, the wall duration and Ollama's own `done_reason`; never a prompt word and never an answer word - keeps returning `None`, and writes it through `jarvis_auto_learn.note_model_failure` into `learning-stats.json`, which `/api/memory/learning` already reads as `counters.model_failures`, `counters.last_model_error` and one plain sentence in `counters.note`; no HUD and no app change. It ships as `backend/learning-extract-reason.patch` because the owner's `jarvis_extract.py` is not one of the modules this repository ships whole, and `backend/test_learning_local_llm_reason.py` (40 checks) proves it, including the bite proof: with the patch taken back off the published base the old code records nothing at all and sends no `think` flag. **It also asks the model to answer, not to think:** `qwen3.5:9b` is a hybrid reasoning model - with a small budget it can spend the whole of it in `thinking` and return the same empty 200 the diagnostic measured (`thinking: "Okay"`) - so the extraction request now carries `"think": False`; an Ollama old enough not to know the field ignores it, so no version probe is needed (Gemini Edition-3 review F1). **It is NOT on the owner's PC yet:** the backend reaches it the way every other one does, by running `scripts/apply-patches.ps1` - no redeploy was done from this branch. **Honest limit:** this pins the one operation on the `after_pass` path that raises `OperationalError` on a first-run or upgrading database; it is NOT proven to be the 2026-10-11 event, because the live counters are stale from that night and today's live database holds no proposals at all - the current zero-facts cause is upstream of `after_pass` and needs the live evidence, and the live reason `_local_llm` gets nothing is still not confirmed: the one authorized POST proves the lane answers a trivial request, not what the extractor's longer, schema-constrained one does.
- **Dead IPC surface removed, and the build folder is ignored where the rule travels (2026-10-28).** The desktop registered and granted a Tauri command, `read_selection`, that no page ever invoked - the selection chip's read happens in Rust before the bar takes focus (`windows.rs`'s `selection::read_selection_quiet`), so the command, its autogenerated permission file and the `allow-read-selection` grant in the `quickbar-surface` set are gone: `tools/check_command_acl.py` now reports **411 commands** (was 412) with `generate_handler!` and `build.rs` agreeing, `tools/check_invoke_grants.py` still reports **344 commands invoked from 11 pages**, all granted, and the selection-chip source pins now assert the dead surface is absent. The read itself is untouched, so the chip still works. Separately, `jarvis-desktop/target/` (**14.79 GB**, 20,134 files on the owner's PC) was excluded only by `.git/info/exclude`, which is never cloned, so a fresh clone could stage it with `git add -A`; it is now in `.gitignore` and `git check-ignore -v` names `.gitignore` rather than the local exclude file. `jarvis-desktop/src-tauri/target/` (33.36 GB here) was already ignored by `.gitignore` and `jarvis-desktop/.gitignore`, so it gained no duplicate rule.
- **The HUD window now follows a theme picked in Settings while it is open, without a reload (2026-10-28 field test).** Driving the installed 0.2.351 app over WebView2 CDP found the theme picker repainting Settings, the main window, the widget and the Brain at once, while the HUD stayed on Reactor until the page was reloaded (`.dsh-scratch/MAXOUT-2026-10-28/FIELDTEST-DESKTOP-2026-10-28/REPORT.md`, row "Theme picker (#theme-list) + fan-out"). The cause was source-visible: `jarvis_hud.html` painted `document.documentElement["data-theme"]` once from `localStorage["jarvis.theme"]` at load and listened for nothing, while every other window subscribes to the `theme-changed` event Rust fans out (`jarvis-link.js` `followTheme`, called by `main.js`, `brain.js`, `settings.js` and `widget.js`). The HUD now listens for that same event with the same payload - the effective theme id, so "Match Windows light or dark mode" arrives already resolved and nothing here reads the OS - and applies it the way `applyTheme` does: normalise, set `data-theme`, write the anti-flash cache so the next load still paints the right theme first. Nothing reloads and no layout moves: the only colours baked into pixels are re-taken (the canvas ink cache, the legend's swatches, and the 3D Galaxy, which bakes its vertex colours when it builds and is rebuilt only while it was drawing - a freshly built engine re-frames its own camera from the look's preset, so a dragged or zoomed 3D view returns to that framing) - the pre-paint read at the top of the page is untouched, so the first frame still has no flash. `jarvis_hud.html`'s three byte-identical copies are updated together. Tests: two new checks in `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud.mjs` - the stored theme is on the root at the first painted frame, and the real `theme-changed` event (driven through the page's own registered listener) changes the window without a reload, with the surface, the ink cache and the legend's swatches all following and the layout rectangles unchanged. Honest limit: the page path and the fan-out are proved in the browser harness here, and the event grant was already in `capabilities/hud.json`, but no build from this branch has been run on the installed app, so the desktop field row is confirmed by the next release.
- **The Galaxy's 3D engine caches its picture instead of rebuilding it every frame, and lets go of its WebGL renderer when the galaxy is hidden (2026-10-28).** The owner's guardrail for this wave: the Planetary Orrery really orbits, so `applyPositions` runs on every frame, and it used to rebuild the picture's bookkeeping each time - a fresh array of position objects, two fresh position maps, and a fresh link array out of `presetLinks` with a control object per link, about 1,500 objects a frame handed to the garbage collector while the GPU was already busy. `galaxy-cosmos.js` now caches the topology behind a dirty flag: the position objects and their map are reused (a moved planet writes three numbers into an object that already exists), the link plan is kept by id and rebuilt only when the SET changes (a new graph, another look, the zen-ring's chords toggle), and every link writes into its own existing `Float32Array` and raises `posAttr.needsUpdate = true`, so three.js re-uploads the vertices instead of the page building a new geometry, material and Line. Measured in Node on this PC against the pre-fix file, same graph and look, 300 frames each: **0.541 ms -> 0.232 ms per frame at 240 names** (2.34x cheaper) and 0.329 -> 0.206 ms at 121 names, with per-frame position-map constructions falling from 8 to 5 - the three removed are exactly the two id->position maps and `presetLinks`' own. The renderer is lazy now too: the page builds no `WebGLRenderer` until the Galaxy is really on screen, and disposes the engine (renderer, geometry, materials, the canvas's context-lost listener) when the owner hides the galaxy or the window is backgrounded; showing it again rebuilds one through the drawing loop's own switch. `tests/galaxy-engine-reuse.mjs` proves the cache with a counting stand-in for three.js (6 checks, no browser): sixty steady frames build no geometry, buffer, line, mesh, material or colour and do not move the engine's reported plan count. **Honest limits:** the layout function itself still runs on an orbiting frame - only the topology around it is cached - the milliseconds above are Node, not a browser, and the GPU frame is untouched; the canvas's GL context is disposed with its renderer but not force-lost, because a force-lost context cannot be handed to a new renderer on the same canvas without `webglcontextrestored` handling and that path is untested at this desk; the engine is deliberately NOT dropped for a mere conversation/map view switch, because the existing idle test measures the engine's draws stopping and resuming and rebuilding a GL context on every toggle would cost more than it saves; nothing here is Playwright-tested locally - CI's frontend job is the first browser run.
- **The HUD's faces panel has a Face size - Small, Medium or Large - that belongs to this device (2026-10-28).** The owner asked that "in the jarvis faces in the left column of the hud there should also be a size option on desktop": the panel now opens with a `Face size` row above the 25 faces (three buttons in its own `.hud-presets-item` look, `aria-pressed` and a `role="group"` like the face rows, Tab/Shift+Tab and Enter/Space all work), Medium is the default and Small/Large are 0.75x/1.25x of whatever size the current shell gives the face. ONE `--face-scale` custom property feeds every existing `#reactor` width/height rule as `calc(base * var(--face-scale, 1))` - the 232px page default, the 150px narrow page, Cockpit's 220/96/88, Studio's 48, Ambient's 320/240/190 - so no shell needed a second rule, and it is width/height rather than `transform: scale()` so the wrap reserves the bigger box instead of the face covering the log; the 48px Studio dock and the 96px pinned core were driven at all three sizes, not just counted. The choice lands in this WebView's `localStorage` under `jarvis.face.size` (the same per-device road as `jarvis.theme`), and the HUD's head script reads it before first paint, so the first frame is already the chosen size and a restart keeps it. It is a look, not a preference the PC owns: it writes no other key, changes neither the theme nor which face is worn, calls no command, adds no route and raises no card. Proven by `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud.mjs` (four new checks: every `#reactor` rule keeps its own base and multiplies the saved scale, Medium pressed by default with Large at least 1.2x and Small at most 0.8x while only the one storage key is written, the 48px Studio dock and 96px pinned core at all three sizes plus keyboard activation, and the three HUD copies byte-identical), `node tests/themes-all.mjs`, `python -B backend/test_hud_static.py` (42), `python -B tools/check_stale_twins.py` and `python -B tools/check_invoke_grants.py`, all green at the desk. **Honest limit:** the page was driven in the test harness's Chromium, not in the installed WebView2, so the first real look still needs the rebuilt app; the phone has no HUD faces panel, so there is nothing to port and `tools/check_parity.py` classifies nothing new.
- **The HUD's left column has a model icon that lists every Jarvis face (2026-10-28).** The owner asked for "a jarvis model icon to the left column of the jarvis desktop that has all of the jarvis models (face models)": `#rail-faces` now sits in the left rail's toolbar beside Presets and Live, and opens a panel listing all 25 faces - Arc, Orbit, Geodesic, Spectrum, Tokamak, Swarm, Coreplate, Workbench, Membrane, Nucleus, Comb, Iris, Shoal, Accretion, Cascade, Rime, Orbital, Fullerene, Spiral, Kirkwood, Red Panda, Pygmy Owl, Sea Otter, Monkey and Robot - rendering `faces-spec.js` directly, so it is the same ONE list the Faces window draws and no copy was made. Switching goes through the SAME `set_appearance` save that window uses (this PC's store first, then `POST /api/appearance`, via the HUD's narrow `hud_set_face` command and its `hud-faces` capability set), so `appearance.json` stays the record, the phone wears the same face and a reload keeps the choice; the current face is marked from the shell's pushed document, never from the click. It is keyboard-reachable as real buttons (focus, Enter/Space, Escape closes and returns focus) and it changes only WHICH face is worn - colours, patterns and the animal stay the Faces window's, no approval card is raised and no route was added. The icon hides itself in a plain browser, which has no shell command to send. **Not yet seen on a real desktop:** the panel's pixels were proved by a source-and-fake-DOM test (`jarvis-desktop/tests/face-models-rail.mjs`) because Playwright is not installed here, so the first real look needs the rebuilt app.
- **Edit HUD: a hidden card can be shown again, and the Hide button is a real toggle (2026-10-28).** The owner's own Edit HUD field test found that hiding a card was one-way: the card's Hide control sent `{type:"setVisible", id, visible:false}` on every press while the app painted it as a toggle, and a hidden card's shell is `display:none`, so the one toggling path (the `h` key, which needs focus on that card's own handle) could never be reached again - after a reload the only ways back were Undo / Discard changes (same editing session) or Reset to preset, and Reset replaces the arrangement of a layout that is not the base preset, so an owner's own layout could not be restored. Now the control sends `setVisible` with no fixed value through the same one reducer (the same door as the `h` key - no second mutation path), so it hides and shows, and while Edit HUD is on a hidden card is painted as a dimmed, dotted ghost: its header, the word "hidden", and its own Show control, with the card's body folded away so the card itself stays hidden. Outside edit mode the shell is hidden exactly as before, so the ordinary HUD looks unchanged. Protected cards are untouched - `approvals` and `bar_launcher` still refuse to be hidden or moved, in the same words, and the refusal is the reducer's, not the button's - and the layout store is unchanged: one document, no new action type, no new IPC command. Proven by `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud-edit.mjs` (82 passed), `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud-edit-fieldtest.mjs` (22 of 22, both scenarios, twice), `tests/hud-edit-geometry.mjs`, `tests/hud-layout.mjs` and `tests/hud-settings-layout.mjs`; the ghost's dimmed look is in the field test's screenshots but no check measures a colour or a pixel, and no real WebView2 window was driven.
- **The hardware detector's cache is no longer born expired, so a card read costs one probe instead of ten seconds every time (2026-10-28).** `jarvis_hardware.detect()` stamps its cache with the timestamp taken BEFORE the probe runs; the probe can legitimately take seconds on this PC (one registry read has a 10 s timeout), so the fresh value was already older than `_CACHE_SECONDS` when stored and every caller paid the whole probe again. Measured on the owner's live backend today: three consecutive `detect()` calls at **10.17 s / 10.06 s / 10.08 s**, and the model advisor's `GET /api/models/recommendations` - which reads that measurement - took **10.07 s** on every open. The cache is now stamped after the probe returns; a second read inside the 10 s window is the cached value. The fix is one line in `backend/jarvis_hardware.py` (and its byte-identical `jarvis-backend/` twin), with a new `backend/test_hardware_cache.py` that stands in a slow probe and proves two calls inside the TTL probe once, a call after the TTL probes again, and `fresh=True` always probes - the check **bites** on the old code (the second call probes again). The advisor route still reads the same measured cards, so its answer is unchanged; only the wait is gone.
- **Google Calendar, both ways: the private iCal link or a real Google sign-in, and only one at a time (2026-10-28):** the owner picked "Both at once" - a read-only private iCal link *and* a read/write Google sign-in - with the app choosing one. The sign-in is the Google "installed app" flow with a **loopback redirect** (`http://127.0.0.1:<random port>/callback`, bound to 127.0.0.1 only - no ngrok, no Cloudflare Tunnel, no Tailscale Funnel), **PKCE (S256)** and a random `state` that is checked on the callback: a wrong or missing state is refused and nothing is exchanged. The scope is `calendar.events` alone (the narrowest that can list, create, change and delete). The refresh token lives in Windows Credential Manager through the existing `jarvis_token_store` helper, access tokens live in memory only, and neither is ever written to a file, a log line, a route answer, a card or a model prompt. Reads raise no card; creating, changing or deleting an event raises ONE approval card showing the exact event, sends nothing before the yes, and is refused outright when the last read of the calendar is stale. `backend/jarvis_google_calendar.py` (+ its `jarvis-backend/` twin) with routes from its own `install()`, called by `plugins/ready/google-calendar` - no patch, no edit to `jarvis_hud.html`. New docs: `docs/GOOGLE-CALENDAR-SETUP.md` (the one-time Google Cloud setup, click by click, including the honest note that a "Testing" app's token expires after about 7 days and that publishing it needs Google's verification review) and JARVIS-API §161. Not built here, said plainly: the Settings row, the desktop chat tool and the phone - all three need doors in files the parallel account-connect work owns (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `jarvis_agent.py`, `jarvis_quick.py`).
- **The Galaxy gets its resolution back on a 30 Hz display, and the frame-budget test stops keeping the better of two samples (2026-10-28).** Two minor findings from the read-only audit of batch B1, fixed against `origin/main`. **Finding 4, the recovery.** `nextRenderScale` climbs only under `FAST_FRAME_MS` (19 ms), and a 30 Hz display hands out a frame every 33.3 ms even when the page does nothing at all, so once the picture had stepped down during a slow moment - a shader compiling, a window resize, another program taking the graphics card for a second - it stayed soft until the page was reloaded. Driven through the real rule, 40 frames at 33.3 ms left the scale at 0.25 (the floor) while 40 frames at 16.7 ms restored 1.0. Widening that threshold is the wrong fix, and this is the part worth keeping: 33.3 ms is also exactly what a machine that can only manage 30 frames a second produces, so a wider threshold would hand pixels to a machine that cannot afford them. The new `nextFrameScale` (pure, beside the old rule, which is unchanged) instead earns ONE probational step up - the same 0.75 factor as every other step - after three samples in a row that land within 3 ms of each other, in the band where the interval cannot tell a display from a slow machine. The answer is the very next sample: slower, and the picture goes back to the EXACT scale it was at (remembered, not multiplied back), the half-rate switch goes back with it, and the wait doubles - from 40 samples up to 2400 - before it asks again; not slower, and the pixels were free, so after another steady run it asks for the next step. That is why it cannot oscillate: a refusal is not a step down, so the picture never lands on a new operating point, and each refusal makes the next question rarer. Measured on the rule itself: a steady 33.3 ms display reaches full resolution in 18 samples (about four seconds at 30 Hz), a 33.0-33.6 ms one does the same, 45-60 ms medians never leave the floor and turn the half rate on exactly once, and a 30 Hz machine that is at its limit exactly is asked four times in 300 samples and ends where it started with its full frame rate. The half-rate switch is also latched now instead of re-derived from every sample, which is the other half of the same problem: while it is ON the intervals measure the display, not the work, so re-deriving it is what made it flap between thirty drawn frames and fifteen at precisely the machine class it exists for. Its threshold did not move, so one stall over `VERY_SLOW_FRAME_MS` still drops the picture to the floor and latches the half rate; what changed is that the latch is cleared by the first probe that succeeds instead of flapping every other sample. **Finding 8, the retest.** `tests/hud.mjs`'s G2 took one more sample after a failed first one and judged the BETTER of the two, so a real 16.7 ms regression that comes and goes could pass on one good sample. It now takes up to three samples once the first is over budget, judges the MEDIAN of what it measured, and prints every sample's p95 in the failure message and on the console, so a scheduling spike on a shared runner cannot fail a page that is fine while a regression that is really there cannot hide behind one good sample. The 16.7 ms bar is unchanged. **Files:** `galaxy-cosmos.js` and its two vendored copies are byte-identical (`tools/check_stale_twins.py` and `backend/test_hud_static.py` both pass, the latter 42 of 42), and `jarvis-desktop/tests/galaxy-cosmos.mjs` went from 42 to 47 checks with the five new scenarios. **Honest limits:** this is pure maths plus the browser suites - no 30 Hz display and no packaged WebView2 were driven here, so the feel on a real 30 Hz screen is what confirms it, and the frame-budget numbers in the audit's simulation are re-derived here as pure maths rather than re-measured on a GPU.
- **The face model and the Galaxy follow one another across the PC and the phone, both ways (2026-10-28).** The owner: *"including changing the jarvis face model and galaxy models"* — one sentence after asking that the voice be the same on both his apps, and the last piece of "the same Jarvis wherever I look at it". **The Galaxy's look is now a key in the appearance document** (`galaxy`, beside `face` and `bindings`): one look id out of `galaxy-presets.js`, the same present-replaces/absent-keeps merge, the same server-stamped `updated`, and the same `appearance` event, so it repaints both apps with no reload, no approval card and no new secret. The HUD's Look switch writes it through ONE new narrow command, `hud_set_galaxy_look`, which proves the id against the module's own list before anything is stored (in the existing `hud-faces` set, not a new one), and **the phone draws the look** with the desktop's own flat layout of it — `GalaxyLayouts.kt`, seven layouts ported from `galaxy-presets.js` and held to the JavaScript's exact numbers by a regenerated `galaxy-picture-cases.json` (`tools/gen_galaxy_picture_cases.py --check`, now a CI step) and `GalaxyPictureTest`. The phone's Appearance screen gains a **Galaxy look** picker, so a look chosen on the phone is the look the PC's Galaxy draws. The one look the phone cannot draw is the desktop's default `constellation` — the PC's own physics simulation — so it draws its zen ring for that one and the Galaxy plate **says which look is on screen**, because a silent substitution is worse than an honest caption. The Galaxy look used to be a `localStorage` key on the PC alone (`galaxy-presets.js`'s `PRESET_STORAGE_KEY`), which no other device can read: that is the one-way/stale point this closes. **Honest limits:** the Brain window's own look switch still writes that device key and so follows on its next load rather than live (a named follow-up); Playwright's browser half of the HUD tests is CI's to run, not this PC's; and nothing here was driven on a real phone — the JVM suite is predicate proof, not device proof.
- **Approval cards that have timed out leave the phone's list by themselves (2026-10-28).** The owner asked "there is approval cards that are expired, how do I clear them easily from my android phone", and the phone was telling the truth: on his OnePlus (`f0a5b32b`, app 0.2.3086) four cards sat under "4 WAITING ON YOU" with "Nothing runs until you decide.", each one saying "Timed out — ask the desktop to raise this again." and drawing no Approve and no Deny, with nothing on the screen to remove them. The cause was one line: the gate's sweep that marks an overdue row `expired` ran inside `_init()`, which is **once per process** (`if _inited: return`), so a card whose waiting thread died with an earlier process — a backend restart, a killed backend, an abandoned request — was not old enough to be swept by the next start-up if it was raised inside the last timeout-plus-60-seconds window, and after that nothing ever swept it. It stayed `pending` for the life of that process, so `/api/pending` served it, every app drew it as an unanswerable "waiting on you" card, and a later identical request was told "the same request is already waiting for you" about a card nobody was waiting on. The sweep is now one helper, `jarvis_gate._expire_overdue()`, that `pending()` runs before it builds the list, so the queue a client is handed holds only rows still inside their own window. **No new control and no new route:** clearing an expired card would approve nothing, but it would also be a second mechanism for a fact the read already knows, and the owner should not have to press anything to get rid of a card he cannot answer. **It is a lapse, not a decision** — the state written is `expired`, never `denied`, and nothing is approved; the row still shows as `expired` in `history` and both apps' Activity lists. **It cannot take a live card:** the deadline is recomputed on the PC at the moment of the read and no client sends an id, so a stale phone page has nothing to ask with. The four cards that have no deadline (pairing, an approval key, a restore) are never touched, and the 60-second grace the start-up sweep always gave is kept. **The phone's only added half is one line** in `MainActivity.onResume` that re-reads `/api/pending` when the app comes to the front, because the list on screen is a copy and nothing told it to re-read — that is what makes "open Jarvis" the whole answer instead of only a cold start. Tests: `backend/test_pending_expiry_on_read.py` (8 tests / 30 checks — the once-per-process defect, an overdue card leaving while a live one stays, the grace, all four no-deadline cards, `decide()` refusing a lapsed card, the clean read, and the "already waiting" refusal going away); `backend/test_gate_no_expiry.py` 183 checks and `backend/test_installed_stand_in.py` 15 checks still pass. **Honest limits:** nothing is deployed here — the owner's live `jarvis_gate.py` needs `scripts/apply-patches.ps1`, and the phone's resume re-read needs a new APK before the list refreshes every time the app comes to the front (the backend half alone already clears it on any fresh open or reconnect). That Kotlin line is compiled by CI's `phone-unit-tests` job and by nothing at this desk, so it is CI-only verification. A card that expires while a screen is already open still sits there until that screen next reads the queue.
- **The live deploy tool can now carry the 2026-10-28 approval-gate patches, and stops calling one of them already present when it is not (2026-10-28).** `no-expiry-cards.patch` was rewritten in place by #965 (`640a16a7f`), which added `_expire_overdue()` (the sweep `pending()` now runs on every read), called it from `_init()` and `pending()`, and moved `expires_in` onto `timeout_for()`. The tool's `NOEXPIRY_REGIONS` still held the eight regions PR #773 had cut, and every one of those probes was present in the owner's live `jarvis_gate.py`, so the dry run reported the patch ALREADY_PRESENT while the live file held **no** `_expire_overdue` at all - the same silent skip that `cloud-escalate.patch` hit, and the reason "0 refusals" alone is not evidence. A second, older gap was measured on the way: region 7's probe (`Sent as seconds LEFT...`) is a line the patch itself re-writes, so it is in the pre-image too and could never prove anything - #773's `expires_in` change had therefore never reached the live PC either. The entry is now a twelve-region chain: the #773 eight, then four more that carry the #773 state to #965's post-image (three rewriting a region #773 wrote, one inserting the new read-time sweep), with region 7's probe replaced by `left = timeout_for(...)`, which only the post-image contains. `backend/test_reconcile_live_backend.py` proves the chain, from two starting points - a gate with none of the patch, and the **measured** live shape (#773's regions 1-6 and 8 present, region 7 absent) - reproduces the patch's own nine hunks byte for byte, compiles, and changes nothing on a second pass (390 checks). Measured read-only on the owner's PC: the dry run goes from "212 already present, 0 to insert" to "211 already present, 1 to insert - `no-expiry-cards.patch -> jarvis_gate.py (12 region(s))`", 0 refusals, rehearsal OK. Its context and new bytes keep this PC's 180-second `approval_timeout_seconds` fallback rather than the patch's 900, because a deploy must not quietly change a number the owner's own file already chose. **Not deployed:** nothing was run with `--apply`; the live folder was only read.
- **DEF-002 is closed: the slow status route is gone and nothing reproducible is left (2026-10-28).** The register's desktop AppHang row from 2026-10-11 is closed, and the record says so honestly: the HUD half was fixed on 2026-10-14 (the status route stopped probing the network inline and answers from a 15-second cache), the desktop deferral fix (`windows.rs` `show_restored`, which restores, shows and focuses a window in one main-thread step) was already in the tree, and the Lead measured the owner's live PC today - `/api/status` returned HTTP 200 in a median 31.0 ms (min 29.5 / max 51.2, n=7) against `/api/pending`'s 30.9 ms, down from 4,598 ms against 9 ms at app 0.2.129, with desktop 0.2.345 installed and today's main backend running. No freeze was reproduced in any pass, so the row closes as "no reproducible defect remains; residual is an unmeasurable AppHang under window-state restore", not as "freeze fixed". The stale comment the row named is corrected too: `window_memory.rs` said the window-state plugin is 2.4.1 on Rust 1.89, and now names what is actually pinned - `Cargo.toml`'s "2.5", `Cargo.lock`'s 2.5.0, Rust 1.90 - with no version changed.
- **A new decision is now one small file per pull request, folded into `docs/DECISIONS.md` when it is safe (2026-10-28).** Every pull request that recorded an owner decision appended it at the end of one 3.2 MB file, so two such pull requests always conflicted - and a conflicted pull request gets no CI at all, while re-clearing one costs a fresh `frontend` run (about 62 minutes; PR #386 is the last crossing on record). A pull request now writes ONE dated bullet under `decisions.d/` (named `YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md`, the same prose that used to be appended) and never edits `docs/DECISIONS.md`; on a quiet `main`, `py -3 tools/roll_decisions.py` appends the bullets at the END of the record in date order (that file is chronological oldest-first, so it is not `CHANGELOG.md`'s newest-first), keeps every existing entry, re-anchors any `docs/CLAIMS.tsv` source that cited a fragment, and removes the fragment files. `--check` validates a fragment's name, its dated bullet, that the date in the name matches the date in the bullet and that the anchor heading is still in the record, on every pull request; the roll itself is deliberate and no workflow runs it. The checkers learned the same rule: a `grep:`/`absent:` that names the record reads BOTH places, so a decision is evidence the day it is written and an `absent:` notices when a sentence comes back as a fragment; a CLAIMS source may cite either place; and a promise's own words are found wherever the record keeps them. Proven by `backend/test_roll_decisions.py` (42 checks: every bad shape named one by one, appended at the end in date order, no lost entry, idempotent, dry-run writes nothing, the CLAIMS re-anchor landing on the entry's own line, and two branches' fragments changing no file in common), `backend/test_claims.py`, `backend/test_promise_guards.py` (130 passed) and the changelog, docs-map and records-encoding checks.
- **A backup can no longer claim data it cannot restore, a damaged `manner.json` refuses writes instead of wiping itself, and every way a chat is deleted now scrubs receipts (2026-10-28).** The chat-history key is PROVED against the database before a backup carries it, and a backup never mints one: `get_existing_key()` is read-only, `get_or_create_key()` only makes a key for a brand-new empty history, and `verify_key_opens_db()` is the AES-GCM check-row proof. A history whose key is missing or does not verify is LEFT OUT, never carried unreadable, and named in `excluded_dbs`; `study.db` and `health.db` get the same rule when their own key cannot be read, and every database whose snapshot failed is named the same way, and "Backed up." becomes "Backed up N databases, except …, left out to prevent an unreadable backup." A restore refuses to overwrite the live `chat-history.db` when neither the archive's key nor this PC's key opens it, and leaves the live key alone either way - the receipt names the database. A damaged `manner.json` refuses every write in plain words, keeps `manner.json.corrupt-<stamp>`, and each valid write keeps `manner.json.bak` - the #881 merge shape is unchanged. Retention (`keep_days`) and an expired "Forget a time frame" Undo now purge receipts like a hand delete, and the old 200-turn cap is gone. Changing the window sweeps BEFORE it saves, so a failure cannot be reported as "could not save the setting". Data-integrity audit F1-F5, with regression tests for each; the audit's F6 (the Undo lives in memory) is unchanged.
- **Connecting an account by talking to Jarvis: one card, Credential Manager, one real test (2026-10-28).** The owner asked whether Jarvis could connect his accounts through chat, with a pop-up for a password — and the answer he had been given ("jarvis cannot connect directly to external calendars … Would you like instructions on exporting your calendar to ICS?") was false about Jarvis, which reads CalDAV and a private iCal link today. Now "connect my calendar", "set up github", "set my github token" and "is my calendar connected?" reach a deterministic answer with no model at all, and the first raise ONE card in the desktop HUD: the private iCal link, the CalDAV username and password, or a GitHub personal access token. The values never enter the chat, the model's context, a tool result, a log line, a file, a database or the clipboard — the HUD posts them to a host-only route (`/api/connect-account/{pending,save,cancel}`, loopback and pairing token checked before the body is read, the body never logged or echoed) and `jarvis_token_store.store_secret` writes them straight into Windows Credential Manager, with **no plaintext fallback**: a store that refuses says so and saves nothing anywhere else. The card is then tested once for real — one `GET` of the iCal link (200 **and** `BEGIN:VCALENDAR`), one authenticated `PROPFIND` on the saved CalDAV address (200/207), or `GET https://api.github.com/user` (200, with the login GitHub answers with) — and "connected" is only ever the verifier's verdict: a saved-but-broken link answers "saved, but it did not work just now — the private link answered HTTP 404". The same fix closes a rule 3 breach the audit found: the CalDAV username/password and the GitHub token had **no Credential Manager entry at all** and were read only from plain-text environment variables (`jarvis_calendar.login()` and `jarvis_research._token()` now resolve through `jarvis_token_store.resolve_secret`, so the environment variable still wins when one is set and nothing already working breaks). The model gains ONE tool, `connect_account`, which takes a service and a note and **refuses any argument that looks like a value**; it ships off like every tool not in the shipped `[tools].enabled` list, and the owner's sentences work without it. PC-only on purpose: there is no phone card and no phone route, because a phone-side card would have to send the credential over the network to reach this PC's store (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §8, `JARVIS-API.md` §162, `tools/check_parity.py` `deliberate`). `backend/test_connect_account.py` (187 checks, measured - the 153 an earlier draft of this bullet claimed was wrong), `backend/test_account_secrets.py` (the required `credential-manager` CI check, now covering the three new entries on real Windows under throwaway names) and the HUD card, driven in a REAL browser (`jarvis-desktop/tests/hud.mjs` hands the page the host's own card list, clicks Save, and checks the boxes built from `card.fields`, the masked secret box, the newest card drawn, the inputs emptied before the message and the one invoke carrying the card id and the values) prove it. **An independent security review then found five real holes, all fixed on this branch:** the card could be spent TWICE - two submits at the same moment both wrote, and the re-check and the spend are now one step under the lock, with a two-thread test that proves one receipt and one write; an expired card's refusal was never tested (it is now, and removing the check turns the suite red); the route wrapper's "another machine" refusal was untested (now driven with a recording body reader: 403 and **zero** bytes read); the "not connected yet" answer had lost #960's jump to Settings, Accounts (`open_settings: "account-secrets"` is back); and "is my phone connected?" was answered by this path with words that never mention the phone (the phone's nouns are out of that list). Three smaller ones too: a right-to-left override in the model's note could make the card read backwards (every Unicode format character is stripped now, with a test carrying U+202E and U+200B), the CalDAV test failed **open** when the plain-http rule's module was missing (it now falls back to the https-only check the iCal path uses), and the HUD sorted the waiting cards oldest first while drawing only the first, so a card raised now could hide behind an older one for up to 300 seconds (newest first now, pinned by the browser case).
- **The cloud lane now says what the local one says, and a crisis turn never leaves this PC (2026-10-28).** From the 2026-10-27 AI-safety audit's H1 and M1. A cloud answer used to be relayed exactly as the provider sent it, so every honesty line a local answer carries - "nothing was actually done", the four newer corrections, and the 988/911 help text - was missing on the one lane where no tool can run and a claim of action cannot be backed at all. It now comes from the same function the local answer uses, written after the model's words and before the stream ends.
