A turn that looks like a crisis is no longer offered to a cloud lane even when one was picked by hand: it is answered on this PC, and `X-Jarvis-Route` says so (`cloud_refused: "crisis"`). The crisis check is one rule now (`jarvis_wellbeing.crisis_in_turn`, the last message when it is the owner's), used by the route header, the answering turn and the quick path - so a distress message pasted, shared, captioning a picture or said with a failed voice print gets the help line instead of only the calm panel, and the quick path can no longer answer a crisis turn before any check runs. The claim classifier also catches the short confirmations it used to miss ("Reminder set for 7pm."), no longer exempts a sentence just because it says "noted" while writing a note, and no longer counts four read-only tools as actions. One limit, said plainly: the route itself needs the owner's PC and a real cloud key to drive end to end, which this change does not do.
- **"Can you connect to my calendar?" now gets the real two steps instead of a made-up limitation (2026-10-28).** The owner asked in the HUD chat and Jarvis answered that it *"cannot connect directly to external calendars like Google or Outlook without you manually granting permissions and setting up a bridge"*, then refused again on "set up a calendar bridge for me". **False about this PC:** `jarvis_calendar.py` already reads Google Calendar through its private iCal link and any CalDAV calendar, read-only, with no OAuth and no bridge, and the model is offered a `calendar_read` tool. **What was missing was an answer:** every one of his phrasings returned `None` from `jarvis_quick.match()` (16 of 16 measured), so the 8B model answered, and its system prompt says nothing about a calendar - so it invented the permission grant and offered to explain an ICS export instead. `jarvis_quick.py` now answers - `quick: "calendar_setup"`, no model, no card, in `READ_ONLY_INTENTS` - with the real steps: Google Calendar → gear → Settings → your calendar → Integrate calendar → copy the **Secret address in iCal format**, then paste it into **Settings, Accounts, the Calendar iCal link** box (Windows Credential Manager, typed on the PC only - the answer says plainly that a link like that cannot be typed in chat). It names the one sentence that raises the card while the model is not yet offered the calendar tool (**"let the AI model read my calendar"**, `enable_reading_tool`, PC only, one card plus Windows Hello, and the shipped `[tools].enabled` list really does not include `calendar_read`), carries `open_settings: "account-secrets"` so the desktop jumps its Settings window to that card without changing anything, and - when a calendar **is** already set up - names the reader's own source ("your Google Calendar (private link)", "your calendar over CalDAV") and never the link or the address. A plain "what's on my calendar today" still goes to the model, and "open my calendar", a reminder that mentions a calendar, and a sentence with one more condition keep their own answers. Tests: `backend/test_calendar_answer.py` (121 checks - both sentences and 16 close phrasings, 11 near misses, every step tied to `jarvis_settings_registry.SECTIONS` / `jarvis_calendar.ICS_URL_TARGET` and its own docstring, the tool sentence run through the real grammar, both set-up branches driven with a fake link and address and held to never printing either, and a mutation check that the sentence reaches the model when the matcher is disabled). Docs: `docs/JARVIS-API.md` §21.13. Honest limit: this fixes the **answer and the jump to the box** - no app can type the private link for the owner (it is a secret, by rule 3), so the two taps in Settings stay his.
- **The Batch-C follow-up audit's two real bugs are fixed: a dismissed App-lock prompt no longer leaves a stale Settings place, and `DEPLOY.md` now states the measured 375 checks; the deploy tool's fifth hunk probe is added too (2026-10-28).** The follow-up audit of #949 found that `windows.rs` stored the tray's destination before `show_settings`/`show_brain` ran, and only the successful `take_pending*` ever cleared it - so an App-lock Windows Hello prompt the owner dismissed (or a refused unlock) left the place set, and a LATER plain "Settings and help..." opened straight into the Updates card (the Brain's own pending place had the same hole on main). One `forget_pending_places()` clears both statics, called from `lock.rs`'s two failure arms; the success path is untouched. `docs/DEPLOY.md` said 371 checks where the suite prints 375. And `f01-manual-lane-gate.patch`'s second hunk had no presence probe in `DELTA_TABLE` (5 hunks, 4 probes), so a live HUD missing only that region could read ALREADY_PRESENT and never be finished - the comment line the hunk adds is now a fifth probe, one per hunk. Proven by `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --all-targets` and `cargo clippy ... -D warnings` (all exit 0 on the changed Rust), `backend/test_reconcile_live_backend.py` (all 375 checks), `tools/check_stale_twins.py`, and the Batch-C audit's own measurements (195 deltas / 16 refusals on the published base, every refusal the documented pre-existing class).
- **The anti-slop batch's nine audit follow-ups are fixed, and "0 work when off" now includes no voiceprint-store read (2026-10-28).** Every finding was re-read in the source before it was changed. (1) `backend/jarvis_agent.py`'s dash exemption read the owner's voiceprint samples BEFORE it asked the switch, so OFF added no prompt token and still opened a file every turn; it now returns at once when `jarvis_antislop.is_enabled()` is false. `backend/test_antislop.py`'s new `t_off_reads_no_voiceprint_at_all` makes the reader record and raise: with the fix in it passes (244 passed, 0 failed), and with the fix temporarily removed the same check FAILS (243 passed, 1 FAILED), so the test bites. (2) Both client cards read a key the backend never sends. `words` is what `jarvis_antislop.status_words()` answers with on both routes and `said` has never existed, so `src/antislop.js`, `src/antislop-settings.js`, `net/Antislop.kt` and their fixtures now read `words` (the leftover `said` is deliberately wrong in the two tests, which proves the real key wins), and `node tests/antislop.mjs` / `node tests/antislop-settings.mjs` pass. (3) `POST /api/antislop/audit` was documented but invisible to `tools/check_parity.py`, because section 159.3 wrote its routes in bold prose the checker's line parser cannot read; the section now carries a method-first route block, the route is in `BACKEND_ONLY` with its reason, and the check prints 30 backend-only routes and "No undecided drift." (4) The vendored MIT notice quotes the upstream licence verbatim: `Copyright (c) 2026 Miqdad Badjuber (antislop)`, checked against the upstream LICENSE at commit 6732aee rather than remembered. (5) The parity entry for `/api/antislop` no longer says the phone has only the switch and the mode - it has the em-dash box too (`AntislopPlate.kt:165-187`). (6) `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 8 no longer says the Model panel asks all five advisor routes through `brain_model_recommend`: the GET is the Brain read allowlist's `model_recommendations` section (`brain/routes.rs`) and the other four are the POSTs. (7) `post_process_reply`'s docstring claimed `run_local_turn` was its only caller; production calls `slop_filter.flush()` directly, so it now says it is the tested unit and must not go back in the turn's path. (8) `_apply` wrote each part as it checked it, so a valid scope plus a bad mode saved the scope and THEN answered 400; every part is validated before any is written, and the route test proves a two-part body with a bad mode changes nothing. (9) `settings.html` no longer hard-codes "29 switches" (the registry has 32, and `antislop` is one of its `BOOL_SETTINGS` rows), and `contract/feature-controls.json` no longer records `antislop_mode` as an unproven planned choice - `backend/test_antislop.py` proves the typed phrase really changes the mode, so the row is `enforced` with that proof and is gone from `known_gaps` (169 -> 168 gaps; `tools/check_feature_controls.py` and `tools/gen_settings_cases.py --check` both pass). The advisor panel keeps its "Newer models" title: the module's own caution is that newer is not better, and no one-line rename would remove the "newer" wording the lede and the owner's own "Check for new models" button carry. **Honest limits:** the phone's Kotlin change is source-only here (there is no local Android build; CI compiles it), and the licence wording was verified against the upstream file, not assumed.
- **The 2026-10-28 audit's desktop follow-ups are fixed: the Learning card names its own switch and uses the one budget, the Galaxy halves its rate only after the pixels are spent, the faces panel Close has a focus ring, and the invoke-grants checker follows the shared budget module (2026-10-28).** The follow-up audit of Batch B1 found the card's "Waiting for your approval card" line belonged to the *sensitive* switch too (`learning-settings.js`), so the wrong row spoke while a card was pending; it now tracks which switch was asked. The card's two calls went straight to `TAURI.core.invoke`, the one class `invoke-budget.js` exists to end, and now go through `call()` (`read` for the status, `action` for the two writes). `galaxy-cosmos.js` enabled half-rate from the same sample that only *steps* the resolution - a 40 ms machine halved the rate at 0.75 - so the `renderScale` guard the comment and `tests/galaxy-cosmos.mjs` both describe is now real. The faces panel's Close button was the only control on that panel without a `:focus-visible` ring; all three HUD copies get the same `2px solid var(--ice)` rule (byte-identical). And `tools/check_invoke_grants.py` could not see commands sent through the imported `call()` at all - measured 338 commands, missing `hud_set_face` and `apply_focus_preset`, exactly the HUD's two newest writes - so it now treats a file that imports the shared module as IPC and resolves same-file command constants (`focus-presets-bridge.js`'s `READ_COMMAND`/`APPLY_COMMAND`); measured 354 commands, every one granted, with `backend/test_invoke_grants.py` pinning that the module import is what counts and a bare `call(` is not IPC. The two counts the audit re-measured are corrected in the fragments they were written into: `test_settings_registry.py` is 775, not 778, and the direct-invoke inventory is 46 files / 97 sites in `src/*.js` plus 12 in `src/*.html` - 109 across `src`, not 107. Proven by `backend/test_invoke_grants.py` (6), `backend/test_hud_static.py` (42), `backend/test_base_matches_repo.py` (131), `tools/check_stale_twins.py`, `tools/check_invoke_grants.py` (354 commands, 11 pages), `tests/galaxy-cosmos.mjs` (42), `tests/learning-settings.mjs` and `tests/focus-outline.mjs`, all green at the desk.
- **An approval card no longer shows a countdown, and a finished request's card leaves the queue (2026-10-28).** The owner ruled: "if there doesn't need to be a time limit don't have one." A routine card used to tick down fifteen minutes and refuse itself; it now shows Approve and Deny with no clock while its request is genuinely waiting. The request that asked still stops waiting on the shipped `approval_timeout_seconds`, and that moment is recorded on the row: once it plus a 60-second grace has passed, the next queue read takes the card off (a lapse, never a decision), so you are never shown an Approve that can only answer 409. Inside that grace a tap is refused in one plain sentence ("…the request that asked has finished, so nothing can be approved from it. Nothing was changed…") and the card is withdrawn. A Deny always works. The four ceremonies of the 2026-10-23 ruling (pairing a device, an approval key, a restore) have no such moment and are answerable for as long as you need. Nothing is auto-approved, no client clears anything, and the Windows Hello / signed-approval receipt is unchanged. **Honest limits:** a tap in the grace window is the race the 409 exists for, and a backend restart does not keep a card whose request died with it. Proven by `backend/test_gate_no_deadline.py`; built in `backend/no-deadline-cards.patch` and `jarvis_owner_check.py`.
- **An Anti-Slop filter, on a switch in Settings → How Jarvis talks, for both apps (2026-10-28).** The rules are the MIT-licensed `miqdadbadjuber/anti-slop` ruleset (v3.2.23), distilled on the PC to the few that fit a local model's prompt budget: no em-dashes in prose, no AI buzzwords (seamless, revolutionary, next-generation), no decorative comment banners in code, no dead buttons, WCAG AA contrast and a visible focus ring. The desktop card has a master switch, three scope pills (Copy & answers, Code comments, Web & UI design), a During/After mode radio, a switch for the four-block Delivery Gate on a finished screen, and the three Liveliness dials (Energy, Rhythm, Motion, 1–3 each). The phone has the master switch, the em-dash box and the During/After mode, in the same words, bound to the same `GET`/`POST /api/antislop` — it deliberately does not get the scopes, the gate switch or the dials: it is a settings mirror, not a second editor, and it grows no catalogue. It now also carries **Allow em-dashes (—)**, off by default, and the phone plate has the same box: Write Like Me's own em-dash habit already wins under the voiceprint rule whether it is ticked or not, and this box is the blanket “keep them everywhere” on top of it — one field (`allow_em_dashes`) on the same route, so ticking it resets nothing else. And the card says plainly what is really enforced: the filter guides generation, then checks a fixed list in code (em-dashes, buzzwords, comment banners, dead buttons, contrast, focus rings, glass and glow caps), while layout, taste and rendered sizes are guidance only. **Neither direction raises an approval card**, because the filter changes only how an answer is worded — it opens no way out of the PC, reads no folder and takes no action — and the desktop's two commands are not held on a stale link either (the phone's writes still are, at its own layer, like its other settings). A change applies on the very next turn, emits its own `antislop` event so an open Settings window or the phone's open plate repaints without a reload, and the card says in one line what OFF costs: **0 prompt tokens**, nothing added to a prompt and nothing rewritten. Honest limits: the desktop card is bespoke, so it is not one of the generated `SETTINGS_ROWS` toggles and its spoken switch is recorded as `planned` in the controls matrix for that reason; the phone's `net/Antislop.kt` is JVM-tested but **not compiled here** — there is no Android build in this container, so CI's `phone-unit-tests` job is the first compiler; and the two registry rows the typed phrases need (`antislop`, `antislop_mode`) live in the engine slice, so `tools/check_feature_controls.py` is green only with both halves together - measured in the merged tree: 142/142 features covered, 32/32 switches, 32/32 rows.
- **The Anti-Slop filter: a cardless switch that stops Jarvis writing like an AI (2026-10-28).** `miqdadbadjuber/anti-slop` v3.2.23 (MIT, 7.3k★) is distilled and vendored into one shipped module, `backend/jarvis_antislop.py`, and its rules arrive in two shapes, neither of which is a model call: a compact `<antislop_filter mode="...">` system note of at most 1,050 characters, scoped to the owner's copy / code / UI switches and carrying the three Liveliness dials (ENERGY / RHYTHM / MOTION); and deterministic Python that scrubs the words the model already wrote - em dashes out of prose (R-02), decorative ASCII/box-drawing banners and emoji comments out of fenced code (`antislop-code`), with "why" comments and all code kept. **Off is exactly 0 tokens and 0 work** - the note is the empty string and no filter object is built at all - and a PC that has never run it starts off. **On is instant and neither direction raises an approval card** (the owner's classification, and the same class as the prompt coach): it changes the words Jarvis writes, opens no network door, reads no folder, takes no action and loosens no gate. Every change still leaves one action receipt with a one-click Undo. It has two modes: `during` filters as Jarvis writes, `after` audits and lists findings first and rewrites nothing, because an audit may quote the very em dash it reports. An on-demand auditor reports HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW findings with the line each is on for R-02, R-10/R-13 (more than two glass or glow elements), R-15 (generic CTAs), R-16 (AI buzzwords), R-25 (**unrounded** WCAG AA - `#777777` on white is 4.4781:1 and fails 4.5:1 even though it rounds to 4.5; `#757575` is 4.6075:1 and passes), R-26 (dead `<a href="#">` and unwired buttons), R-32 (`outline: none` with no focus ring put back) and `antislop-code`, and closes a UI task with the rulebook's 4-block Delivery Gate. Under R-37 "Write Like Me" wins: when the owner's own samples use em dashes, R-02 yields in both the note and the scrub. `GET`/`POST /api/antislop` and `POST /api/antislop/audit` come from the module's own `install()`, called by the drop-in folder `plugins/ready/antislop` - **no patch of its own** - and it is wired into `jarvis_agent.py` (the note for the round, and the scrub on the words as they stream, keeping the streaming feel by holding back only a trailing space, a trailing em dash or a line inside a code block), `jarvis_settings_registry.py` (`antislop` switch, `antislop_mode` choice, both tier none), `jarvis_feature_guide.py` (a real state reader, so "is anti-slop on?" can never be a guess) and `jarvis_quick.py` ("turn on/off anti-slop", "enable/disable anti-slop", "is anti-slop on", "set anti-slop mode to during/after", "turn on/off anti-slop for copy/code/ui"). `backend/test_antislop.py` proves it - 239 checks, including seven mutation bite-proofs, each of which breaks a throwaway copy and requires the same check to FAIL. **Gemini's review rulings (edition 2, 2026-10-28) are applied in the same change.** The audit report now names its **Class A** rules as checked here (R-02, R-10/R-13, R-15/R-16, R-17/R-36/R-38 through `jarvis_claims`, static R-24/R-26, unrounded R-25, static R-32 and `antislop-code`) and labels its **Class B** rules - R-03's dynamic overflow and tap targets, R-18, R-20, R-23, R-27, R-28, R-30 and R-34/R-35 - **PROMPT-GUIDED ONLY, never mechanically enforced**, in a header built from the same two tuples the code uses. The owner gains an `allow_em_dashes` flag (default off) that allows em dashes outright while the rest of the copy scope keeps working, with R-37's voiceprint precedence still the automatic default, and the scrub is proved to run BEFORE `jarvis_claims`' corrections and `jarvis_wellbeing`'s crisis line, so a helpline or honesty-gate sentence containing an em dash survives it. The code-comment scrub is narrowed to two shapes that carry no information - a wordless separator line, or a comment led by one of ten decorative emoji - inside the assistant's own fenced code, and never touches tool input, file writes, diffs or existing files; shebangs, coding/type/noqa/pragma lines, Rust doc comments and diff headers are guarded and tested. `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` carries the exact MIT attribution (`miqdadbadjuber/anti-slop (v3.2.23, commit 6732aee)`, Copyright (c) 2025-2026 Miqdad Badjuber).
- **The Brain's "Newer models" list stops inventing a size and a fit (2026-10-28).** A model the public listing reports no download size for is now left out and counted ("N models were left out because the public listing gave no download size"), instead of appearing as "size 0.00 GB" with "estimated to need about 1.5 GB" and a FITS label - the 27B-row wrongness the desktop field test found (`.dsh-scratch/MAXOUT-2026-10-28/FIELDTEST-DESKTOP-2026-10-28/REPORT.md`). A kept row quotes the real size and its estimate scales with it. Row links and words now agree: a row that goes to `ollama.com` says "Ollama's library", not "Hugging Face".
- **"Update both halves" now says what really happened, and stops the backend before the Windows installer runs (2026-10-27).** The desktop half ends the app: the updater's Windows installer path launches the installer and then calls `std::process::exit(0)`, so the button could never report its own result - the Settings window vanished with no desktop line at all, and the chat path's "Both halves are updated" sentence was unreachable. That ending is now written down before the process goes: a record at `~/.openjarvis/pending-desktop-update.json` (beside the update lock; `OPENJARVIS_CONFIG_DIR` is honoured) names the version being replaced, the version being installed, which commit each half came from, and what the backend half did, and it is written in one step - a temporary file flushed to disk and moved into place - so the next start can never read half of it. The order is fixed and tested: the backend half must finish AND prove its own checks, then the desktop package is downloaded and its signature checked, then that record is written and read back, then the Python backend is stopped (waiting at most three seconds), and only then is the installer called, last. A backend that patched but skipped its checks is no longer enough to let the desktop half start; the card says so and points at the Install button for the desktop half on its own. The updater's own `on_before_exit` hook still stops the backend and only then marks the record "installer launched", and the backend this app runs is now also held in a Windows job object that ends it when this app's last handle closes, so a crash cannot leave a Python process behind holding the graphics card. The lone **Install** button gets the same treatment - it used to skip the backend stop entirely, leaving the model resident while the installer replaced files - and a start that fails before the installer says plainly that nothing was installed. "Both halves are done" can now only appear for a desktop half that landed AND a backend half that was patched and proven. Because the installer closes Jarvis, the app now says so before the button is pressed: "Jarvis closes while this installs, and starts again by itself when it is done. Save anything you are working on first." rides in the data the Updates card already reads, in the notification that announces a new version, in the desktop half's first line, and in the chat line an approved update starts with (the card itself still has to print that field - four lines in `settings.js` and `settings.html`, which another change owns). The backend patch log also names its commit beside the app's ("Backend at <sha>, app at <sha>; they differ"), because the two halves still share no version and an out-of-date program folder can still be patched under a newer app. Nothing installs itself, no new approval card is added, and nothing in this change pretends a rollback exists: a bad desktop release still has no "go back" button (the release workflow deletes the previous installers and downgrades are refused), which stays an owner decision. The ending logic, the order and the job object are unit-tested; only a real update on the owner's PC can prove the installer's exit is reported correctly and that the backend is really gone before it starts.
- **The HUD's three.js r128 is vendored for the approved "exact Gemini cosmos" port, with no page change yet (2026-10-27).** The official MIT build (`three.min.js`, 603,445 bytes, SHA-256 `9274bbcec8d96168626c732b5d31c775aa8cfb7eaa0599bec0c175908a2c1ce2`) was fetched from `https://unpkg.com/three@0.128.0/build/three.min.js` and the cdnjs r128 and GitHub `r128` tag copies returned the same size and hash that day; the file's own banner says Three.js Authors/MIT and its revision constant is `"128"`. It ships byte-identically as `backend/static/three.min.js`, `jarvis-desktop/src/three.min.js` and `jarvis-backend/three.min.js`, so the HUD can draw offline and never load a CDN; `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` gains the provenance and the MIT text, and the file is registered exactly where the existing static assets are (`backend/_where.py`'s `SHIPPED`, `scripts/apply-patches.ps1`'s `$SHIPPED`, `tools/gen_base_from_backend.py`'s `BESIDE`, the base README, and the base's derived `_where.py`). It is deliberately **not** a `STATIC_ASSETS` whitelist key and no HUD page loads it: `backend/test_hud_static.py` refuses a key nothing loads, so the port PR adds the page's `<script>` and the key together. `backend/test_vendored_three.py` pins the real-r128 bytes in every copy and proves the key/loader arrive together. **No HUD behaviour changes in this change.**
- **The tray's "Update available" row opens the Updates card itself, and Settings no longer calls any of its settings "Everyday" or "Rare" (2026-10-27).** Right-clicking the tray icon and choosing the update row used to open Settings at the top, so the version, the release notes and Install were several screens down; it now opens Settings at its `#updates` card - a new window is built at `settings.html#updates`, an already-open one hears a `settings-place` event, and either way the card is scrolled to with its first control focused. App lock is still asked before any of it, and the tray still never checks, downloads or installs anything. The page's Everyday/Rare bands are gone, with their two jump-list labels and headings - every card, id, anchor and jump link is unchanged, and the read-only "What Jarvis does" group stays as the one heading. Settings search now orders what it finds by where the words matched: a card's own title or a name the menus catalogue already answers to for it (exact, then prefix, then a typo), then its labelled rows, then its detail text; ties go to the shorter title, then the title itself, never to the section or the page's order. Typing "update" now puts Updates first instead of fifth behind cards that merely mention updating, and "update" joined the Updates card's spoken names in `jarvis_menus.py`, so "open update" names it too. Limits: this is desktop only - the phone has its own Settings search and its own update row - and the scroll/focus proof is interaction evidence from a local headless Chromium, not a claim about a packaged build or about what the owner sees until he opens it himself.
- **Asking "how do I change the personality?" now answers at once, from the Settings registry (2026-10-27).** Five names the owner uses - the personality, the voice speed, the focus preset, the theme and the HUD layout - were not feature-list titles, so those questions fell to the local model (about 2 seconds), which has no settings tool. When no feature name matches, the same question is now matched against the settings registry and answered on the fast path (about 3 ms): a choice setting names its real Settings card and a real "set ... to ..." phrase, the two desktop-only controls name their card and the "open ... settings" phrase and say plainly they cannot be changed by asking. The page-only refusal still wins first - including hyphenated and filler phrasings, and a sentence naming two page-only switches that used to fall through and turn chat history off with no card - an unknown name still reaches the model, and a refusal for a mixed sentence now suggests asking for the other setting separately.
- **The required-check count is seven, not six, because `phone-unit-tests` became required on 2026-10-25 (2026-10-27).** The branch ruleset says so itself - id `24677804` ("protect", target branch, enforcement `active`, no bypass actors) requires `rust`, `frontend`, `backend`, `backend-windows`, `credential-manager`, `powershell-5` and `phone-unit-tests` - seven contexts - while `audit` and `python-advisories` stay informational. The ruleset's own history shows when the seventh arrived: `phone-unit-tests` was added at `2026-10-25 22:43:29 -0700` (history version `54742523`), and six was the count before it, apart from a two-minute window on 2026-10-11 when a `build` job was also required (ruleset versions `52797553` then `52797672`; `ci.yml` records that job beside `phone-unit-tests`). The job itself landed earlier, at `eaa099d23` (2026-10-11 22:26:36 -0700), and was simply not required yet - so six was true for every dated document written up to 2026-10-25, and those stay dated and untouched. #899 proves the seven half: merged 2026-10-27 17:07:20Z as `aaad48540` with all seven required checks green while `audit` was red. Corrected the five places that stated the set as current: `CLAUDE.md` (the rulebook every session loads), `docs/ABOUT-JARVIS.md` (which listed `phone-unit-tests` among the ten jobs and then excluded it from the six that gate), `docs/DESIGN-PHONE-LIVE-STREAM.md` section 10 (whose build-plan heading said six), and two maintained comments in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the `audit` note at line 1888 and the `phone-unit-tests` note at line 1925).
- **A release build now fails loudly when R8 strips a class the app creates by name (2026-10-27).** The `Jarvis client` workflow's existing `build` job runs `tools/check_apk_reflective_keeps.py` on the release APK right after `assembleRelease`, so every pull request that touches the client is covered, not only `main` - no new job and no new required check. The script parses the APK's own `classes*.dex` in plain Python (no SDK, no device, under a second) and exits 1 when a constructor reflection needs is gone, naming the class and saying "reflective instantiation hole - see proguard-rules.pro". It guards the 2026-10-27 failure - all five home-screen widgets sat on the launcher spinner for ever because R8 stripped `androidx.work.OverwritingInputMerger`'s no-arg constructor, so every WorkManager job died with "Could not create Input Merger" while the unit tests and the debug build were green - and the earlier Room one (`androidx.work.impl.WorkDatabase_Impl`, the keep rule at `proguard-rules.pro:76`), plus a sweep of every concrete `*InputMerger` / `*Database_Impl` the dex carries. Proven both ways locally: a release APK with PR #908's keep passes (0.82 s, 6,824 classes), the tree without it fails on `androidx.work.OverwritingInputMerger` (0.63 s), and a build with the Room keep removed fails on `androidx.work.impl.WorkDatabase_Impl`; `dexdump` agrees with all three. Honest limit: it only knows the reflective entry points in its table and the two family sweeps, so a future hole nobody has listed will still ship.
- **A tenth built-in personality: Reflective Coach (2026-10-27).** `contract/personalities.json` gains `reflect`: calm, warm, unhurried, it reflects first and asks one gentle question at a time, suggests a step only when asked, never diagnoses, prescribes or advises on medicine, never calls itself a therapist, makes no promise of secrecy, and keeps no therapy record. Its 348-character prompt block is assembled on the PC and checked against the 750-character budget (729 characters in the worst case - spoken-live plus the longest word on every dial). Wording only: no new route, no store, no network, no model call and no approval card, and the code-owned 988/911 message and the crisis block that replaces every personality are untouched - the new persona carries no phone number and no crisis script, and `backend/test_manner.py` now proves that for all ten built-ins. `tools/check_personalities.py` pins the ten ids; the generated copy in `backend/jarvis_manner.py` and its `jarvis-backend/` twin is regenerated by its own tool; the desktop Settings grid, the HUD Presets menu copy, both apps' feature list and `README.md` say ten.
- **The phone's History gets the DeepSeek conversation-list look, with pinnable chats and one section per project (2026-10-27).** The search box is the first thing on the screen, beside a Sort control (Newest first, Oldest first, Title A-Z) and a **New chat** action that starts a conversation the same way the idle timer does - a new id and a cleared chat on the phone, no new route and no request to the PC. Below them the list is grouped: **Pinned** first, then one collapsible section per chat tag (the owner's projects) with its icon and count, then the chats in no project; a pinned chat appears once, in Pinned, and a tag's header count has the loaded pinned rows taken off it. Rows now carry the conversation's type icon, its title and its age ("25min", "7d"), and the chat Home is in says "You're here". Each row's overflow menu carries Open, Pin/Unpin, Move to and Delete; a long section shows five rows and "Show N more" over what is already loaded, and "Load older" still brings more from the PC. Pin is **metadata only**: `POST /api/history/pin` sends the chat's id and the wanted state, raises no approval card, is held on a stale link, and the row moves only after the PC says yes - a refusal says so and leaves it exactly where it was. Delete from a row asks inside that row, in the same words as Delete inside a chat. Everything already there still works: opening, Continue this chat, Fork from here, delete and confirm, Forget a time frame, History settings, the word search, the Show filters, the Tags editor and hidden lists. JVM tests cover the grouping, pin, sort, age, "Show N more" and menu rules; the Android compiler is CI (`phone-unit-tests`).
- **The phone read-aloud and Home's three doors were driven on the owner's own phone (2026-10-27).** Records only, no code change. PR #898's own CI build (`app-release.apk` from run `40513029611`, head `3eabe74c4`, `versionName 0.2.2906`, sha256 `64155E6640EC019A1D083DBC4B96BB3077317DF1499AE4134031A34AF292AA1A`) was installed on the owner's OnePlus `CPH2419` and driven - an ordinary typed answer still read aloud, and `The Galaxy →`, `Presets →`, `Model →` outlived Face size Hidden and the keyboard, with `Model →` opening Brain's `MODEL` plate and its real models - and the merged build (`jarvis-client-67089d2.apk`, v0.2.2908, sha256 `5542ff95…50f5a1`, from main `67089d2`) was then driven for the leaving-the-app stop: a HOME key while a sentence was playing paused then stopped the track 25 ms after the app's own window went invisible, 19,328 frames into a clip whose natural end needed 28,581, with no later start. Matrix rows 133, 135 and 136 now read DRIVEN with their limits stated there. The limits are kept: the key was an adb HOME event, not a finger power press (power/lock and "another app" were not driven), audio is the AudioTrack lifecycle and not an ear, and row 133's stop line was the clip's natural end. Row 134 (the withheld sentence) and the unknown-route fail-closed path stay NOT-TESTABLE; row 132 (the cloud line) stays BLOCKED even though the one-file cloud deploy has since made the block live on the PC, because no phone was driven on that path.
- **The phone shows the PC's cloud setting read-only, and it was driven on the owner's own phone (2026-10-27).** Records only, no code change: the approved one-file cloud deploy put the `cloud` block on the live PC, and the installed merged build (`jarvis-client-67089d2.apk`, v0.2.2908, sha256 `5542ff95…50f5a1`, built from main `67089d2`) draws the phone's one read-only sentence at the bottom of the Presets/Hardware plate - `Cloud escalation is off - set on your PC.` - raised no card and offered no switch. - **The phone's chat answers lose their always-visible "Was this answer right?", gain a per-message chevron menu with Pin message, and the chat finally says where old chats and new chats are (2026-10-27).** The owner's words: *"i dont want a 'was this answer right' on every response from jarvis. put a carrot that opens a menu on each chat that has this, along with a pin message button that pins the message and remembers it and can be found easily in a new menu. also where can i find old jarvis chats? and how do i open new ones?"* Each Jarvis answer - the one on screen and every answer folded into the thread above - now carries a drawn chevron at the end of its own "Jarvis" line that opens a small menu holding exactly two things: the same Right / Wrong pair (`feedback.patch`, unchanged: one mark per answer, tap again to take it back, never a change to memory, the same unavailable and not-connected sentences) and **Pin message / Unpin message**. Pins are kept on THIS PHONE only, in a private preference file, and listed newest first on a new **Pinned messages** screen reachable from the chat's More menu, each row showing the pinned words, which chat they came from and how long ago, with Unpin and Open chat (which opens History at that one conversation - History is not copied). The list hides under "Hide memory lists and chat history" exactly as History does, survives a restart, holds at most 100 pins, refuses rather than dropping an older pin, and says so. A temporary chat cannot be pinned at all, because the PC keeps nothing of it. The same More menu now leads with **New chat** (the idle timer's own reset, no new mechanism) and a labelled **Earlier chats**, and an empty chat says once, above the box, where both are. Honest limits: there is no local Android build here, so CI's `phone-unit-tests` job is the first compiler; the menu, the chevron and the new screen have not been driven on a phone; pins are phone-local and are not shown on the desktop HUD (which has no feedback control either), and deleting a chat on the PC leaves its pins behind until they are unpinned.
- **A new PC is led to the signed installer, told to teach Jarvis its voice first, and the two first-run cards now name each other (2026-10-27).** `docs/INSTALL.md` leads with the published `desktop-latest` installer - `jarvis-desktop_0.2.336_x64-setup.exe`, signed, with the update public key in `tauri.conf.json` - and moves the from-source build (Visual Studio build tools, Rust, Node.js) to Appendix A; step 2.1 no longer says "there is no download yet", and the page now leads with the everyday `qwen3.5:9b` (plus the optional Fast model `qwen3.5:4b`) and marks the older `jarvis-primary` copy - a tuned `qwen3:8b` - as the legacy fallback that typed chat still names by default and Live still names as plain `qwen3:8b`, kept because `backend/jarvis_chatbot.py`'s `MAIN_MODEL_DEFAULT` and `scripts\setup-jarvis.ps1` still require it. The five-screen walkthrough now says "once you have taught it your voice - hold the microphone button and speak", with the one honest sentence voice needed: Jarvis acts only on your voice, so teach it yours first, and until you have, a spoken command is refused. Settings' "Let your phone reach this PC" row now reads supervision before it answers - it used to say the phone could reach the PC while "Let Jarvis Desktop start and stop Jarvis" was on with no program set - and the two cards both called "first run" point at each other ("First-time setup" tells you to do "Set up Jarvis" first). Two served routes that had no entry are now documented with their shapes and status codes: the Today board (`GET`/`POST /api/today/cockpit`, one `change_own_config` card for its switch) and the hand-off's "Stop early"/"Keep offering it" setting (`GET`/`POST /api/chatbot/handoff_mode`, one `handoff_keep_offering` card on the PC). The permission descriptions that still said five focus presets say nine. Also on the record, not in the app: README and ARCHITECTURE carry the owner's 2026-10-27 standing cloud grant instead of "No standing grant", the phone's read-only Galaxy picture replaces "never a canvas map", the feature count is 141 (41/2/15/71/12), the required CI checks are seven, the route counts are 294/259, and the tray's update row is described as the version label it actually is. Wording and docs only: no setting, route or approval changed, and nothing here was field-tested (no real install, no Windows Hello, no phone).
- **Accessibility: the HUD's first safety warning is spoken, its page has a language, a doctype, visible focus rings and the app's text size, its selected states survive Windows High Contrast, and every dot on the phone's Galaxy picture is reachable by touch exploration (2026-10-27).** The 2026-10-27 HUD + phone accessibility audit found that the safety rail wrote its App-lock / rush-latch / tainted-answer / stale-link words into a live region that was still `hidden` and revealed it afterwards, so the FIRST announcement — the one that matters — was silent; `hud-safety.js` now reveals the strip and the chip before writing, and a browser-free DOM-order check in `tests/hud-safety.mjs` fails on the old order (it drives each of the four chips on a fresh DOM, so a loop that revealed only one chip would be caught too). `theme.css` was the one stylesheet with no `@media (forced-colors: active)` answer, and the HUD, which links only it and `fonts.css`, lost its chosen preset, lane, Cloud button and stage tab to a single system colour under Windows High Contrast: the token file now emits the system `Highlight` ring for `:focus-visible` and for `aria-pressed` / `aria-selected` controls (from `tools/tokens/build.mjs`, so it cannot drift from the tokens), and `tests/focus-outline.mjs` now requires every window-carrying stylesheet — `theme.css` included — to answer forced colours. The HUD page itself carried three more of the audit's findings and one hole in the copy step. It declared no doctype and no `lang`, so WebView2 parsed it in QUIRKS mode and a screen reader had no language for the page (it now opens `<!doctype html><html lang="en">`, with `charset` still inside the first 1024 bytes so WebView2 cannot guess windows-1252 and break Tauri's inline-script hashes); it had no bare `.sr-only` rule although `#rail-live-status` asks for that class, so a live region meant to be silent on screen sat in the layout (a real clip rule is there now); three `:focus-visible` rules — a spoken answer's speaker, the rail's tool buttons, the Layouts menu's rows — set `outline:none` and signalled focus by colour alone, which is gone under Windows High Contrast (each draws the page's own 2px ice ring, and `tests/focus-outline.mjs` now reads the page and refuses an `outline:none` on any of its `:focus-visible` rules, so that scope gap cannot return); and the window never called `followZoom`, so Settings' Text size and Ctrl+= / Ctrl+- / Ctrl+0 reached every other window and left the largest one at 14px (it now follows the same shared function through a guarded dynamic import: a page served without `jarvis-link.js` beside it, or opened in a plain browser with no webview to zoom, is left exactly as it was — `tests/hud-text-size.mjs` proves both halves without a browser). The browser-served HUD's own copies of `hud-safety.js` and `theme.css` in `backend/static/` are re-taken as well, so the page served over the network is the code the desktop window runs. On the phone, every dot on the read-only Galaxy picture was a fixed 48dp TalkBack box on names about 18dp apart, so the boxes covered each other two or three deep and only the topmost one under a finger was ever reached, and the node never said it was a button: each dot is now `Role.Button` with a box sized to its own dot's room (`GalaxyPicture.semanticSides`), so every name keeps an area where it is the topmost node — proven by a JVM test on the real 48-name layout. The Settings focus-preset chip also keeps its open list and its refusal across a rotation (`rememberSaveable`). No new route, permission or write; the visible changes are a focus ring where there was none, an `sr-only` class that now really hides, and the page no longer being parsed in quirks mode. **Not tested here:** real screen-reader output, Windows High Contrast, TalkBack on a device, and the page's new standards mode (the Lead's CI browser suites are the first to render it) — those stay field-test rows, never a pass.
- **A reminder added from the HUD is really written, and a refused Jarvis Live says so in a readable popup (2026-10-27).** Two owner-reported field bugs. (1) "i added a reminder on the current jarvis hud and jarvis said he added it, yet when i go to reminders i see nothing coming up": the fast path before the model did not know the natural "add a reminder ..." / "i need a reminder ..." / "reminder to ..." phrasings, so the sentence went to the model - and the model's own `[tools].enabled` list does not name `set_reminder`, so it had no tool and could only say it had. Those phrasings, and the same ones after "please"/"can you"/"i want to", are answered here now and really write the row that `GET /api/schedule` and the Reminders tab show; and a model answer that claims a reminder, timer or to-do item with the verb first or the job first ("Added a reminder ...", "Reminder set for 6pm.") now gets the same "(Nothing was actually done - no action ran in this answer.)" line as "I've set a reminder...", including when a read-only `feature_guide` answer ran in the same turn. (2) "if i click on the left column jarvis live icon, and since no mic is found it gives me a message but its in the left column and hard to read. it should be a pop up": the page never defined `.sr-only`, so the refusal drew as ordinary text in the narrow rail. A refused start now shows a readable popup in the stage - its own plate, wrapped words, "This PC couldn't find a microphone. Plug in or turn on a microphone in Windows, then try again." for the no-microphone case, live.rs's own sentence for every other refusal, a real Close button, Escape, a click outside and a 20-second fallback that pauses while it is hovered or focused - and the rail's line stays as the hidden screen-reader mirror. The browser rendering, focus and the 20-second timing are NOT field-tested here (Playwright is not installed on this PC); the model's own reminder tool is deliberately still not enabled by default, so a wording the fast grammar misses is corrected rather than guessed.
- **The HUD's History column icon opens a DeepSeek-style conversation panel: Pinned, one section per project, then Untagged, with a search box, a sort, and Open/Pin/Move to project/Delete on every row (2026-10-27).** The owner asked for "a history tab with a column icon that opens up a menu that looks like all the coversations in the deepseek harness on desktop for the jarvis HUD ... both should have pinnable chats, project organization by chat, and more". The panel is ONE new module (`jarvis-desktop/src/hud-history.js`) plus the row-action table it shares with the Brain (`jarvis-desktop/src/history-actions.js`), loaded by the page with `<script type="module" src="hud-history.js">` - still ONE external file each, so this page's inline-script count and every CSP hash stay exactly as they were - and a `window.hudHistoryHost` object at the end of the page's own last script: the module adds its own `#rail-history` button to the left rail beside Presets and Live, its panel (`#history-sidebar`), its stylesheet and its row menu, and the page's own markup is not touched. Pinned chats sit first and are not repeated inside their tag; then one collapsible section per chat tag - the owner's tags ARE the projects, as `docs/CHAT-TAGS-DESIGN.md` defines them, and no second "project" concept was added - then the chats with no tag under the phone's own word, "Untagged". A section shows five rows and a `Show N more sessions` row; the search box filters by a chat's NAME only (what was SAID in a chat is still the Brain's search, section 71); the sort is newest-first or A to Z; the current chat is marked. **Pinning is the PC's record, not the window's:** a `pinned` column and its moment `pinned_at` in the same encrypted `chat-history.db` every other History fact lives in, set by `POST /api/history/pin {"id", "pinned"}` (new; metadata only, no approval card; `200 {"ok", "id", "pinned", "pinned_at"}`, `400` for a body that is not exactly `{"id", "pinned"}` or a `pinned` that is not a boolean or an id that is not a string / is empty / is over 128 characters, `404` with nothing written for a chat that is gone, `503` when the history will not open - JARVIS-API section 18.3) and read back as `pinned`/`pinned_at` on every list, open and search row, so a pin survives a restart and the phone reads the same flag. The Pinned group is drawn **`pinned_at DESC, updated_at DESC`**, and pinning twice keeps the FIRST moment, so a chat that gets a message never jumps above one pinned after it. **While App lock or "Hide memory lists and chat history" is on, the list withholds every row - the pinned ones included - and the pin route answers `423` for every id**, before it looks one up, so a locked PC cannot be asked whether a chat exists (no 200-versus-404 oracle); the server applies that withholding itself through `jarvis_private_view`, and the desktop keeps its own redaction on top. **The row words are ONE copy:** `history-actions.js` holds the action labels (the Brain's own History row now reads "Delete" from it), the three sentences of the are-you-sure, and the args/body a write sends; `history-view.js` re-exports the sentence it used to own, so the Brain says the same words from the same place instead of a second copy held equal by a test. On the PC side ONE Rust command `hud_history_act` (pin, file under a tag, delete ONE chat) shares `tag_chat_body`/`delete_body` with the Brain's own commands, is refused while the private lists are hidden, held on a stale link (rule 4), raises no card, has no delete-all - and the three History reads joined `hud_proxy.rs`'s fixed allow-list with their query parsed in one place (`brain/history.rs::history_read_path`). **The panel's keyboard and screen-reader behaviour is part of it:** a row is a `<div role="listitem">` with TWO SIBLING buttons (the chat, then its `...`), never a button inside a button; ages are real `<time datetime title>` and a clock ahead of the PC is clamped; group headers are `<button aria-expanded>` and toggling one keeps the keyboard on it; Escape returns the keyboard to the `...` and a second Escape to the left column's History icon; "Show N more" focuses the first chat it revealed; and the page's global Space push-to-talk bails for `#history-sidebar, input, textarea, button, [role="menu"]` and for an open sidebar. Tests: `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud-history.mjs` (37 checks, pure Node - grouping, pinned order, ages, the cap and the one-off expansion, search, sort, the three write shapes, the shared module being the only copy, the row markup and the focus rules, and the page/Rust/shiplist wiring) and `backend/test_chat_pins.py` (71 checks against the real encrypted store - persistence across a restart, `pinned_at` keeping its first moment, the exact 400/404 refusals with no ghost row, the 423/hidden-list rule, and an old history file migrated in place). **Not done here, said plainly:** nothing is deployed to the owner's live backend or app (that needs a rebuild, `apply-patches.ps1` and the HUD whitelist deploy), the phone's half is the next lane, and a real WebView2 painting of the panel was not driven on this PC - the pure suite and the Rust/Node wiring are what is proven, and Playwright is not installed here.
- **The Galaxy opens on the Planetary Orrery, and all eight of its looks are drawn in 3D (2026-10-27).** The owner: *"none of the galaxy on the current jarvis hud look like the one that gemini made"* — he chose the exact 3D port and Planetary Orrery as the default look. The HUD's Galaxy now renders the Gemini page's look through one three.js scene: its eight coordinate systems at its own radii and wave amplitudes, its palettes and fog (density 0.0016, ACES exposure 1.25), its camera positions and look-ats, its guide bodies (three ecliptic rings, three hexagonal cortical plates with six apical trunks, twelve forked twigs and six basal roots, four nebula wells inside a 3,200-point cloud, a 72-step double helix with 37 base-pair rungs and a laser disk that rides the time axis, a radar floor with three zone rings, four crosshairs, a rotating sweep and a containment dome, four tree baselines, one zen halo), its 1,800-star sky and its five animation branches. It draws the **real** graph — never the page's sample facts — at the HUD's own 120/240-node and 18/36-label budgets, with every name still a real `<button>` and its arrow/Home/End walking projected from the 3D camera. The new module is `galaxy-cosmos.js` (`jarvis-desktop/src/`, served from `backend/static/` and carried by the published base like the other page modules); the HUD page itself changes only at the mount point, in its three byte-identical copies. The Galaxy opens on the Planetary Orrery, chosen once and remembered under the existing look key, and the other seven remain in the same Look control. Reduced motion, a browser without WebGL, and a page served without three.js all fall back to the **flat layout of the same look**, so no machine loses a look; the 3D engine stops drawing the moment the Galaxy leaves the screen. The three fields `/api/graph` does not send are derived rather than invented (nebula wells by group, helix order by any date in `meta`, radar rings by kind of thing), and the Radar dome's rings are not a claim about safety. three.js itself is copied into this repository by its own pull request (with its licence in the notices), and the page loads it from this same origin with the matching whitelist key. **Every real name is kept inside a comfortable frame**: the camera is Gemini's own when the picture fits it, and otherwise backs straight away from the same angle until every name fits - at the 120 and 240 budgets at 1440x900, on the HUD's real stage shapes, and around the orrery's orbit (Gemini's own page crops its nebula, tree and ring at that size). Labels stay the page's own 10-14 px pass rather than the page's ~30 px over a banner, the fog thins with the camera so a 240-name picture is not fogged to black, the far plane grows with the frame so nothing is clipped away, and the two looks that used to spin the whole scene now orbit the camera instead, so their labels and buttons stay on their names.
- **The HUD's Galaxy controls move under ONE "Galaxy options" surface, with a left-column icon, a Presets word and a Hide the galaxy switch (2026-10-27).** The owner's ask was to "simplify all the galaxy options ... to have it under Galaxy options", with "a new icon on the left column", "an option next to the word telemetry", "presets as a word in the right order next to the galaxy, approvals, reminders and other menu options", and "an option to hide the galaxy under the Galaxy Options menu". The three lenses, the Look switch, "Search the brain…", Reset view, Pause spin and the computed counts line moved - same DOM nodes, ids and listeners - into one `#galaxy-options` dialog opened by a new left-column icon (the rail's in the shells whose left column is the rail, the Studio dock's in the Studio), by the "Galaxy options" word beside Telemetry in the stage's own words row, and by the same word beside the Telemetry toggle in the shells whose stage has no words row. The words row now reads Galaxy, Approvals, Reminders, Memory, Telemetry, Galaxy options, Presets, and Presets opens the ONE existing Presets menu through a third door on the same `jarvis:hud-presets-open` event. Hide the galaxy is a device-local view preference (`localStorage["jarvis.galaxy.hidden"]`), reverses from the panel and from the map's own place holder, stops the one drawing loop while hidden, and adds no route, command, approval card, backend or phone surface. **The Galaxy's visual look and rendering engine are deliberately untouched** (a separate workstream ports the new look); the new browser-free `hud-galaxy-options.mjs` passes here and the Playwright half is CI's to run, not run on this PC.
- **The documents page's own count and size are re-measured, and its measurement date is brought up to today (2026-10-27).** `docs/README.md` still said it held `405` documents "adding up to about **10.5 MB**" on "**2026-10-25**", with the byte comment reading `10,481,754 bytes` - the measured tree at `7aec8ab8d` holds the same **405 documents** and **10,684,325 bytes**, so the size had drifted **202,571 bytes (1.9%)** low (inside `tools/check_docs_map.py`'s tenth, which is why CI never saw it) and the date was two days old. This is the periodic refresh the owner's 2026-10-13 rule asks for, in its own PR with nothing else in it: the prose figure, the "around" reminder and the byte comment are re-measured with the page's own two commands, and the `measured on` line is set by hand in the same commit because `tools/gen_docs_map.py` rewrites only the figures and never the date. **The count does not move** - the page already said `405` and the tree holds `405`, because the two pull requests that landed since the last refresh (#901 and #902) only edited documents that already existed and added none. The figures are the generator's own fixed-point rewrite (`py -3 -B tools/gen_docs_map.py`), and `gen_docs_map.py --check` and `check_docs_map.py` both exit 0.
- **Nine focus presets, four of them new capabilities, and Fast moves to `qwen3.5:4b` (2026-10-27).** The Presets menu now offers nine local presets: the original five plus **Reflective Coach** (talking a difficult thought through), **Pictures & Screens** (a screenshot or picture), **Documents & Notes** (long reading and study) and **Browser & Forms** (long pages and multi-step form work). All four capability rows run `qwen3.5:9b` on the RTX 2060 12 GB - Reflective Coach, Documents & Notes and Browser & Forms at 196,608 tokens (the measured General setup: 44.9 tokens a second, 9.17 GB of video memory, no spill) and Pictures & Screens at 32,768 - while Fast runs the small `qwen3.5:4b` on the RTX 2080 SUPER at 16,384 tokens, measured at 77.1 tokens a second and 4.15 GB at a 65,536-token window. The split is deliberate: only the 12 GB card holds the 196,608-token window, and the 8 GB display card keeps its memory for Windows and the desktop while Fast runs there; Coding's 30B model uses both cards only while it is applied. An apply that needs a different model raises ONE approval card (if that model is already the one loaded, the choice is saved and nothing is approved), and nothing switches by itself - no per-feature automatic model switching is added. Reflective Coach is a **model choice only**: not a therapist, with no store, no route and no network, and the matching personality wording is not part of this change.
- **Every Jarvis home-screen widget ends in words instead of a spinner, and the release-build defect that stopped it drawing at all is fixed (2026-10-27).** On the owner's Pixel and OnePlus all five widgets sat on a bare spinner for ever while the app itself was connected and drawing live PC data. The spinner was not the app's: it was the launcher's `initialLayout`, Glance's own `glance_default_loading_layout`, because no Glance worker could ever start in the release APK. R8 had stripped `androidx.work.OverwritingInputMerger`'s no-arg constructor; WorkManager instantiates that class **by name**, so every job failed with `Could not create Input Merger` and `provideGlance` never ran (proved on the connected phone: the logcat lines, and the shipped APK's own dex, which has the class and no `<init>` at all - `work-runtime-2.7.1`'s consumer rule `-keep class * extends androidx.work.InputMerger` keeps the class but not its constructors). `app/proguard-rules.pro` now keeps those constructors, the same class of fix as the `WorkDatabase` keep rule already beside it, one reflective call further along. So that this can never again look like "buffering", three more things changed: all five widgets' `initialLayout`/`previewLayout` now point at a new `res/layout/widget_initial.xml` that says "No update yet — open Jarvis to connect" instead of showing a ProgressBar; every widget draw is decided by one pure `WidgetStates` state machine with no loading state at all; and the only two waits a widget makes - the runtime starting up and `GET /api/widgets/show` - are bounded at 4 s and 8 s, so a slow Keystore or an unreachable PC draws "Jarvis could not start" or "Could not reach your PC" rather than an open-ended wait. App lock is unchanged and its "hide the card's words" rule is now a pure function (`ApprovalWidgetRules.cardTitle`/`cardDetail`) instead of logic inside the composable. `WidgetStatesTest` adds 13 JVM tests: every approvals/board/link combination lands in exactly one terminal state, a genuinely blocked wait still returns inside its budget, the timeout/error sentences, App lock hiding the card even when it has words, a real `/api/pending` row still decoding and reaching the card, and no widget XML naming Glance's spinner. **Not proven here:** the widgets on a phone - no APK built from this branch was installed, so the field-test step is the CI-built APK, add the five widgets, and expect terminal states.
- **a reminder can open a page or a file, or raise one card to run a task (2026-10-27).** A reminder may now carry ONE action: show a note, open a web page or a file on this PC, or put the morning briefing together. A page or a file is only a notice with an Open button - nothing opens until it is pressed, and pressing it is the approval (a 15-minute card would expire before "when I get to it"). The button sends the reminder's id only; the PC reads the target back from its own answer and checks it again at the tap (https only, a folder Jarvis may look in, and an extension allow-list that refuses `.lnk`, `.exe`, scripts and `.html`). A task is acting, so it raises exactly ONE `schedule_action` card at fire time and runs nothing if that card is denied, expires or is refused; its detail carries ids and the task name only, so no address, path or note reaches the audit log or the phone. Confirming the design's second target was wrong: a backup is locked with a fresh recovery code that nothing would be left to show, so `backup` is refused when the reminder is saved, with that reason. Creating one is the desktop HUD's; the phone shows a note and the card, and creates nothing (ARCHITECTURE §8).
- **Two phone fixes: a withheld read-aloud now says so, a read stops when the phone leaves your hands, and Home's three doors outlive the face (2026-10-27).** Three corrections to this morning's typed "Read aloud" and one to Home's doors, all phone-side, no new route and no new control. A missing or unreadable `X-Jarvis-Route` header means the privacy gate and the injected-fact counts are unknown, and unknown is never spoken: the wiring asked a reader that could never answer "unknown", so the fail-closed branch was dead code and such an answer could be read aloud - `routeOrNull` is that reader now. A read stops the moment the app leaves the screen (the phone locking, HOME, another app) and the live lock is asked again before every sentence, because the "locked" fact was a snapshot taken when the answer arrived. A withheld read is no longer silence: the one fixed sentence "It's on your screen." is shown where the Read aloud control would have been, and nothing beyond it - a locked phone or a stale link shows nothing. And "The Galaxy →", "Presets →" and "Model →" used to live inside the face's caption strip, so they vanished with Face size Hidden (the owner's own setting, which meant the Model door he asked for was not on his phone at all) and while the keyboard was up; the row is one composable drawn under the lane pill and above the conversation, the caption gate is gone, and a source pin fails if it returns.
- **The phone reads a typed answer aloud, in Jarvis's voice, beside Copy (2026-10-27).** The desktop HUD already read typed answers aloud (the owner's decision of 2026-10-16), so the phone's silence was a parity gap: a finished typed answer now has one Read aloud control beside Copy and Share, which asks the PC's existing `/api/voice/say` for the answer in Jarvis's chosen voice (one request per sentence, eight at most) and plays it through the phone's one speaker - no new route and nothing sent beyond the answer text the PC already produced. The control only appears when the answer on screen is the one the phone holds and the same never-speak rules as the voice path allow it, all failing closed: an answer that used saved or sensitive facts, a private gate, a screen or camera read, a tool that ran, a stream that could not say, a stale link or a locked phone keeps the words on screen with one fixed sentence; turning the link down and the App lock holding never start a clip. The decision and the sentence cap are proved off-device, the wiring by source pins, and the on-phone look remains a field-test row, not a pass.
- **The phone's model name opens the model list, and the PC's cloud setting is shown read-only (2026-10-27).** Two small phone-side halves of the owner's Presets asks, with no new route and no write: the lane pill under the face ("Local · <model>" / "Cloud · <model>") is now a button that opens the Brain's existing Model plate, and because this PC's status names no lane or model (so the pill is not drawn at all there), Home also gains a one-tap "Model →" door beside "Presets →" that always exists - both call the same action the plain-word model errors already use, navigation only, so switching or installing a model keeps its approval card. And the new additive cloud block on the presets answer is read as one line ("The cloud may answer hard questions through <provider> - set on your PC", or that escalation is off) with no switch, no provider write and nothing shown by an older PC. The "Answered by a cloud model, not on your PC." sentence and the pill's Cloud/Local words are pinned by tests now, and a source pin fails if any phone file ever gains a cloud-escalation write. The per-question "Try the cloud model" offer is unchanged, and the on-phone look is a field-test row, not a pass.
- **A card stays readable even when one of its fields arrives null (2026-10-27).** The phone now settles every declared field of a waiting card's `risk`, `notice`, `raised`, `options` and `replaces` before decoding it, because a single explicit null inside any of them would otherwise throw the whole row out of the queue exactly as `replaces_kind` did. Unreadable values settle toward caution, never convenience: an unreadable `replaces` keeps the two-press ask, an unknown reversibility/reach is read as the worse one, an unreadable weight makes the card heavy (slower Approve), Deny stays allowed, Approve never switches on by itself, and a raise that was sent still refuses every quick gesture. A null-bomb test pins a row where every one of those fields is null and asserts it is read with those values; no server change, no new route, no write.
- **Every approval card waiting on the PC is readable on the phone again (2026-10-27).** The PC sends `replaces_kind: null` on any card that replaces nothing (`jarvis_gate.pending()` sets it from `replaces_words_for(...)`, which returns nothing for an ordinary card), and the phone declared that field non-nullable while its JSON reader does not coerce nulls - so the explicit null made the whole row fail to decode and the phone showed only "N waiting approval could not be read on this phone" with an empty queue, for every ordinary card (picture mode's card was the one seen on 2026-10-27). The three replaces fields are now normalised before decoding, so ordinary cards read exactly as the HUD shows them; no server wire change, no new route, no write. A pin test decodes a real gate-shaped row with those nulls; the phone's own look at a real card remains a field-test row, not a pass.
- **A Live button in the HUD's left column, and read-aloud speakers on answers that are opt-in (the owner's asks, 2026-10-27).** The big "Jarvis" window's rail carries ONE **Live** button in its own `.rail-toolbar` - the rail block no shell hides - and it is the floating bar's control rather than a second idea of Live: the same `live_start` (`by: "button"` plus a fresh chat id) and `live_stop`, the same "Jarvis Live" name and on/off sentences, the same `aria-pressed`, and the same state read (`live_status` on opening, then the shell's `live-status` event through `live-rules.js`'s `onHere`), so a session running on the phone does not paint this window as on. It needed one new grant and no new command or route: a `hud-live` set holding `live_status`/`live_start`/`live_stop` and deliberately not `live_act`/`live_mute`/`live_hold`, with every refusal still Rust's (stale link, App lock, no microphone) and no approval card. Separately, Settings -> "The big HUD window" gained **"Read-aloud buttons on answers"**, OFF by default and stored on this computer like the switch beside it (no backend, no card): with it on, each finished answer gets a small speaker that reads THAT answer through the page's existing `voice.speak`, the same object and OS voice the window already uses - and speaks nothing by itself, while with it off nothing changes at all. An answer that has to stay on screen gets **no speaker**, judged by `private-speech.js`'s `mayReadAloud` on that answer's own route (the same call the automatic read-aloud makes, failing closed if the rule cannot be loaded), with the shell's `speechSynthesis` wrapper still refusing it as a second gate; the owner's own messages and the canned hello never get one. `tests/hud-live-read-aloud.mjs` (13 checks) drives all of it, including the private answer that must stay silent.
- **The HUD's menu is called Presets, and now lists the PC's own presets and personalities (the owner's asks, 2026-10-27: "you didnt put presets, you put hud layouts", "i also want the presets to include all the ideal local ai presets that are made (like coder, or general, etc) along with the different personalities of jarvis that i can select").** Above the saved HUD layouts, the one menu now draws the five local-AI focus presets from `GET /api/presets` and the nine personalities plus "My personalities" from `GET /api/manner`, with only the PC's own `chosen_preset`/`personality` marking what is current. Applying a model preset starts its lane and raises the PC's ONE existing `switch_model` approval card, and nothing is marked chosen until a refetch of the contract names it; switching personality is immediate, asks for no card (wording only, as the backend itself says) and shows Jarvis's own switch line. The settings gear beside the button now wears the page's own rail-button class and a line-drawn 24-unit gear instead of the bar's filled one, so the two controls are the same box and the same icon style ("the spacing of the two new buttons is uneven", "i want the settings to be in a similar style of icon to the others"). The menu's saved-layout actions are unchanged, it is still ONE menu, and the HUD page still holds no token: the shell adds it through `get_focus_presets`/`apply_focus_preset` and the new narrow `hud_personalities`/`hud_switch_personality` commands, while a plain browser uses the routes with `X-Jarvis-Client: hud`.
- **A lane you pick by hand now runs the same privacy gates as an automatic turn (2026-10-27, F-01).** `POST /api/chat` with `"auto": false` and a `"lane"` skipped every routing gate: any client with the pairing token could send `{"lane": "<cloud lane>", "auto": false}` and have the turn answered on a cloud model with private memory text, a tainted conversation, a picture or the owner's screen attached, because the manual path built its own decision and ran no gate at all. Both paths now go through the ONE `jarvis_router.choose()` call, the pick stands in for the owner's own yes for that one question, and a pick the router would not have escalated is answered on the local model with the router's own `gate` and a plain `reason` in `X-Jarvis-Route` ("you chose <lane>, but this turn stays local: ..."). A pick of the local model is unchanged, no wire field was added, no app changed, and a PC with no `jarvis_router.py` fails closed. `backend/f01-manual-lane-gate.patch`, proved by `backend/test_hud_manual_lane_gate.py`.
- **The Studio HUD's lane dropdown is a "Current Models" button now, opening a read-only panel of what Jarvis is using and has loaded (the owner's ask, 2026-10-26: "under the button on the jarvis hud that says auto, theres several options including bulk, critic, escalate, can you just get rid of this button and replace it with a button that says \"Current Models\" and clicking it will show what models are being used and or loaded on jarvis?").** In the Studio (split-stage) header the `<select id="lane-compact">` is gone; a `#current-models` button in the same slot, with the same plate/border/mono tokens and the same Studio-only visibility, opens a panel wearing the Presets menu's own popover classes. It shows the PC's own `GET /api/models` `current` and `previous` (plus `/api/status`'s `default_model` only when it differs), its `offload.models` with `size_mb`/`vram_mb`/`on_gpu_percent` - or the owner's exact sentence "The PC did not say any model is in memory right now." when the PC says nothing - and `GET /api/presets`'s `chosen_preset`/`chosen_model`. Its fourth section routes the NEXT message: the buttons are built from the page's own `#lanes .lane` buttons and click them, so every lane the old dropdown listed (auto, the model, and the cloud lanes) is still there, still one list, and the mark is the page's own. Nothing is switched, installed or approved: `/api/models` joins `hud_proxy.rs`'s fixed read list while `/api/models/switch`, `/install`, `/rollback` and `?recommend=1` stay refused, there is no new command, no card and no new route, and a read that fails says the PC did not answer and changes nothing. A Refresh button re-reads all three.
- **A rushed approval card can no longer be swipe-approved because its reason was cut short (2026-10-27).** `jarvis_gate.check()` stored the rush latch's chip as `json.dumps(raised)[:1000]`, and that chip is a couple of kilobytes once `json.dumps` escapes non-English text: the real bounds are `quote[:80]`, `context[:200]` and `source[:120]` (`jarvis_content_risk.py:319`, `:217-219`), and every non-ASCII character becomes six characters of `\uXXXX`. A chip cut at 1,000 is no longer JSON, so `pending()`'s `json.loads` threw and its `except` served `raised: null` - and BOTH apps read that as "not raised" (the phone's `swipeable = risk.swipeOk && raised == null`, the desktop's `if (approval.raised) return false`), so the one card the latch had raised arrived looking ordinary with its swipe back on. `jarvis-backend/jarvis_gate.py:1803` now stores the chip whole (the column is `TEXT` and `chip_from()` already bounds every value, so there is nothing to trim); the separate 4,000-character `detail` cut is deliberately untouched, because that is a different fault (a cut-off request the phone has no guard against) and changing it here would alter a second behaviour. The published base's three companions are kept in step with it, because the repository's own suites reverse the patch stack out of that file: `backend/gate-touches.patch` (its added side now carries the same line, so it still reverses out and re-applies - `test_gate_touches`, `test_approval_guards`), the deploy block `tools/hud-deltas/gate-digest-insert-new.txt` (must be the base's own text, byte for byte - `test_reconcile_live_backend`), and `backend/patch-history/gate-touches/` with the previous patch text. Measured through the real `check()` with a real `jarvis_content_risk` latch: a 2,068-character chip was stored as 1,000 characters, `JSONDecodeError: Invalid \uXXXX escape`, `raised: None`, `risk.swipe_ok: True`, card swipeable; after the change the same card stores 2,106 characters, parses, and is not swipeable. Proved by `backend/test_gate_rush_chip.py` (19 checks, 12 of which fail on the old line), including a control that an unraised card of the same action keeps its swipe, and a check that `detail` is still cut at 4,000. **Honest limits:** `jarvis-backend/jarvis_gate.py` has no twin in `backend/`, so the two-file rule does not apply to it; `jarvis-backend/` is the published base and a running PC is not updated by committing this, so this fixes the repository's gate copy and does NOT claim the owner's live backend is already fixed - that needs a `tools/reconcile_live_backend.py` delta of this region (the existing digest deltas are already applied there and are skipped) or a base re-take, measured separately. Nothing on the phone was touched, and the phone's rule is applied here as `ApiModels.kt` declares it, not compiled.
- **The Galaxy has five more looks and an easy switch for them, and the HUD's left column now follows the words above it (2026-10-27, the owner's own ask).** `galaxy-presets.js` builds the five rich looks the blueprint listed first - **Planetary orbit** (one sun, homothetic ellipses around it, spaced by arc length so no two names crowd), **Neural arbor** (a crown of rows widening upward with faint dendrites down), **Nebula** (one golden-angle cloud: the only look with no rings, rows or lines), **DNA helix** (double helices side by side, two strands and a rung per row) and **Radar dome** (names on arcs over a ground row) - so nothing anywhere says "not built yet" any more, and keys 1-5 that used to announce a plan now draw a picture. The HUD's own Galaxy (the `#brain` view with the Memory / Neural / Unified lenses) gets ONE plain **Look** list beside those lenses, filled from that module's own list of looks: choosing one lays the same names out in that look, remembers it on this device under `jarvis.galaxy.preset`, brings it back on the next load, and an unknown or corrupt stored value is the constellation rather than a broken canvas. The page imports the module dynamically (the same way it already loads the speech rule), so the vendored page stays byte-identical in all three copies. And the Studio dock's icons were in a different order from the inspector's words - the owner: *"reminder is out of place for example"* - so the dock now reads Galaxy, Approvals, Reminders, Memory, Telemetry like the words do, with every id and data attribute unchanged.
- **Cloud in the Presets menu, and one switch that lets Jarvis escalate without asking first (2026-10-27).** The Cloud row now sits beside General/Coding/Agentic+Vision/Fast and opens the six connected API services; picking one is ONE `change_own_config` card, nothing is marked chosen until the PC's own `cloud-choice.json` says so, and a cloud apply **starts no lane and switches no model**. `GET /api/presets` gains two additive keys and nothing moved (`version` stays 1): `cloud_preset` - a sixth row in a preset's own shape, deliberately not in `presets[]`, so both apps' five-row list is unchanged - and `cloud {provider, providers[], escalate{on, why}}`, where a provider row carries whether a key is **saved** (presence only, never a key or a fragment) and the one honest connect action, the command line to run on the PC. `POST /api/work/apply` gains one optional `provider` field, read only for the cloud preset; `POST /api/work/cloud-escalate {"on": true|false}` is new - ON is ONE card and nothing is written before it, OFF is instant. The **"no standing grant" rule is changed by the owner** and recorded in `docs/DECISIONS.md`: the standing yes is read from THIS PC's `escalate-cloud.json` by `jarvis_router.choose()` and from nowhere else, so no client flag can forge it, while the HUD page's `cloud_yes` counts only while that file still says on. Asking in the message ("escalate this", "send this to the cloud") is a yes for that turn only, evaluated after every privacy gate. Every gate still runs first and still only pushes a turn back to local. `X-Jarvis-Route` gains `auto` (`true` when nobody clicked); on such a turn a cloud reply with no usable answer is **not** shown as its working-out - the PC answers it instead under one honest line (`jarvis_chatbot_api.CloudAnswer`, `jarvis_agent.answer_here`), and the 2026-10-14 rule is untouched for a turn the owner clicked for.
