The three lane names become **one**: `jarvis-critic` and `jarvis-bulk` are gone from `CLOUD_LANES`, `jarvis_auto_router` and `jarvis_projects`, `degrade_chain` is `["jarvis-escalate"]`, and `_lane_names()` no longer reads `litellm-proxy.yaml` - a dead config for a proxy that was never installed - but asks the chosen provider instead, so no provider chosen is gate `unavailable` rather than three names that do not exist. `cloud-escalate.patch` is new in the stack (three hunks, no install block) and `backend/_stack.py`'s ratchet records it honestly (58 → 60). The phone reads all of it and writes none of it; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §8 says why. Tests: `backend/test_presets_cloud.py` (46), `backend/test_cloud_escalate_setting.py` (72), plus the 15 suites the lane removal touched.
- **CI now parses every Python file, stops counting two non-tests as frontend suites, has one shard-count definition, and cannot report a suite that skipped every check as a pass (2026-10-27).** The 2026-10-27 test audit found 14 backend suites (364 checks) that no CI job ran and that no job even parsed; the required `backend` job now compiles every `.py` file under `backend/`, `jarvis-backend/` and `tools/` in memory (a few seconds, no `__pycache__`) and fails on the first syntax error, before the ~35-minute sweep - not `compileall` or `py_compile`, because both write bytecode even under `-B` (measured: 1,090 `.pyc` files in a copy). `poll-hygiene-measure.mjs` and `make-before-overlay.mjs` say in their own headers that they are not tests (one prints JSON, the other copies `src/` to `src-old/`), so the frontend loop no longer runs them, and the selected count is 225 real suites out of 229 top-level `.mjs` files. The frontend shard count has ONE definition - the `matrix.shard` list - with `SHARDS` and the job name reading `strategy.job-total`, so a `SHARDS`/matrix mismatch can no longer drop a shard's worth of suites silently. `backend/run_suites.py` reports a suite that exits 0 having skipped every check as `NO-CHECKS`, a new state that is neither a pass nor a skip, and fails the sweep unless the suite is on the new `ZERO_CHECK_ALLOWED` list (three suites needing an optional package: playwright, cryptography, Pillow). The same 2026-10-27 probe moved four of the 14 audit suites into the required backend job - `test_bitemporal.py`, `test_extraction_wiring.py`, `test_gate_push.py`, `test_import_history.py`, 61 checks that now run - and kept the other ten skipped by name with the owner files each needs. Proven by `backend/test_suite_report.py` (74 checks), `backend/test_suite_state.py` and the repo's checkers. **The rule's first CI run paid for itself immediately (run 40468616102):** the required `backend` job went red on exactly the two suites that were proving nothing on every push. `test_decks.py` printed one skip and ran NO check because `py-fsrs` was never installed by either backend job - 349 checks sat unrun on every push while the job reported green; `fsrs==6.3.2` (the version `requirements.lock` pins for Python 3.12) is now installed by BOTH the Ubuntu and the Windows backend jobs, so its 351 checks (2 skipped only where `time.tzset` does not exist) really run. `test_tool_calling_wiring.py` lifts `do_POST`'s text out of `jarvis_hud.py` in all six checks, and that file is the owner's patched entry point that no checkout holds, so no CI runner can run it at all: it is now in `run_suites.py`'s `NEEDS_OWNER` list (skipped by name, saying which file it waits for) and out of the Windows job's suite list, where six skips and exit 0 would have read as a pass - the owner's PC remains the only machine it runs on, for real. **Honest limit:** the Windows job still trusts exit codes alone, so a later suite that skips every check there would still read as a pass; widening the rule to that job is named as remaining work, not claimed here. The amended branch's own green CI run is what has not been seen yet - the next run is what shows the fix.
- **A setting can be changed by asking for it on or off, and the answer is a read-back (2026-10-27).** "Make the prompt coach off", "set web search to off", "I want the prompt coach off" and "the prompt coach should be off" matched nothing in the chat grammar, so they reached the model, which has no settings tool - while "turn on/off <name>" already worked. 27 of the 31 on/off switches now take the plainest phrasings too, through the SAME setter their Settings switch, the HUD route and the "turn on/off" phrase already call, so the card, the tier, the PC-only refusal and the wording are unchanged. A sentence naming a switch with no state ("change the prompt coach") gets ONE question back and changes nothing; a switch outside the list keeps the guide's own explanation word for word; a password, key, token, deep config or an irreversible ask is refused in one plain sentence. When the writer applies at once, the value is RE-READ and the reply names the old and the new value; when a card is up it says a card is up and never claims the change; a value that cannot be read is never written at all. `backend/test_ask_to_change.py` proves it, including a real end-to-end run against a throwaway config folder.
- **A blocked click no longer freezes the browser for eight seconds and then reports the wrong reason (2026-10-27).** When Jarvis clicked a link that leaves the allowed sites, the navigation fence answered it with an empty `204` - a navigation Playwright never sees finish - so `locator.click()` sat out its whole 8-second timeout and raised `TimeoutError`; `run()` then reported "step 1 failed: TimeoutError: Locator.click: Timeout 8000ms exceeded" and listed the step it actually attempted among the steps that "did not run". The click now passes `no_wait_after=True`, so a blocked click returns immediately and `run()` gives the fence's own sentence ("the page tried to go to ..., outside the allowed sites - Jarvis blocked it before it loaded"); the fence itself is unchanged and the tab still never leaves the allowed site. Measured headless on Windows 11 with Playwright 1.64.1 / Chromium 156.0.8078.4: `backend/test_browser_control_live.py` 66 passed, 0 failed (was 63 passed, 3 failed). Two test-side corrections came with it: the live suite's record-shape assertion still expected the nine keys of 2026-09-23 and now includes the `box` added on 2026-10-15, and `backend/test_browser_control.py` - which CI does run, unlike the live suite, which self-skips without Playwright - now pins that exact key set so the next `box`-style addition cannot re-break the live suite invisibly.
- **The Jarvis bar reads like the HUD: flat plates, line glyphs, one Incognito chip, and fewer buttons (the owner's decisions, 2026-10-27).** The second row is no longer 999px pills: the mode chips, the lane badge and the preset chip are 3px plates with mono 10px uppercase 0.14em labels, every chip glyph is a fine stroke on the bar's 16-unit grid tuned to render the HUD's 1.05px line weight (1.2 at 14px, 1.05 at 16px), and a pressed mode is the HUD's ice text plus an ice-edged border instead of the old glow or Live's red. "Auto" is renamed "Hey Jarvis" (the wake-word listener, id unchanged), "Private" becomes "Incognito", and the milder "Temp" chip leaves the DOM so Private chat and Temp collapse into that ONE Incognito chip with the strict zero-write behaviour `private_chat` already enforces; the server capability, the `private` flag and the Tauri commands are untouched. The "?" primer door and its panel, the pin button and the Esc key badge also leave the bar (Esc still hides it, the close's tooltip still names it, and the settings gear and close are what remain), and the help words the panel carried still live in Settings' Brain tab, "Tutorials and the FAQ". The window still pins itself while a card is up; it is just no longer something the owner toggles from the bar.
- **A read-aloud now stops when the next question is asked, and turning the phone sideways no longer cuts it off (2026-10-27).** Four wiring fixes behind the phone's read-aloud: a new typed question - from Home's box or the Live screen's - stops a read that was still playing and clears its offer, instead of letting the old answer finish while the control said "Stop" for the answer before this one; a question that starts by voice (the talk button, "hey Jarvis" heard, or Train my voice) stops a read before the microphone opens, because the recorder has no echo canceller and the clip would be recorded into the question; and a rotation no longer stops a read that is still on screen, since a configuration change is not leaving the app - that is the Lead's 2026-10-27 ruling, pinned at the guard's source line because a JVM test cannot simulate a configuration change. The question-start stops are guarded on `readingAloud`, so a typed question sent over a *spoken* answer never cuts the voice's own speech. Still open and named: turning Jarvis Live on does not stop a read, and whether Live's own echo-cancelled listener would remove it is not measured. Four new tests in `ReadAloudWiringTest`; the whole phone suite is 2,706 tests, 0 failures, 0 skipped.
- **Jarvis can talk about an update in chat, and start one only through ONE approval card (2026-10-26).** "Is there an update?" and "what changed in the update?" are answered without the model from the same public release manifest the app's updater reads, using the version the desktop shell reports in the chat body; a release's changes come from the changelog, now published into `latest.json`'s notes by the release workflow, and an empty or one-release-only list says so rather than inventing changes. "Update Jarvis" raises ONE card (action `desktop_update`, tier ask, PC-only with Windows Hello) that names both halves, the version, the restart and the pace; only the owner's Approve makes the shell run the same `update_both` the Settings button runs - backend first, then the signed desktop release, then a restart - so no page, timer, page script or chat message can install anything, and the HUD gained no update command. The phone is one-sided on purpose: its reply points at Platform checks, its own update card, and the PC-only card refuses a phone approve with nothing changed. Proven by `backend/test_update_chat.py`, `jarvis-desktop/tests/update-chat.mjs`, `jarvis-desktop/tests/release-notes.mjs` and the existing Rust suite; a real signed install is the owner's field test, not a pass.
- **The owner-secret hand-off can raise its card on the live backend (2026-10-26).** The backend's HTTP request threads never initialised Windows COM, so `POST /api/secret-handoff/create` refused every card with "the host could not verify a password-masked control" even when a password box really had focus; every thread that calls UI Automation now initialises COM once before its first call, and a refused create names a safe reason code (`E_*`) instead of a bare exception type.
- **The desktop's watchdog now waits up to about three and a half minutes before calling the backend hung, and its "stop after 3 restarts" cap works again (2026-10-26).** It used to allow 45 seconds (3 failed checks, 15 seconds apart) and then restart the backend, and on 2026-10-26 that cut off a real answer: "tell me about the Galaxy" ran 37-38 seconds and was restarted mid-turn. Now 10 checks have to fail in a row, and one probe gets 5 seconds instead of 1.5 because a cold first probe costs about 3.0 seconds while `hello()` builds the voice sessions. The wait is not simply 10 x 15 s: a backend that is listening but silent makes each failed check wait out the whole probe, so the honest worst case is 10 x (15 s + 5 s) = 200 seconds - which is what the app's own settings screen and `docs/INSTALL.md` now say, instead of the "two and a half minutes" they said before. The restart window is 15 minutes, not 10, for the same reason: three slow hangs are noticed about 200 seconds apart, so a 10-minute window could never hold all three - the fourth attempt would arrive after the first had aged out, the cap would never trip, and the app would restart a wedged backend every three and a half minutes for ever without ever telling the owner. The 15-second check interval and the Windows service are unchanged. Two Rust tests pin the arithmetic and the window-versus-cycle relationship, so raising the patience again cannot silently break the cap.
- **The owner-secret hand-off is wired on the desktop: the agent hits a password field, the HOST measures it, the owner types the secret into a two-box card, and the host types it in.** `backend/jarvis_secret_handoff_wiring.py` hooks `jarvis_ui_control.plan()`, serves host-only `GET /api/secret-handoff/pending` and `POST create/fill/cancel` (token + loopback, body never logged), and calls the engine only; `get_window_state`/`read_selection` now refuse a target the 15-minute taint protects. The HUD card shows the host-verified target with an UNENCRYPTED HTTP badge and the agent note labelled unverified, and clears the password box after submit. Browser path is deliberately NOT WIRED (no HUD-namable session); documented in ARCHITECTURE section 8 and JARVIS-API section 157.
- **The owner-secret hand-off's rule engine (2026-10-26).** `backend/jarvis_secret_handoff.py` (+ byte-identical `jarvis-backend/` twin) is the state machine behind the one flow that can safely let Jarvis log in somewhere: the owner types the secret into a card the host draws, the engine refuses unless the caller's live snapshot still matches the card (same target, kind, focused control and password mask), marks a 15-minute read taint BEFORE the first character is typed, types through an injected function, and returns a receipt that names the target and never the value. Keys are canonicalised page addresses (scheme and host lower-cased, a default `:80`/`:443` dropped, `.`/`..` path segments resolved, a trailing slash dropped, query string, fragment and any `user:pass@` userinfo stripped) or pid+hwnd+window class, so a secret value can never be part of one; a caller that does not assert the card is being answered on this PC is refused, because `from_this_pc` has no safe default. No disk, database, log, clipboard, model context or tool result; the module imports no storage or logging library and its suite proves that from its own AST. The card shows the target separately from the agent's cleaned, explicitly-untrusted note, and voids with zero characters typed on any target/kind/control/password-mask change, an expired card, an empty or whitespace-only value, or an unproven caller. A typing failure is reported in the module's own fixed words, so the injected error cannot echo the value. `backend/test_secret_handoff.py`: 93 checks, 0 skipped, 0 failed, no GUI and no network. Nothing imports the module yet - the wiring into `jarvis_ui_control.py` / `jarvis_browser_control.py`, the host-only route and the HUD card are the next slice; `docs/DESIGN-SECRET-HANDOFF.md` records both halves and the honest limits that Python cannot wipe memory, that the snapshot is caller-supplied until the wiring measures it, and that the taint is in-memory only.
- **The hand-off defect row now records its live 13/13 field test, and four places that describe the suite runner stop calling the visible-browser skip a missing-file skip (2026-10-26, records follow-up).** DEF-015's measured count is corrected from the stale 260 to 267 on the two suites it names, `test_ui_control.py` (214) + `test_secret_handoff_wiring.py` (53) at main `ad0b1cab7`, and the closed row records the live PASS on the backend deployed at that ref: `live-handoff-fieldtest.log` 13/13 checks, `probe-after-uia-fix.log` RESULT PASS, `private-smoke-after-uia-fix.log` RESULT PASS, and `deploy-after-859-second.log` "no file changed (already current)". PR #864's bullet now states its two fill-path bugs separately, because the id-mismatch bug voids with "the focused input control changed", not "no longer password-masked". `backend/run_suites.py`, `tools/update_backend.py`, `README.md` and `backend/README.md` now say the four visible-browser suites are skipped even when present unless `JARVIS_ALLOW_VISIBLE_BROWSER_TESTS=1` is set, and `update_backend.py` no longer claims a test pins the full phrase (only `VISIBLE browser` is pinned, on one suite's runner line). Checks: `check_defect_register.py`, `check_claims.py`, `roll_changelog.py --check`, `check_docs_map.py`, `test_base_matches_repo.py` and `check_stale_twins.py` all exit 0, and the two counted suites measured at 214 + 53 = 267 passed, 0 failed.
- **The product-check capture seam is named, without a live browser (2026-10-26).** `backend/jarvis_product_capture.py` classifies one supplied page string into exactly one of `product_page`, `owner_login_required`, `challenge` or `not_a_product_page` (reusing the read half's parser and warning codes), and `capture_page(url, driver=...)` calls an INJECTED driver - a callable or an object with `get_html(url)` - so the later browser slice can supply the HTML. It refuses with the documented `CaptureUnavailable`, `OwnerLoginRequired` (the owner signs in once; Jarvis never sees the password) and `ChallengeDetected` (a CAPTCHA is never solved), and `real_driver()` says plainly that the real browser-session driver is not built yet rather than opening a window for it. No route, tool, card, app surface, browser import or network call exists; standard library plus the read half only; tests are fixture HTML only (`backend/test_product_capture.py`, 33 checks) and the real driver, persistent profile, one-time login and a real Amazon read are NOT-TESTABLE in this container. It is not wired yet.
- **The private-chat catalogue row's follow-ups now say what is true, and the records that still said 139 say 140 (2026-10-26).** An independent review of #862 found what CI cannot see, fixed on one branch. (1) `contract/feature-controls.json`: `label_legend` still said Command-Only 69 while the canonical counts are 70 of 140 - now 70; and the 169-row `known_gaps` had no row for `private-chat` while every other non-enforced feature has one - the generic not-a-setting row, in `temporary-chat`'s shape and words, makes it 170. (2) The `private-chat` row in all three byte-identical `features.json` copies: `what` no longer says it "reads, searches and answers" - it answers from the model alone, searches nothing (not the web, your history or your files) and uses no saved memory; `asks` says "none from it", not "none while it is on", because background work can still raise a card while the chip is on; `limit` says an older PC "leaves it off" (the chip stays visible and unpressed) and that the phone's private mode is Incognito but not the same guarantee - Incognito can still run tools. The shipped catalogue and its `jarvis-backend/` twin and the desktop `feature-directory.js` with its test fixture were regenerated by their own writers (the directory because the contract's hash moved); the phone's `FeatureDirectory.kt` is unchanged because no contract row moved. (3) Nine stale "139" comments now say 140: both apps' directory and Settings comments (`FeatureDirectoryPlate.kt`, `net/FeatureSearch.kt`, `ui/SettingsSearch.kt`, `SettingsScreen.kt` twice, `feature-directory-ui.js` twice, `settings.html`) and `backend/test_agent.py`'s docstring, the half-updated pair whose `backend/jarvis_agent.py` line #862 had already moved to 140. (4) One dated `docs/DECISIONS.md` entry appended at the end records that the desktop Private chip (#856) and the phone's Incognito are the apps' half of the 2026-10-25 private-chat decision, and says its "Neither app sends the flag and neither has a toggle or marker; Slice 2 is the apps' half" sentence is superseded - the older entry is not edited. Checks: `check_feature_list` 9 passed/0 failed (140 entries), `check_feature_controls` OK (140/140, 170 known gaps), `check_parity` no undecided drift, `check_docs_map` 405 documents/10,548,108 bytes, `roll_changelog --check` clean, all three feature generators `--check` clean, `run_suites` 1960 passed/1 skipped/0 failed, `test_base_matches_repo` 119/0, `test_base_regeneration` 39/0, `check_stale_twins`/`check_no_source_pins`/`check_vacuous_checks` clean. Deliberately left: `docs/ABOUT-JARVIS.md`'s 139 (a measurement pinned to commit 8104e6d01) and the dated field-test records. Honest limits: no Android build or browser-driving test ran here; the backend's own refusal sentence still says "A private chat can read, search, and answer" (the architect's agreed copy in `backend/jarvis_private_chat.py` and JARVIS-API section 156), which this records-only branch did not change.
- **A private chat can no longer pick up a stored "remind me next time" reminder, and the reminders that come due because you are at the PC now say which of them were written while the gate was tainted (2026-10-26, Ruling 2.3 residuals).** Two small backend fixes, no new route, setting, card or permission. First, `jarvis_schedule.due_active_reminders()` is a read-only polling getter, so it still latches nothing - but every reminder it returns now carries the row's own stored mark (`"source_taint": ""` for an ordinary row, `"read_outside"` for a row written while the gate taint was active), so a caller that ACTS on a reminder can latch at that moment instead of the getter guessing. Second, a private chat no longer works out a stored "remind me next time" reminder at all: the end-of-turn write was already skipped for private turns, but the note could still be dressed into the private prompt by a direct caller passing `private=True` with a `"typed"` provenance; that lookup is now gated on the private flag (fail-closed, empty answer, so every downstream use stays valid and ordinary turns are unchanged). Tests: `backend/test_schedule_taint.py` (41 passed: the key on every returned row, empty for an untainted row, `read_outside` for a row written while tainted, and the getter latched nothing) and `backend/test_private_chat.py` (130 passed: private + typed carries no stored next-time note and marks nothing brought up, while the ordinary turn still carries it and marks it once). Bite: the same tests run on the parent code fail exactly on the new checks (`every due reminder carries the source_taint key`, and `private turn: a stored next-time reminder is NOT carried into the prompt`). The two changed modules and their published-base copies are byte-identical; `backend/test_base_matches_repo.py` 119/0, `backend/test_base_regeneration.py` 39/0, `tools/check_stale_twins.py` clean. Honest limit: this is server-side plumbing only - the desktop and phone need no update, and a caller that acts on a due reminder must still latch itself (the getter deliberately cannot do it for them).
- **Jarvis Presets now shows the presets, and the Hardware card cannot sit at "Asking your PC…" for ever (2026-10-26).** More -> Jarvis Presets still opens the Brain's Hardware plate (the owner's 2026-10-25 decision), but the Focus presets section is now drawn and read even when the hardware status has not loaded, and it is the first thing in that plate so the anchor lands on the preset rows. A hardware read is bounded at 25 s and ends in a plain-words "did not answer" line with Try again; the Brain header turns "Refreshing…" back into an enabled Refresh after 30 s. The More row says where it goes ("In Brain, under Hardware"). Tests: `PresetsLandingTest` pins the More action -> Brain + hardware, the anchor, both plate branches, and the failure/empty read lines; no emulator in this container, so the on-phone check is the PR's device step.
- **The phone's Home screen now has its own one-tap "Presets →" entry, beside "The Galaxy →" (2026-10-26, the owner's ask: "also i want a presets button easily accessibler on the phone app").** It sits in the same row of text doors under the face and calls the SAME action the More menu's "Jarvis Presets" row calls, so it opens the one Brain Hardware plate where the Focus presets are drawn - navigation only: no new route, no new read, no permission or approval change, and choosing a preset still raises the contract's ONE approval card. The More row stays as the second door, TalkBack reads the entry as "Jarvis presets", and `PresetsLandingTest` pins that both entries call the same one action and that the Home entry names no route. Honest limit: this container has no Android compiler and no emulator, so CI is the only compiler and the on-phone look is the pull request's device step.
- **The records now describe the phone's read-only Galaxy picture (2026-10-26).** The owner approved a read-only picture on the phone, and only the "no picture/canvas on the phone" part of the standing rule is replaced: no add, edit, delete, merge or drag from it, and no new route. `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/JARVIS-API.md`, `docs/GALAXY-PANEL-DESIGN.md`, `docs/ABOUT-JARVIS.md` and the parity note now say what the picture is - the desktop's deterministic zen-ring layout, at most 48 names, a TalkBack line per dot, and a tap that opens the existing facts view - while the desktop keeps the interactive map. The feature list's `galaxy` row now says both apps with the phone's picture read-only, and the field-test matrix records the picture as NOT-TESTABLE: nothing has run it on a phone or an emulator. No app behaviour, route or write changes; the parity note, the generated feature catalogue and the controls contract follow the list because their checkers require it.
- **The phone draws a read-only picture of the Galaxy (2026-10-26).** Above the existing "at a glance" band and the People and things list, the Android app now draws the Galaxy itself: the same data the desktop draws, with each name a dot and a shared saved fact a line, worked out from the `/api/memory/entities` read the plate already makes. The positions are deterministic - no force simulation and no animation - because the picture is the desktop's own zen-ring layout, ported to the phone and pinned to the desktop JavaScript by a generated fixture. It draws at most 48 names, the names with the most saved facts first; every other name stays in the list below, and a tap on a dot opens that name's existing facts and connections view. It adds no route, no write and no approval card, and it is hidden under "Hide memory lists and chat history" and while the list shows a past date. This reverses only the old "no canvas on the phone" part of the rule, not the read-only part, and the picture's look on a real phone is a field-test row, not a pass.
- **Personality presets can explain what each one does (2026-10-26).** `backend/jarvis_quick.py` (+ byte-identical `jarvis-backend/` twin) adds the cardless, read-only `personality_describe` quick intent, so the owner can ask in chat what a personality is, not only switch it. One preset - "what does the Classic Butler personality do", "what is the Classic Butler personality", "describe the butler personality", "tell me about TARS", "what is TARS like", "tell me about the Classic Butler" - answers with that preset's own `description` from `jarvis_manner.py`'s contract (never new copy) and `Say "switch to the <title> personality" to use it.`, the sentence measured to switch all nine titles; titles, aliases and the owner's saved "My Personalities" all resolve through `jarvis_manner.normalize_personality_id()`. All of them - "describe your personalities", "describe my personalities", "explain your personalities", "tell me about your personalities", "what does each personality do", "what are your personalities like" - answers with one `- <title> - <description>` line for each of the nine in contract order, then the four dials and "make me a personality that ...". Both are 0 ms, no card, never the model, and listed in `READ_ONLY_INTENTS`; like every quick answer they still record the sentence's usual command digest, and nothing the owner can see is written. The old "what personality are you using?" answer keeps its shape, with its own switch example corrected to `switch to the Classic Butler personality`; a sentence that names no preset ("what does it do", "tell me about yourself") stays with the model. `docs/JARVIS-API.md` §27.7 records the question forms; `backend/test_manner.py` proves it (staged run: 577 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed).
- **A Windows Hello prompt now closes when its card stops waiting, can never be given more than 180 s, and only an action with written words can prompt (2026-10-26).** The measured defect (`.dsh-scratch/WINDOWS-HELLO-2026-10-26/FINDINGS.md`) was that `POST /api/approve` read the card's remaining seconds BEFORE waiting for the one-prompt-at-a-time lock and never read them again, so a queued card kept its original 900 s even with 2 s left; and nothing watched the card while the dialog was up, so a card that was denied, withdrawn or expired under an open prompt left the dialog on screen for the rest of that number. Now the card is read again after the lock - a card that has run out by then is a 409 with no prompt - and the prompt gets `min(the seconds left now, 180 s)`. While the prompt is up a watcher polls the card four times a second and tells the child to close when it stops waiting, so the dialog goes within about a quarter of a second instead of being waited out; a queue that cannot be read is not treated as a card that went away. The watcher can only close its OWN prompt: the prompt is tagged when it is raised and the cancel is matched by that tag, because a queue read can outlive the watcher's two-second stop and the next card's prompt could otherwise be the one on screen. The wait for the lock is bounded too (`_PROMPT_WAIT_MAX` = 210 s, covering the worst legitimate hold of about 194 s: 180 of prompt, ten for the child's answer, two for the cancel and two for the watcher's stop) and refuses with 409 rather than waiting for ever. The long-lived `--hello` child no longer reads an empty stdin line as `{}` (which gave a cardless prompt the 900 s default) and clamps any timeout it is given to `_PROMPT_MAX` itself. Separately, an action with no written title in `jarvis_card_words` is refused with 403 `owner_check: "unlabelled"` before any prompt is shown - checked again on the post-lock live row, and stamped for that live action - because the title could only quote the action's name back at the owner; `unclassified_tool`, `send_email` and every other action with a phrase still prompt. A clean backend shutdown now cancels a live prompt (an `atexit` hook writes "cancel" to the child's stdin, and the child's own watcher calls the Windows cancel), instead of leaving the dialog to its own deadline. **Deploying it means copying BOTH `jarvis_owner_check.py` and `jarvis_card_words.py` into the live backend in one go, then restarting the backend:** `has_written_title` lives in the words module, and a one-module copy makes every risky approval on this PC refuse with 403 `unlabelled` before any prompt - silently, and it looks like the new guard working. `scripts/apply-patches.ps1` copies every shipped module in one run, so use it rather than a hand copy. Proven with fake verifiers and a fake child process only - no real dialog was raised: `backend/test_owner_check.py` 214 passed, 1 skipped; `backend/test_card_words.py` 168 passed. Honest limits: the prompt is still a detached child process and is still not parented to the app's window (the next fix, needing a real-prompt half-day test), so a hard kill of the backend - the desktop's job object, Task Manager - runs no Python, cannot run the cancel or the watcher, and the dialog then ends only at its deadline; a queue read that keeps failing also leaves the dialog to its deadline (nothing is approved either way); the desktop's own prompt (`op.get()` with no timeout) is unchanged; and the off-Windows test that used to call the real verifier on Windows now skips before that call, so the suite can no longer raise a real prompt on the owner's PC.
- **The default backend test run cannot open a visible browser window, and CI still runs the code-only checks (2026-10-26).** A plain `py -3 -B backend/run_suites.py` ran `backend/test_chatbot_sites.py`, and Playwright opened a real Chromium window for every chatbot site profile on the owner's PC - the "browser keeps opening and closing rapidly" - because Playwright being installed is the normal state there, so the suite's own "is Playwright installed?" check could never stop it. The four suites that drive `jarvis_chatbot_web`'s visible launch (`test_chatbot_sites.py`, `test_chatbot_gemini.py`, `test_handoff.py`, `test_support_widget.py`) now print one SKIPPED sentence and exit 0 unless `JARVIS_ALLOW_VISIBLE_BROWSER_TESTS=1` is set, and `run_suites.py` skips all four by name with the reason, so the default full run starts none of them; `backend/test_browser_test_guard.py` pins both layers and runs the refusal path with no browser available. **Review round (the independent review of #861):** the 441 code-only checks stay in CI - both backend jobs in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` set `JARVIS_ALLOW_VISIBLE_BROWSER_TESTS: "1"`, and a runner has no Playwright browser, so with the opt-in each suite runs its code-only half (sites 293, handoff 109, gemini 29, support_widget 10 - measured by the review with browsers disabled) and its browser half prints `SKIP the real-browser half: playwright is not installed` and exits 0, exactly as before the guard. The owner-facing skip note no longer says these suites "need files that live only on the owner's PC": `tools/update_backend.py` and `scripts/apply-patches.ps1` now say they open real, VISIBLE browser windows and name the opt-in to run them, while either kind of skip still means NOT fully proven. `run_suites.py` no longer calls `jarvis_chatbot_web`'s launch "the only visible launch in the backend" (`jarvis_browser_control.py` also launches visibly by default, and the completeness pin covers only direct importers of `jarvis_chatbot_web`). And the runner's command line is safe: `--help` prints usage and exits 0 without running a suite, and a path-shaped or unknown argument (`backend/test_x.py`, a typo) fails with the bare form to use (`test_x.py`) instead of being silently dropped and running the whole fleet - the trap two agents hit on 2026-10-26. Honest limits: the real browser tests still run only on explicit opt-in, and the completeness pin is not a backend-wide guarantee.
- **The Jarvis Live screen is shorter.** The long headset-button explanation that sat under the microphone line is gone, at the owner's request of 2026-10-26; the button works exactly as before, and the one-line "Microphone: this phone's" note stays.
- **The owner-facing name is "Incognito", not "Temporary chat" (2026-10-26).** The words the desktop and the phone own change, in both apps and in the words fixture they share: the quickbar button's screen-reader name and its two tooltips, the strip's label and its one line on an empty chat, "Incognito started" / "Incognito ended", the honest refusal an older PC gets ("Incognito isn't available on this PC's version of Jarvis, so nothing was sent…"), the unconfirmed-answer warning, "Remember: is off in an incognito chat", and the game line ("This looks like a game or role-play, so it's an incognito chat: nothing is kept or learned"); Settings' Write like me detail names it too. Grammar follows the name rather than a blind replace ("an incognito chat", "Incognito is off: a continued chat is kept"). `tools/gen_history_cases.py` carries the two shared history sentences and was run twice into both fixtures (byte-identical: `sha256 C50E353F…`, and `--check` clean). Nothing internal moved: the `temporary` request/route field, `capabilities.temporary_chat`, `TemporaryChat` / `setTemporaryChat` / the `TEMPORARY_*` constant names, ids, permission names and patch filenames are unchanged, and the feature catalogue's title stays "Temporary chat" for the later rename. One visible line is deliberately unchanged: Live's strip still says "Temporary is on - this Live session will not be kept in History.", because that sentence is `jarvis_live.SEEN["temporary_on"]` on the PC, which both apps copy and no app owns (backend out of scope for this change).

- **The secret hand-off's fill now re-checks the SAME control, not one whose name is a suffix of it (2026-10-26).** `secret_handoff_live_snapshot` matched the stored control id with `expected.endswith(":" + identity)`, so an id whose identity had shrunk from `login:foo:bar` to `bar` (or any id whose last colon segment equalled the live identity, `uia:` or not) read as unchanged and the card followed a different control; it now splits the stored id into at most six parts and compares the whole sixth field exactly, and an id that is not a well-formed `uia:` id never matches, so a moved target still reads as "changed" and the engine still voids it. The HUD-focus suspicion was measured, not assumed: with the owner's own `jarvis-desktop.exe` holding Windows foreground and the target password control reporting `HasKeyboardFocus=False`, `fill` answered 200 and the throwaway control received exactly the fake secret (8/8 live checks), so nothing was added to the fill path; two headless tests now pin that measured shape (the stored control is focused before typing, and no focus reading reaches the snapshot the engine voids on), and the four live-test scripts stay in gitignored scratch because they need a real desktop and a live backend.
- **The owner-secret hand-off can fill a password field that is the first control in its window (2026-10-26).** Two comparison bugs in the fill path, found by the live field test one after the other: the stored walk index 0 was read as "missing", so a real password field was re-measured as "no longer password-masked" and the fill was voided with nothing typed; and the target's stored id was matched against the wrong part of the id, so the engine would have voided with "the focused input control changed". Both checks are fixed and pinned by tests, and `UiaMeasurementError` now records the exception's `winerror` when it has no `hresult`, a value kept on the in-process error only - the create refusal still answers 409 with the same fixed `E_*` reason_code and a plain sentence, so the response is unchanged.
- **Jarvis can explain any of its 140 features, in detail, and say how to change it (2026-10-26).** The owner's field test asked for Jarvis to talk about any feature of the project: chat knew only "what is <feature>", "is it on" and "how do I turn it on". It now answers nine questions from the same checked-in list both apps draw - "tell me about", "what does it do", "explain", "how do I use", "where is", "how do I turn on/off", "can I change" (new, read-only, built from the five owner-facing labels and the real switch table), "is it on" and "what is it" - and every answer names the feature, what it does, where it lives, whether it asks first, which apps have it, and how to use or change it. Three bugs that made features unsayable are fixed: a feature's own trailing "options" was eaten ("Animal options" became "animal"), the context-stripping step turned "Floating Jarvis" into "floating", and the "turn on" phrase stripped "Jarvis" from four names that contain it, silently emptying their typed phrase in `control_matrix()` (4 of 140 rows before, 0 after). The feature list gained `aliases` (30 rows, 55 names like "customer support" for "Deal with customer support for me", matched exactly so they cannot steal a general question) and a `surface_reason` for every desktop-only row, so a one-sided answer says why the phone has none; `tools/check_feature_list.py` now demands that sentence in both directions. The review's follow-ups are in too: "what are you capable of?" (the owner's own screenshot question, which the fast grammar could not match at all) now answers from the fixed "things you can say" list with no model, "where do I change the jarvis presets?" is a real where-question that reaches the Focus presets and How Jarvis talks rows, and the five phone-only controls (`phone-notifications`, `phone-watch`, `phone-floating`, `phone-tiles`, `phone-screen-rate`) no longer say they are changed "on this PC" - the row's `surface` wins over the PC-Only label's wording, and the owner-approved label counts are untouched. Measured: all 140 rows answer all nine questions by title, id and alias (before: "how do I use", "where is" and "can I change" reached no feature intent at all, 0/140; five rows could not be named; 127 answers never said how to use or change the feature). `backend/test_feature_guide.py` grew the completeness sweep (1732 checks when the nine questions landed, 1789 after the truth pass below), and `backend/test_feature_list.py` pins the two-way reason rule. The truth pass that landed with the phone's read-only Galaxy picture (the two branches met in `features/features.json`, and the Galaxy row is both apps' now) corrected the remaining false sentences: the four features BOTH apps have whose setting control is the PC's (the chat thread, the connection, the appearance, and the faces) no longer read "It is changed at Settings, a fragment on this PC" - the sentence is built from the row's own `surface`, names the Settings place as a place, and says the phone has the feature but cannot change the PC's setting; a phone-only row's Status line says "Phone-Only Setting (Changed in the phone's Settings)" instead of "PC-Only Setting", derived from `surface` for display only, so the owner-approved five-label counts and both apps' directories are untouched; `phone-floating` and `phone-screen-rate` no longer deny a control the PC has, because each row's own surface reason names the PC's different one; and "how do I change the presets?" now reaches the same row as "where do I change the presets?", through the same alias path and the same exact-name gate. Not verified: any live server or phone - the live probe is a script for the Lead, and no response from a running Jarvis was tested here.
- **The feature catalogue now has the desktop Private chat row it was missing (2026-10-26).** `features/features.json` (all three byte-identical copies) gains `private-chat`, the row the 2026-10-26 Private chip (#856) never got: the chip beside Incognito, what a private turn may do (read, search, answer) and must not do (change settings, save items, run actions), that it fails closed on an older PC rather than sending an ordinary chat, that it cannot be on at the same time as Incognito and that flipping it starts a new conversation, that it raises no card, and that the phone has no such chip on purpose (Incognito is its private mode). The row is Command-Only, so the contract's label mix moves 69 -> 70 there and 139 -> 140 total; `contract/feature-controls.json`, `backend/jarvis_feature_catalogue.py` and its `jarvis-backend/` twin, the desktop `feature-directory.js` with its test fixture, and the phone's `FeatureDirectory.kt` are regenerated by the repo's own writers, never retyped, and the `check_feature_controls` pin and the Android label-mix test follow. `private-chat` also joins the feature guide's `NO_COMMAND_REASON` (the reason a row has no typed control) and `check_feature_list`'s `LESSON_EXEMPT` (no lesson teaches it yet, with the reason written down, like Incognito). No app rebuild: the directories are generated data. - **Every Jarvis feature with a switch can now be changed by asking, in the owner's own words (2026-10-26).** The owner's request was to change any of Jarvis's own features in chat, on either app. Measured on the feature directory: 14 of 140 features could be switched by saying their own name, and 29 features the directory calls switchable were refused with the false sentence "that is a Command-Only ability ... there is no fixed sentence for it" - including Write like me, Your past chats, Lights, plugs and fans and Facts Jarvis saved by itself, whose switches all exist. A feature's own control table is now read when the section link misses it (17 switched by their own name, and the rest say which of their real switches to name), the PC's own "hey Jarvis" switch is reachable ("turn on the wake word", one approval card), and so is which browser Jarvis uses ("set the browser to headless"). The model's `change_setting` tool also resolves settings with named values now (the voice speed, the personality, the captcha hand-off), instead of refusing them as "on or off only". Nothing new loosens a rule without its own existing card, the deliberate page-only settings still refuse - and a sentence that names one is now answered by name even with filler words in it ("... the chat history setting" used to change chat history instead of saying where it lives) - and the "what can I change?" count is the measured one (69, was 67).
- **The feature answers stop contradicting the app (2026-10-26).** App lock is no longer called unturnoffable (it is a real Settings switch whose OFF asks Windows Hello first, and the rule that does stay on is named), the phone is no longer said to be unable to change the face or the shared appearance when its own Appearance screen pushes both to the PC, "A change that loosens a rule still asks on a card first." appears only on the switches that really raise one instead of on four that never do, and the Settings place is written the way the card writes it ("Settings, How Jarvis talks", not "how jarvis talks"). Every one of the five owner labels, their pinned counts and both apps' feature-directory data are unchanged.
- **Jarvis changes its face when asked, from either app, and both apps follow (2026-10-26).** Say "change your face to Spiral", "use the Orbit face", "switch the face to the red panda", "make your face the accretion face" or "put the spiral face on" in the desktop's chat box or on the phone, and the face changes on both, with no approval card: it is cosmetic. "What face are you using?" only reads. The face was already shared end to end - one `appearance.json` behind `GET`/`POST /api/appearance`, one `appearance` event, both clients repainting from it - so this adds the ASKING, not new plumbing: one named choice in `jarvis_settings_registry.py` whose setter calls `_appearance_set_face` in `appearance.patch`, and the same one write the Faces window uses, with one `face` key and one event. A face name is checked against `jarvis-visual-spec.json` itself, so an unknown face is refused with the real list and a PC with no spec refuses rather than storing a guess. The voice does not change, and the answer says so: a face never changes the voice by itself, and the "does this animal use its own voice?" question is still the Faces window's offer (an open owner question, below). **A pre-existing defect is fixed first, in its own commit:** `POST /api/appearance` used to replace the whole document, and the phone sends `bindings` with a blank face left out, so the first colour change pushed from a fresh phone wrote `face: null` and erased the face the desktop was wearing; the write now merges - a key it carries replaces, a key it does not carry keeps its stored value - and clearing is explicit (`face: null`, `bindings: {}`). Records moved to the measured truth: the `faces` feature row is no longer PC-Only (it is changeable by asking, so the label mix is 41/2/15/70/12), the contract gains the face choice with its measured options, the three `features.json` copies and the phone/desktop generated directories were rebuilt by their own writers, and `docs/APPEARANCE-API.md`, `docs/JARVIS-API.md` §58, `backend/README.md` and the counts in `README.md`/`docs/SETTINGS-BY-CONVERSATION.md` say so. Checks: `test_appearance.py` 19/19 (five new merge checks), new `test_appearance_command.py` 16/16 (every sentence, the refusal, the honest no-patch answer, the one-key write), `test_settings_registry` 141, `test_settings_tool` 145, `test_settings_switches` 89, `test_settings_help` 88, `test_settings_rows` 21, `test_quick_wins` 217, `test_feature_guide` 1644, `check_feature_controls` clean, `check_feature_list` 9/0, both directory generators `--check` clean. **Two narrow limits remain, and they are written down rather than claimed away:** the read-modify-write has no lock, so two saves that overlap inside the threaded server can still lose one side; and a stored file that cannot be read just now is treated as corrupt, so a save in that moment can lose the rest. Neither is the ordinary partial write this fix addresses; `appearance.patch`'s own docstring, `docs/APPEARANCE-API.md` and `backend/README.md` record both. **Honest limits:** no phone or installed desktop was driven here, so "both apps follow" rests on the already-wired event path (`stream.rs` and `JarvisRuntime.refreshAppearance`) plus a test that the event carries the merged document; the desktop `frontend` job is what proves the regenerated directory and the Android compile. **Open for the owner (Q1):** a face asked for by chat skips the Faces window's "use the animal's own voice?" offer, so an animal face arrives with the voice unchanged - the answer now says that out loud, and whether the question should be asked on whichever device is showing is still the owner's call.

It starts a new conversation on every on/off, cannot be on at the same time as Incognito, and resets when the chat ends; a PC that does not confirm the turn as private is never pretended to have kept it. Tests: Rust capability/flag/route checks + `jarvis-desktop/tests/private-chat.mjs` against the existing headless uikit mock.

The new DELTA_TABLE entry says `"hunks": True` and carries the patch's own hunks: each hunk's whole pre-image block is replaced by its whole post-image block in file order, a hunk whose own added lines are already in the file is skipped so a second deploy is a no-op, and a hunk whose pre-image block is missing or not exactly once refuses the whole file instead of editing a shifted live HUD. `backend/test_reconcile_live_backend.py` proves it hermetically against the published base HUD as the pre-image: the 13 inserts, `py_compile` on the staged file, a clean second plan (`ALREADY_PRESENT`), and a shifted hunk refused with nothing staged. The read-only dry run against the real live folder now exits 0 and prints `HUD DELTAS … to insert: 1: private-chat.patch -> jarvis_hud.py (13 hunk(s))`, `rehearsal: py_compile OK for jarvis_hud.py`, `PLANNED CHANGES: 16 file-level change(s) - DRY RUN, nothing written`; two runs are byte-identical. Nothing was installed, restarted or written live while verifying.
- **The "All features and abilities" directory now says the Memory tab, the Work tab and "Bring in old chats" are on the phone too, and three docs stop claiming wiring they do not have (2026-10-26, records honesty pass).** `features/features.json` marked `memory-tab`, `import-chats` and `work-tab` as desktop-only although the phone draws all three (`jarvis-client/.../MenuCatalog.kt:101` the Memory tab, `:104` the Work tab, `:112` "Bring in old chats"), so the rows now say `both` in the owner's list and its two app copies, and every generated artefact was rebuilt with the repo's own writers (`tools/gen_feature_guide_catalogue.py`, `tools/gen_feature_directory.py`, `jarvis-client/tools/gen_feature_directory.py`) - never hand-edited. `docs/WHAT-ACTUALLY-SHIPS-2026-10-17.md` is re-checked against `origin/main` `7acd7db54`: the ten pull requests it called open are nine merged and #554 closed, and the shelf rows for self-improve, ollama-contract, tool-schema, hf-inspect and the decks slice now name their real callers instead of "Open PR". `scripts/apply-patches.ps1` and `docs/MODELS-ON-D-2026-10-10.md` no longer call `may_download()` "the ONE gate every download passes": it has no caller outside its own test yet, and both now say so plainly. README's price-radar line says there is no Settings switch, route or app screen for it yet, matching ARCHITECTURE §8. Checks: `check_feature_list` 9/0, `check_feature_controls` clean, all three generators `--check` clean, `jarvis-desktop/tests/feature-directory.mjs` green, the four staged feature suites 1741 passed / 0 failed, and parity, docs-map, README-truth, source-pin, scratch, stale-twin and both base suites clean. Honest limits: there is no Android build here - the Kotlin generator is checked and its JVM test runs in CI; and no feature surface was added - this pass only stops the catalogue and docs overclaiming what exists.
- **The visible-browser facts for `jarvis_browser_control` are pinned, not assumed (2026-10-26).** `backend/test_browser_test_guard.py` (70 checks on a PC where `JARVIS_BACKEND` names the installed copy, 69 in a clone, up from 64) reads the shipped module through `_where.BACKEND` and pins that `_HEADLESS` is `False` and that its launch passes `headless=_HEADLESS`; pins that `test_browser_control_live.py` is the ONLY backend suite that assigns `_HEADLESS` (setting it to `True` for itself); and freezes the module's importer set at the five suites that import it today, so a new suite that imports it is noticed. It does not claim every importer launches invisibly - an import cannot decide that, so the live suite's override is pinned by name instead - and the guard suite's existing completeness pin still covers `jarvis_chatbot_web` only.
- **A partial appearance save merges instead of erasing the other half (2026-10-26).** `POST /api/appearance` used to replace the whole stored document, and its writers send different shapes: the phone sends `bindings` on every push and a `face` only once its owner has picked one (`AppearanceStore.toSyncDocument` leaves a blank face out), and the Faces window sends a fresh document. So the first colour change pushed from a fresh phone wrote `face: null` and erased the face the desktop was wearing, and a face-only save erased every state binding the same way. The write now merges - a key the request carries replaces its stored value, a key it does not carry keeps the stored one - and clearing is explicit (`face: null` or `""` clears the face, `bindings: {}` clears the state bindings). Only the request is validated, so a stored document a newer spec would refuse cannot make a colour change fail. Proved by `backend/test_appearance.py`, 19 checks, five of them new for the merge; against the old text the suite fails at the first merge check with `KeyError: 'idle'`. **Two narrow limits are recorded, not claimed away:** the read-modify-write has no lock, so two saves that overlap inside the threaded server can still lose one side, and a stored file that cannot be read just now is treated as corrupt, so a save in that moment can lose the rest - both are in the patch's own docstring, `docs/APPEARANCE-API.md` and `backend/README.md`.
- **The stale teaching sentences now say what the apps really do, and "skill" is defined the way the engine defines it (2026-10-25).** `features/features.json` (all three copies kept byte-identical): "How Jarvis talks" describes the nine built-in personalities, the four dials and "My personalities" (up to 20 saved mixes; a personality per chat, which a temporary chat carries today); the chat-thread row adds the quiet "Used in this answer" line and the facts it lists; temporary chat's `limit` says the third promise - it learns nothing, no fact saved even with a "Remember:" - instead of repeating the `what`; the pictures row's `asks` says a draw may first ask, in words, whether Jarvis may set the everyday chat aside for about seven minutes, and sending the same request again is your yes from either app; the Skills row's empty `limit` now says a skill is instructions that cannot run anything, is installed only on the PC, and is checked again on every load; Galaxy's `what` now names the three flat layouts (Star map, Clean tree, Zen ring) that sit beside the constellation; the faces row's `where` says the HUD's face page also shows in a browser on the PC; and the approvals `limit` names the four cards that wait for the owner instead of expiring after 15 minutes - pairing a device, unretiring a shared key, registering a phone's approval key, and restoring a backup. `backend/jarvis_tutorials.py` (carried byte-for-byte to `jarvis-backend/`): the `how-it-talks` lesson gains "Pick a personality, and hear it" (and its first step no longer names the gone "Short answers" switch); the `model-page` step describes a skill as a folder with a `SKILL.md`, pointing at Brain → Model → Skills; the `faces` step says the face also sits in a browser page on the PC; and the `screens-and-pictures` lesson gains "Ask for a picture" - what drawing is, where the kept ones are on both apps, and that sending the request again is your yes. Four lessons' `version` goes 1 to 2 (`how-it-talks`, `model-page`, `faces`, `screens-and-pictures`), so a finished lesson is offered again on the new words. The catalogue is regenerated; 139 features, 139 rows and 36 lessons are unchanged.
- **A feature row must name words its lesson really uses (2026-10-25).** The teaching gate could not see the last lie in `features/features.json`: a row that names a lesson which never mentions the feature. "Save a draft to your Drafts folder" could name `home-calendar-email` - a lesson with no "draft" in it - and every rule stayed green, so "it is taught" was a claim nothing tested. New rule 7 in `tools/check_feature_list.py`: every taught row carries `must_mention`, 1-3 lowercase words or short phrases that must occur, whole word and case-insensitively, in the step `title`+`body` text of the lessons that row names. A missing word fails naming the feature, the lesson and the word; so does a taught row with no `must_mention` (the gate cannot be skipped) and a `must_mention` left on a row no lesson teaches. 104 of the 118 taught rows now carry 174 verified words, and the three copies of the file are kept byte-identical. The other 14 rows are a measured result rather than a silence: their lessons genuinely lack the subject - `home-calendar-email` has no "draft", "archive", "trash" or "tidy"; `when-something-is-wrong` no "rush", "latch" or "banner"; `limits-and-notes` no "update", "version" or "release" - so they are listed in `TEACHING_GAP` with the lesson and what it leaves out, a work list for the teaching side that empties itself (a row that gains keywords fails until its gap line is deleted). `backend/test_feature_list.py` proves the rule bites: a synthetic row whose made-up lesson lacks its word fails, naming all three, and the real list passes; all three defects planted in the real file at the desk failed the checker too. The checker reads step text only, never `where`, so a keyword cannot be earned by a breadcrumb. 139 features, 139 rows and 36 lessons are unchanged.
- **A schedule job now remembers the private turn it was written in (2026-10-26).** A job, to-do, timer or reminder created or edited while Jarvis's private latch was on stores `source_taint = "read_outside"` in a new `jobs` column (added by the existing migration loop, so an old schedule.db opens exactly as before and its rows read as trusted). A snooze copy inherits the mark, a repeating job keeps it when it re-arms, and firing the row replays it through `jarvis_gate.latch_taint(why="scheduled_task:read_outside")` once, before anything the row can influence runs - so the taint is no longer lost when the latch's own 30 minutes expire. No `on_fire` callback in this checkout can start an agent turn or run a tool today, so this is defence in depth for a future one rather than a fix for a live exploit.
- **Four forgotten branches' documents and records land (2026-10-25, docs/records only).** Review #1's prompt-stack measurer and its findings page, the Design 5C and watcher quiet-rules design documents, one new claims row (R10) and one closed defect-register row (DEF-014); `docs/README.md`'s count moves 400 to 403 and its size figure is deliberately left alone. No product code, no backend, no Android, nothing pushed.
- **The HUD whitelist deploy is rehearsed and refuses drift (2026-10-25).** `tools/deploy_hud_whitelist.py` reads a live `jarvis_hud.py` and the published base at a git ref, refuses any live-only or differing `STATIC_ASSETS` key (the drift the 2026-10-25 hand insertion's safety argument did not cover), and dry-runs the exact missing entry lines by default. `--apply` is a pure insertion before the AST-found closing brace, with a `.before-<date>` backup, a temp-file-plus-`os.replace` write, and a read-back compile and table check that restores the original on failure; `--verify-live` checks the sixteen face keys and their served files without git or network. `backend/test_deploy_hud_whitelist.py` proves the pure insertion, the refusal, the no-op dry run, the atomic apply and the corrupted-anchor refusal in temp folders only. The tool never copies the served files or restarts the server.
- **Records: the feature list is 139 rows and Jarvis has nine personality presets (2026-10-26, bookkeeping only).** The guide and settings modules were still counting the older, smaller lists - "138 rows" against the 139 rows `features/features.json` actually holds, and "eight personality presets" against the nine `jarvis_manner.py` carries - and the docs that quote them repeated it. Corrected in the shipped modules and their base twins, in the test's printed check line, in the generator's own docstring and in `docs/JARVIS-API.md`; and `plugins/registry.json` plus `plugins/README.md` are regenerated now that `private-chat.patch` is in the stack, so the `audit` job's plugin-catalogue check is green again. Nothing here changes what Jarvis does: the row counts are prose, and the generated catalogue is byte-identical to what the patcher now reports.
- **The record files are now proven to be real UTF-8, not just bytes that happen to decode.** A new suite, `backend/test_records_encoding.py`, fails if `docs/CLAIMS.tsv`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/defect-register.tsv`, `docs/DEFECT-REGISTER.md`, `docs/ABOUT-JARVIS.md` or `README.md` stops decoding as strict UTF-8 or carries a double-decoded character (a lone U+00C3, a lone U+00C2, U+00E2 U+20AC, or U+FFFD). That is the class that put a garbled section sign into one `docs/CLAIMS.tsv` row on 2026-10-25 (fixed by #816): mojibake is *valid* UTF-8, so every count checker stays green while the text is wrong, and only a reader noticed. The suite also proves its own detector against the incident's own bytes, so a pattern that silently stopped matching cannot pass as green. `changelog.d/` is deliberately not scanned: one fragment quotes the bad bytes on purpose, as the record of the bug.
- **The Python lock is rebuildable again, at Python 3.11, and filelock's three advisories are cleared (2026-10-25).** `backend/requirements.txt` no longer names `tomli` (the Python 3.10 backport, for the install range the lock stopped covering on 2026-10-21) and now names `filelock>=4.2.1`; both `requirements.lock` twins were regenerated with the repaired recipe (`uv pip compile --python-version 3.11` plus five documented one-package cut-off exceptions) and `filelock` moved 4.0.0 -> 4.2.1 through it, the order the 2026-10-23 decision requires. Measured against the file it replaced: every (name, version) pin the old lock carried for Python 3.11 and newer is unchanged with the same PyPI file hashes, the only version that moved is filelock, and the 13 dead Python 3.10 entries and their 3.11-only markers are gone (lock body: -372/+40 lines; both lock copies byte-identical). `tools/check_python_advisories.py` passes both offline and against PyPI, the 2026-10-21 owner approval of the 3.10 -> 3.11 floor is recorded in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, and the lock's header now carries the exact working command and the measured proof.
- **The product check is built offline, in two halves (2026-10-26).** The engine (`backend/jarvis_product_research.py`) takes review records - rating, date, verified purchase, Vine label, title, body, helpful votes, variant, every field optional - and returns a reasons-based report: the "what Amazon chose to show, not all of the reviews" caveat; eight evidence-only signals with counts, examples and thresholds (verified share, Vine share, incentive wording, own shingle/MinHash near-duplicate wording with possible/shortlisted/compared pair counts, date burstiness whose flags actually use the stated listing-or-sample baseline, rating-histogram shape, stylometry, review-versus-product mismatch); dates calendar-validated so an impossible date is unknown instead of crashing the report; what buyers agree, complain and are mixed about from the local model's aspect reading, with every quote accepted only when it is at least 8 characters and a real substring of ONE review's title OR body (invented, short and boundary-crossing quotes dropped and counted); a "set aside, and why" list that removes nothing; body-only `with_text`; "not enough trustworthy data" as a first-class outcome; and what could not be checked. The read half (`backend/jarvis_product_read.py`) turns one page's supplied HTML into the listing facts and review records in that same schema, with honest warnings for a challenge/sign-in page, not-a-product page, missing listing fields, no reviews, a truncated page or review block and missing review fields, plus `assemble_report()` which returns the engine report with the records for debugging; `capture_with_owner_browser()` is the named seam and raises a documented `CaptureNeedsOwnerBrowser` instead of pretending to fetch. Deliberately no per-review fake/real label and no numeric trust score, no route/tool/card/Settings/app surface, no network or storage, standard library only, nothing imports either module yet. Tests: `backend/test_product_research.py` (118 checks) and `backend/test_product_read.py` (67 checks), synthetic fixtures only, network spies and AST source scans that would fail on a forbidden import or call. **Honest limits:** all evidence is synthetic - no real Amazon page or review sample has been parsed; the thresholds are readable rules, not a calibrated classifier; the capture seam is a placeholder until the owner's signed-in browser session is wired; the sample is Amazon's curated list, not all reviews. Docs: `docs/DESIGN-PRODUCT-RESEARCH.md`.
- **A private chat is strictly reads-only (backend Slice 1, 2026-10-25).** `"private": true` on `POST /api/chat` now implies the temporary chat - no memory used, nothing learned, nothing kept in history - and additionally refuses every disk write a turn can reach: chat history and its own housekeeping (the module is not called at all), the memory store and the background learner, the feedback turn row, `speed.jsonl`, the route audit line, `hud-budget.json`, `approvals.db`, `receipts.db`, the answer-sources row and the device's `last_seen` (held by the devices wrapper and dropped when the body says private). It runs no tool at all: the agent itself is handed `private: true` and empties its own tool list (the owner's framework file cannot re-enable one), answers any tool call with a refusal instead of dispatching it (so even the settings/schedule shortcuts, which bypass the approval gate by design, cannot run), skips the tool-chain audit line, the next_time/energy writes and the Write Like Me drafting block (no voiceprint.db, no embedding-model download, no stored writing sample in the prompt), raises no card (no approval or undo receipt exists for one), refuses every mutating fast-path command with one plain sentence BEFORE anything changes or is receipted, and leaves nothing in RAM-only registries either. The gate fails closed: a missing or broken shipped module still refuses the private turn instead of making it ordinary. The single gate and the sentence live in the new shipped `jarvis_private_chat.py`; `X-Jarvis-Route` carries `"private": true` and `/api/version` advertises `capabilities.private_chat`, false on an older server. The capability reports true only when both the hud patch and the shipped module are present. Neither app sends the flag yet and neither has a UI for it - Slice 2 is their half; the backend contract is already the one they will read (JARVIS-API section 156).
- **The PC is now the one owner of the picture swap's sentence, so neither app can print it twice (2026-10-25).** One `POST /api/pictures` answer used to carry the same sentence - *"Your everyday chat stepped aside for N seconds and is back on the card after another M seconds."* - in two places: the top-level `words` (and `error` when a draw failed) and again in `card.words`. PR #818 stopped the desktop panel printing both, but the answer still carried two copies and the phone's own `Pictures.madeWords` joins them, so the phone printed the sentence twice too. The backend now sends it once: a drawn picture says it in `words`, a failed draw says it in `error`, and `card` keeps only the machine-readable times and has no `words` key; the desktop panel's `words || card.words` guard is unchanged for a PC that has not taken this update. The desktop, the phone and the Rust boundary all show the owner the sentence exactly once, with no Kotlin change, and the new tests count the sentence in the whole answer (a phrase, not the word "words") on a drawn picture, a failed draw and a chat that did not come back - so a second copy in any field fails them.
- **A slow picture draw no longer looks like a failure on either app (2026-10-25).** One drawing on the PC can legitimately take up to **1,680 s** in the worst case - the five ceilings are `unload_lane(timeout=120)` 120 s, `DRAW_WAIT_S = 600` 600 s, the `POST /free` call's own `FREE_WAIT_S` 180 s, the `/system_stats` poll's second `FREE_WAIT_S` 180 s and `warm_lane(timeout=600)` 600 s, and 120 + 600 + 180 + 180 + 600 = 1,680 s (`backend/jarvis_picture_card.py:526` and `:564-565`; `backend/jarvis_pictures.py:197` and `:208`) - but both apps gave the one long silent request only 1,200 s, so a legitimate slow draw plus the chat warm-back could outlast the app and the owner saw a false failure. The desktop's `PICTURE_TIMEOUT` and the phone's `CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_S` are now **1,740 s**, a minute over that sum, and the "about 20 minutes" wording where it described the client wait is now "about 29 minutes". `FREE_WAIT_S` is counted twice because `free_models()` calls `/free` with `timeout=FREE_WAIT_S` and *then* sets `deadline = time.time() + FREE_WAIT_S` for its `/system_stats` poll: the earlier draft of this change counted that step once, said four ceilings and 1,500 s, and was 180 s short. The relationship is pinned on all four sides so the two cannot drift apart again: a Rust unit test asserts the budget exceeds the 1,680 s sum and names each of the five terms, a JVM test asserts the same for the phone, the desktop source-pin suite reads the budget back out of `brain.rs`, and `backend/test_pictures.py` reads the PC's own defaults plus `free_models`' source (to prove the second wait is really there), asserts their sum is 1,680 s, and reads both clients' source to prove each budget is above it. No backend production code changed - the PC's own timeouts were correct and only the clients were too eager. The phone change is CI-verified only: there is no Android build, emulator or phone here.
- **The phone's microphone works again, and Home is one shared More menu lighter (2026-10-25, Android only).** A tap on the talk button now starts a recording that outlives the tap - a second tap sends, a sustained hold still sends on release, sliding away still cancels - because the gesture was keyed on the very capture state it changed, so every press began and instantly cancelled itself; Full screen's microphone is drawn only when the PC offers push-to-talk. Home's redundant "Open the Brain →" caption is gone; Find moved from the status line into Help (hidden on an unpaired phone, because Find is not reachable there); and the chat bar keeps only the typing box, Send and the microphone: Photo, Think, Coach this, the temporary-chat toggle, Earlier chats, Notifications, Quick note and Jarvis Presets now live in one More menu, shared by the chat bar and the full-screen face bar, each row shown only when this PC offers it. Android-only on purpose - the desktop has no equivalent of this phone Home screen - and proved by source pins plus a pure JVM test, not by a run: this container has no local Gradle, so CI's frontend job is the compiler.
- **You can now make a personality by talking to Jarvis (2026-10-25).** Settings -> "How Jarvis talks" already let you type a name, one line and one example; what was missing was the conversation. Say **"make me a personality like a grumpy but caring mechanic"** (typed or spoken, desktop or phone) and the PC answers in chat: it asks the LOCAL model once to draft a name, how it should sound, one example exchange and which of the nine built-in presets it starts from, then shows the whole draft and says `"save it"` keeps it. If you did not say what it should be like, Jarvis asks for one line and treats your next message in that conversation as the description (remembered in memory only - nothing is written to disk, and a new conversation never inherits it). Refine it in your own words - "make it shorter", "call it Old Timer", "base it on the butler" - and say `"save it"`; the row is saved through the SAME `jarvis_manner.save_user_personality()` path the Settings form uses (name 60, line 240, each example half 240, 20 saved at most), so `"switch to <name>"` finds it straight away. `"forget it"` drops a draft; a temporary chat is refused because it remembers nothing. Wording only: no new route, no approval card, no permission change, and the model is only ever the local one - asked on the same lane the chat uses, for the chat's OWN model name (the HUD's own resolver, never a copied base name like qwen3.5:9b), at the chat's own context size, so drafting neither loads a different model nor reloads it at a smaller one - the draft is fields the PC validates, never a finished prompt, so a personality cannot change what Jarvis may do. Two stale defects found on the way are fixed: the quick answer and the settings catalogue both said "the eight personalities" and then listed nine. New `backend/jarvis_personality_creator.py` (shipped whole, called from `jarvis_quick.py`'s `/api/chat` fast path), `backend/test_personality_creator.py` (132 checks), docs in `docs/JARVIS-API.md` section 27.7. Honest limits: no real WebView2 or phone window was driven - the checks run the real chat fast path with the model stubbed - and the Settings "Draft it for me" button is the next slice, not this one.
- **The Settings description of "How Jarvis talks" now matches the screen (2026-10-25).** The menu catalogue still said "Warm and brief, or plain." while the screen has nine built-in personalities and four tuning dials, warm and brief by default. The true sentence replaces it in the backend menu list, the published base copy, and the generated desktop and phone catalogues. Wording only - no setting, approval or behaviour changes.
- **A changed MCP `tools/list` asks the owner again (2026-10-25).** Next to the existing start fingerprint, `jarvis_mcp.py` now remembers a 32-byte SHA-256 of each server's normalized `tools/list` result in `mcp-approved.json`, and a later list whose digest differs raises the same start card again (the server is stopped, the old yes is forgotten, and nothing new is written without a person's yes). Only the 64-character hex digest is stored - never the tools/list text, a tool name, a description or a schema - and tools are put in name order first, so a server that reorders the same list does not ask. A record with no digest or a malformed one is treated as unconfirmed and asks (fails safe), so an approval written before this check asks once and then records its digest. `backend/test_mcp_digest.py` proves the stored shape, the no-card unchanged path, the re-ask and refused-change path, and the missing/malformed digest fallback against the real fake server.
- **Image mode: asking for a picture now sets the everyday chat aside for the draw, brings it back whole, and either app may approve it (2026-10-25).** The picture-maker could not use this PC's 12 GB card while the everyday model held it, so a draw now takes TWO requests: the first answers `409` with `needs: "free_card"`, a plain sentence saying the chat will pause for a few minutes and come back, and the measured estimate; nothing is unloaded and nothing is drawn. The same request sent again with `"free_card": true` is the owner's consent, and the route unloads the everyday chat lane (`keep_alive: 0` - the lane's settings untouched), draws, asks ComfyUI to release its models (its own `POST /free`) and waits for its own `/system_stats` to show no more than 1 GiB before warming the chat back, reporting both wall-clock times in the answer's `card` block and in words. The warm-back runs in a `finally`, so a refused or crashed draw never leaves the chat unloaded, and there is still no approval card - the card belongs to the "Making pictures" switch, which stays off by default. **Either app may now confirm the swap** (owner decision 2026-10-24, in their own words: "i would like to be able to approve this from my phone as well"): the swap is local and reversible and the chat reloads on its own, so a paired phone's `free_card: true` is the same deliberate request as the desktop's; only changing where pictures are kept or which program draws them stays PC-only. One picture is drawn at a time: a second confirm while a draw is running answers one plain sentence (`409`, `needs: "drawing"`) and starts no second draw. Way A (the recommended one) is the swap; way C never touches the chat; way B is recorded but not wired and says so. `DRAW_WAIT_S = 600` covers the measured 274.4 s draw plus a cold load, and `FREE_WAIT_S = 180` bounds the release wait. **The measured `/free` answer is HTTP 200 with a 0-byte body** - the old client parsed that empty body as JSON, failed in 0.0 s, and warmed the chat back while ~10.9 GB was still being released; the first real draw (2026-10-24) came back 1.36 GB spilled. The wait now accepts the empty answer (`allow_empty=True` on that one call) and polls the card. **Measured on this PC, four real Chroma1-HD draws through this code** (RTX 2060, ComfyUI 0.39.0, 1152x1152 at 26 steps): the 2026-10-24 red panda (2,008,307 bytes, draw 383.6 s, wall 420.8 s, the 1.36 GB spill above); a 2026-10-25 lighthouse (1,409,863 bytes, draw 379.0 s) which found the empty-answer cause; a 2026-10-25 cabin with the wait fixed (2,087,566 bytes, draw 381.0 s, `models_freed: true`, chat back WHOLE - `size_vram == size`, spill 0 - 23.6 s after stepping aside); and the 2026-10-25 phone-path ship where both requests carried `X-Jarvis-Client: phone` (1,721,489 bytes, draw 382.0 s, wall 500.1 s, `models_freed: true`, chat back whole, spill 0). `docs/JARVIS-API.md` §129.4b/§129.8 records the contract and the numbers, `docs/DECISIONS.md` carries the phone decision, and `backend/test_pictures.py` (245 checks) proves the 409, the order (aside before draw, free and warm-back after), the empty-`/free` answer, the release wait, the error-path warm-back, way B's sentence, the phone's first 409 and confirmed success, the one-draw-at-a-time sentence, and the still-PC-only settings route, with the card module stood in for.
- **The Studio layout's Galaxy stage draws its map again.** The stage was a visible, permanently empty canvas: the map's draw loop only ran when the page's own view was "brain", and Studio shows the map beside the conversation while that view stays "talk". The loop now treats the Studio Galaxy tab as on screen, and a tab change tells it to re-check. A new `hud.mjs` check counts the pixels the canvas actually painted instead of trusting a visible canvas.
