- **The browser HUD's three measured cockpit defects are fixed: overlapping right-rail cards, the `faces.html` 404, and preset names crushed to nothing (2026-10-25, the 1440x900 HUD sweep).** In the ordinary Cockpit view the right rail painted three cards on top of each other - `#rail-right`'s card region was a shrinkable flex item squeezed to 342 px while its six cards needed 798 px, so REMINDERS covered SECOND BRAIN & VAULT, FREE-TIER BUDGET and VOICE (three overlapping pairs measured at 239x119, 239x108 and 239x136 px), with a 0 px scroll range and no way to reach the two that were invisible. The rule that stops it already existed but only under `body[data-hud-edit="on"]`, so it worked while editing and not in the ordinary view that ships; the ordinary view now carries it, and Edit HUD is left exactly as it was: **3 overlapping pairs and 0 px of scroll before, 0 overlaps and 457 px after, Edit HUD unchanged at 813 px and 0**. Next, `#reactor` asked for `faces.html?mode=display&feed=parent` in every client, but the web server answers a closed list of named files (`STATIC_ASSETS` in `jarvis_hud.py`) with no such key, so a browser painted `{"error": "not found"}` where the face belongs and logged that 404 on every load: **1 request and 1 console error before, 0 and 0 after**, because a plain browser now leaves the frame at `about:blank` and draws a built-in CSS orb in the frame's own box (one shared grid cell, so every `#reactor` size rule sizes both), while the desktop shell's animated face is untouched. Last, `.preset-model` would not shrink and `.preset-name` had no minimum width, so a 60-character GGUF tag held 324 px, pushed the button to 272 px inside a 220 px rail and squeezed "General Uncensored" to 0 px and "Coding" to 5 px: **the names now measure 97 px and 88 px and the button 191 px**, nothing sits outside the rail and the page width still equals the window. Serving the face page instead was turned down on cost - it would mean 12 static files (the 405,699-byte `faces.html` plus the 11 scripts it loads), a 12-key whitelist and deploy-sync entries, all for one 5-line fallback. The page ships three times byte-identically (`backend/`, `jarvis-backend/`, `jarvis-desktop/src/`) and all three changed together; no backend, Rust, API or live-folder file did. Measured in headless Chromium at 1440x900 in Cockpit - a real tablet or laptop browser is a field-test row, not a pass.
- **The feature count is derived everywhere now, not typed as 139 (2026-10-25, tools).** Adding a 140th feature row used to break hardcoded count literals by construction: `tools/gen_feature_directory.py` refused any contract without exactly 139 rows, the backend feature-guide suite summed the five labels to 139 and pinned the Command-Only count at 69, the desktop abilities-directory suite typed 139 across its checks, and the Android directory test asserted it twice. This is a no-behaviour refactor: the total now comes from the single sources of truth - `features/features.json`, `contract/feature-controls.json`'s `feature_count` and row list, and the generated Kotlin `COUNT` (`len(rows)`) - the generator's header comment is written from `len(features)`, and the label counts are compared with the contract's own approved `label_counts` instead of retyped. Nothing visible changes: 139 features, 139 rows, 36 lessons, and every generated artifact is byte-identical. Honest limit: the Android test keeps the approved label-mix numbers, because no workflow runs that generator's `--check`, so it is the phone artifact's only stale-copy guard.
- **Six built-in FAQ answers now match what Jarvis really does (2026-10-25).** The privacy answer says a web search from your own words can go without a card, and names customer-support chats and a quiz sent to a cloud service as other ways things can leave the PC. "Jarvis isn't connected" no longer blames a loading model: a down model is its own "model not running" state, with the PC answering perfectly. Updating Jarvis now names `scripts/update-jarvis.ps1`, which patches the backend, updates the desktop app and starts Jarvis again, with the phone app as the next step. The second-graphics-card answer lists all seven optional extras. History no longer promises a "continued chats" mark that neither app has. The Windows Hello answer says a raise is done on the PC today because the phone has no Raise control yet, and that turning a private topic back on is just a card. The 20 questions stay, in the same order, and nothing else in the file changed.
- **A failed test run now says why it failed (2026-10-25).** When a test suite died on an exception, the report was `493 passed, 1 failed` and the word `Traceback`, with the reason - `PermissionError: [WinError 5] Access is denied` - hidden on stderr that nothing read. `tools/check_claims.py` and `backend/run_suites.py` now echo the exception line(s) as well as the summary. Verdicts did not move: the pass/fail answer still comes from the exit code and the suite's own counts, and no retry, timeout or tolerance was added. The test-only cause of the 2026-10-25 backup flake is fixed in the same pull request - the suite was archiving and restoring the checkout's own tracked `jarvis-framework.toml`, so two runs at once collided on the swap - and after the fix three concurrent runs gave 496/496/496 with no `.old` litter left behind.
- **The documents page's own count and size are re-measured, and its byte figure is checked again (2026-10-25).** `docs/README.md` still said it held `403` documents "adding up to about **9.7 MB**" on "**2026-10-13**", with the byte comment reading `9,687,760 bytes` - the clean tree at `8f92e38fb` holds the same **403 documents** and **10,481,745 bytes**, so the page's figure was 7.6% low (inside `tools/check_docs_map.py`'s tenth, which is why CI never saw it), and the date was twelve days old. The refresh's own nine bytes - the page is itself a document under `docs/` - make the self-consistent figure it now states `10,481,754`. This is the periodic refresh the owner's 2026-10-13 rule asks for, in its own PR with nothing else in it: the prose figure, the "around" reminder and the byte comment are re-measured with the page's own two commands, and the byte comment says "**about** `10,481,754` bytes" again - without that word the checker does not read the comment as a size statement at all, so it had been going unchecked since the wording lost it. The count is unchanged because no document was added or removed; the figures are the generator's own fixed-point rewrite (`python -B tools/gen_docs_map.py`), and `gen_docs_map.py --check`, `check_docs_map.py` and `check_readme_truth.py` all exit 0.
- **Image mode on the desktop asks before the chat steps aside, and says how long it will take (2026-10-25).** When the everyday chat is holding the 12 GB card the PC answers the first picture request with `409 needs: "free_card"` and draws nothing; the pictures panel now shows the PC's own question with the PC's measured minutes and one confirm button, "Set the chat aside and draw (about N minutes)", plus a "Not now", and only that click retries with `free_card: true` — nothing is re-sent on its own. While the handshake runs the panel says so and for how long, because a draw takes minutes, not seconds, and the waiting state is shown for the minutes the PC names. A drawing that comes back paints the PC's own success sentence, which already ends with the line saying the chat is back — shown once, not twice; a "Not now" leaves the everyday chat where it is and says so. No new approval card and no new permission: the "Making pictures" switch is still the card, still off by default, and where pictures are kept and which program draws them stay PC-only. **Honest limits:** the panel's two steps, waiting state and words are proven by the page tests against a stand-in PC; the installed desktop window was not driven through the two steps against a live backend in this wave, and no draw was made from the desktop's own confirm — the four measured draws are the backend's script and the phone-request path. The apps give one picture request 1,200 s, while the PC's own declared step ceilings add up to about 1,500 s (draw poll 600 s + `/free` 180 s + warm-back 600 s + unload 120 s), so a pathologically slow draw plus a slow reload could time the app out while the PC is still working; measured draws are 274-384 s, the phone uses the same 1,200 s, and changing that budget is a separate decision.
- **The claims register's one non-ASCII row is encoded correctly again.** A merge earlier tonight decoded `git show` output in the Windows code page instead of UTF-8, so row `C18`'s section sign became `Â§` and then `Ã‚Â§`; the row is restored byte-for-byte from its last clean copy (`5e1667971`) with its meaning unchanged, and `backend/test_claims.py` is 67 passed / 0 failed on the repaired register.
- **The task patch's generator check fails fast in CI (2026-10-25).** `tools/gen_tasks_patch.py --check` moved from the "Job list" step after the backend suites to its own step before them in the `backend` job, so a stale `backend/tasks.patch` is reported before the ~35 minutes of suites rather than after them. It is a CI step reorder only: the check itself, the two contract checks it used to share its step with (`gen_tasks_cases.py --check` and `job-list.mjs`), and everything they compare are unchanged, and the patch check still runs on every pull request.
- **The claims checker ignores this PC's own `JARVIS_BACKEND`, the way the suite runner already does (2026-10-25).** `tools/check_claims.py` started every `test:` row's suite with the environment it inherited, and `backend/_where.py` puts the folder `JARVIS_BACKEND` names - on this PC the LIVE backend folder - first on `sys.path`, so a routine claim check ran live code instead of this checkout and reported a red row on a `main` that was never red: claim MED-01 read `backend/test_medical_advocate.py exited 1: AssertionError: MedicalStoreUnavailable not raised`, on a suite that is 95/95 green with the variable removed. The checker now holds the contract `backend/run_suites.py:881-884` has held since 2026-10-21: without `--use-env-backend` it pops the ambient value, prints one line naming the value it ignored, and starts every suite with `subprocess.run(env=...)` explicitly, so nothing inherited from a parent process can put it back; with the flag the value is passed through on purpose. `backend/test_claims.py` proves the flag exists on the checker's own parser, that a child suite cannot see the ambient value, that it does see it when the flag asks, and that the note names the value.
- **Chat's fast lane answers the clock and stops promising what it cannot do, an answer is corrected when no tool backed its claim, and the two graphics cards keep their names and roles across a reboot (2026-10-25).** Seven live-campaign defects are fixed in the fast path that already owns the words, so no model choice is involved: `what time is it?` is answered from this PC's clock in 0.15 s instead of 82.1 s and 9.5 h wrong; `open <app>` refuses shell commands, scripts and two-part sentences and never promises a program the backend did not name; `turn on/off <real setting>` reaches the setting's own ask-tier card from code; `cancel that` after a read-only turn takes the reminder back again; DuckDuckGo is ready only when its last live test worked (choosing a provider tests it first); the feature guide answers when it is named; and the briefing stops repeating its Pulse lines, the PC-health answer is bounded to 2.5 s, and "how are you / how am I doing" get one honest line. `feature_guide` now accepts the blank "list the groups" call its own schema documents (the four-retry refusal loop cannot recur), and one plain correction line follows an answer that claims a search, a calculation, a feature list or a missing setting that no tool backed (per source; denials, questions and honestly backed claims are left alone). The two graphics cards are named by their job in both apps - "Chat card (RTX 2060 12 GB)" and "Spare card (RTX 2080 SUPER 8 GB)" - and the approved card keeps its role after a reboot, because the stored `master_card` UUID decides instead of load order; the existing message about moving monitor cables now only appears above 2,500 MiB free. Live preload also warms the prompt prefix, saving ~10.5 s on the first answer, and D10 (`/api/status` tool count) and D11's verified-quote receipts stay open, named in DECISIONS.
- **The browser HUD shows the real red panda again (2026-10-25, the owner's decision: serve the face).** The page's `#reactor` iframe has always asked the web server for `faces.html`, but the server's closed whitelist did not name that page or the scripts it loads, so every browser load painted `{"error": "not found"}` where the face belongs (and logged that 404 as its only console error). The face page and its whole import closure — sixteen files, 1,150,734 bytes: `faces.html`, the eleven scripts it loads, `face-words.js` and `jarvis-link.js` (imported by its inline script), and `card-words.js` and `replace-confirm.js` (imported by `jarvis-link.js`) — now ship beside `jarvis_hud.py`, are served by the same closed whitelist under the same token gate, and are pinned to their `jarvis-desktop/src` copies by `backend/test_hud_static.py`. The desktop app was already unchanged; no route, setting, approval or stored data changes.
- **The browser HUD asks the web server for the real face page again, and keeps the built-in orb only when the real page is not what loaded (2026-10-25, the page half of the serve decision #814 landed on the server).** #814 put `faces.html` and its whole import closure in the server's closed `STATIC_ASSETS` whitelist, but the page still hard-coded the browser fallback: `sync()` returned before setting any URL without the desktop shell (`jarvis_hud.html:2789` at `bb7699ee`) and the wrap always carried `.reactor-builtin` (`:2722`), so a browser never asked for the page and always drew the CSS orb. A plain browser now builds the same URL the shell builds - `faces.html?mode=display&feed=parent`, plus `&face=` once an appearance document is pushed - and the orb is only the fallback: the wrap starts with `.reactor-builtin`, the existing `load` handler reads the frame's own document, takes the class off only when it carries `display-mode` (the real page), and keeps or puts it back for anything else - a 404 body, an error page, or `about:blank` when the Galaxy view is up - so the server's 404 text is never painted and the orb is drawn in the frame's own box. The desktop shell's face path is unchanged: `sync()` already ran there, the class is still never added, and the state-feed, appearance, standby, serious and Galaxy-unload checks pass unchanged. Tests: two new checks in `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud.mjs` pin the browser branch - the served page loads, draws and is fed by this page's state machine, and a 404 answers exactly once with the orb kept (frame hidden, same box measured) - and one new check in `faces.mjs` pins `display-mode` as the success marker the page relies on; the old page fails both new HUD checks, so they are mutation-tested rather than written to fit. No route, whitelist entry, Tauri command, approval or stored data changed; the page ships three times byte-identically (`backend/`, `jarvis-backend/`, `jarvis-desktop/src/`). **This is not the browser showing the face yet**: on the owner's live install the server's whitelist and this page have not been deployed, so the live browser still falls back to the orb until the page, the whitelist and the sixteen static files are deployed with the owner's go.
- **The browser HUD's real face is live on this PC now, not just in the repo (2026-10-25).** The owner said merge and deploy, so the page, its sixteen face files and the sixteen `STATIC_ASSETS` keys went onto the live install and the backend was restarted; it now answers `/faces.html?mode=display&feed=parent` with 200, and a real browser draws the served face while the built-in orb stays the fallback for whenever that page is not the document that loaded. Before the deploy the same browser harness measured zero face requests and the orb, so this is a measured before/after on his own install. Nothing in the desktop shell changed, the two paths with no token still answer 401, and the whole deploy is reversible from the copies taken before it.
- **The phone's picture-confirm handshake gets a device test against a stub PC (2026-10-25).** The emulator smoke job now drives the phone's REAL client - the real `JarvisApi`, OkHttp clients, token store and picture reader - against an in-process stub PC, so the two-request handshake is checked on a device and not only in JVM source pins: the first request carries the owner's words alone and is answered `409 needs: "free_card"` with the PC's own sentence and minutes; only the owner's own tap sends the SAME words plus `free_card: true`; a second ask while a draw runs gets the PC's own still-drawing sentence; and an older PC's `403 pc_only` reads as its sentence plus the one honest line, never a bad pairing token. Every tap makes exactly one request to exactly `/api/pictures`, with the token and `X-Jarvis-Client: hud`, and nothing is retried. Honest limits: the Compose screen itself is not driven (this project has no Compose test rule on purpose, because the face's animation never idles), so this is the wire contract rather than the pixels; it runs on CI's emulator, not the owner's phone; and the stub answers at once, so the real 1,200-second draw budget is not exercised here.
- **The phone can confirm the graphics-card swap too, so image mode works from the phone (2026-10-25).** The owner's decision of 2026-10-24, in their own words: *"i would like to be able to approve this from my phone as well"*. The first request still draws nothing: the PC answers 409 with its own plain sentence and its own measured minutes, and the phone shows that sentence word for word with "Set the chat aside and draw (about N minutes)" and "Not now". Only that tap sends the same words plus `free_card: true` - one long request given a 1,200-second read timeout, because a real draw on this PC measured 500.1 seconds wall clock. The FIRST request gets the same long budget too: when the everyday chat is not holding the card, that request is the draw itself (measured 381-383 seconds), and the old five-minute bound would have thrown the picture away on the phone while the PC kept drawing it. Nothing is re-sent on a timeout, because the PC may still be drawing; a second request while one runs is the PC's own "Jarvis is still drawing the last picture" sentence, shown as sent; and an older PC's `403 pc_only` is its sentence plus one line saying it needs the newer Jarvis, never a bad-token message and never a raw code. "Not now" sends nothing and keeps the typed words. A 200 shows both of the PC's sentences (the drawn-in-N-seconds line and the swap's "your chat is back" line) and reads the gallery again. The plate is headed "Image mode", the owner's own name for it, and no new screen or preset was invented. The tests are pure JVM; the emulator smoke stays CI's.
- **The phone names its own failures instead of implying success (2026-10-25).** App control's hands now refuse a window Android gave but with nothing readable on it - the new stable code `ui_tree_blank` with a plain sentence - instead of sending the PC an empty snapshot that came back as "I could not work out one safe step from that screen"; and a `type` counts only when the field's own text reads back exactly the words Android was told to set, so a model can never be told "done" for a field that stayed empty (`text_not_taken`). No screenshot or guessed pixel tap was added: the PC's action set has no screenshot action, and raw pixel tapping stays declined. The change is Android-only (no backend change), and the two codes are phone-local because `/api/app-control/step` accepts only `result: done|failed`. The phone module was compiled offline on this PC and the full JVM unit suite passes (2,630 tests, including the 3 new source pins); GitHub Actions still compiles the release, and nothing ran on a phone - NOT-TESTABLE is not a pass.
- **The About page's "Where it is now" table is measured on a named commit again, and its one false checker claim is corrected (2026-10-25).** `docs/ABOUT-JARVIS.md` said its figures were "measured on this branch" without naming the tree, and eight of its fourteen rows had drifted since they were written: commits 3,115 → **3,713** (845 merges), features 129 → **139** (as `features.json` holds them: 110 on both apps, 21 desktop-only, 8 phone-only), backend patches 145 → **154**, backend suites 315 → **428**, desktop suites 184 → **226**, phone test files 218 → **247**, documents 379/about 9.3 MB → **403/about 10.5 MB**, and `check_*` tools 26 → **39**. Claims (158 rows, 143 built / 7 open / 8 unverifiable) and the defect register (14 rows, 13 closed, 1 open) were already right and are untouched. The documents row had said "the page's own figures are checked by `tools/check_docs_map.py`" — they were not: that checker reads `docs/README.md` (its default `--readme` is `<docs>/README.md`, and `tools/check_readme_truth.py:66-67` says `docs/ABOUT-JARVIS.md` is another page "with their own owners and their own checkers"), so the row now says where the checked copy really lives. The section names its tree — `main` at `8104e6d01` — because the page's own rule (its line 734) is that a checkable figure is *"a quotation with a date on it, not as a fact"*. Every row was re-measured with the command in its own third column, and the five quoted checkers were re-run at that commit and all exit 0: `check_docs_map.py` (`docs map OK: 403 documents, 10,477,392 bytes`), `check_feature_list.py` (8 passed, 0 failed), `check_claims.py` (158/143/7/8), `check_vacuous_checks.py` (433 suites, 31,510 checks) and `check_parity.py` (`No undecided drift.` with two `planned` warnings). The page's quotation of that checker now gives the line in full, including its closing "(the reason is in each row)."; the sixth About spot in `backend/test_claims.py` is re-anchored to accept the verbatim form as well as the older abbreviated one, which is what its own docstring asks for when the page is reworded. **Deliberately not touched:** the page's other dated snapshots outside this section (for example its `DECISIONS.md` line-count figure at line 829) still describe the 2026-10-14 tree the page was written from.
- **The About page's citations are dated to the tree it was written from, so its line numbers and sizes can be read honestly (2026-10-25).** `docs/ABOUT-JARVIS.md` said `path:line` meant "a line in a file on this branch" and named neither a branch nor a commit, while its prose and §10 table quote figures measured while writing. A read-only audit of the page against `origin/main` (`94b57ed0a`) produced 20 measured findings, most of them line pointers that no longer land: `README.md` has since been cut from 849 lines to 283 (13 citations now point past its end), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` grew by 1,047 lines (the §9 passages the page quotes moved from :2290-:2335 to :3172-:3215), `jarvis-desktop/src/settings.html`'s About card moved from :2755 to :3257, and one line inserted at `docs/DECISIONS.md:1745` shifted 16 citations by +1. The fix is not to re-point dozens of lines against a moving tree but to say which tree they are lines in: the page now names `origin/main` at `1e7d5c2a` (2026-10-14, the newest commit it read), says it was written from that tree "with a few figures refreshed by later edits", and says the counts and §10's sizes were measured for the page rather than today; §6 remains the one section re-measured since, at the commit it names. The named tree was measured to check the claim: at `1e7d5c2a`, `README.md` is 849 lines, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` 2,801, `CHANGELOG.md` 1,311, `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2,170 lines / 145,353 bytes = **142 KB**, and `backend/README.md` 19,148 — the page's §10 figures except that one, which was left as written rather than silently "corrected" against a different tree. The audit also confirmed a dozen numbers still exactly right (the 158 claims at 143 built / 7 open / 8 unverifiable, the 14-row defect register, `about.mjs`'s nine checks, the 531,882-line deletion commit, the six numbered videos), so none of those was touched. Not done here and named so it is not mistaken for done: re-pointing the individual citations against each source's current file, and the About page's other stale counts (145 `.patch` files and 26 checkers are 154 and 39 today) — those are stated as history now, and refreshing them is its own pass.
- **Third-party notices are current again (2026-10-24, build).** Regenerated `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` from the committed `Cargo.lock` after the dependency bumps in #778 and #792 left the generated file behind (Tauri 2.12.5, ico 0.6.0 in place of png 0.17.16, static_vcruntime added, serde_json patched). Nothing the owner can see changes; the informational `audit` job stops reporting the file as out of date.
- **The backend deploy tool can no longer plan from a stale checkout (2026-10-24, tooling).** `tools/reconcile_live_backend.py` now reads `$SHIPPED`/`$PATCHES` from the `--ref` it deploys (`git show <ref>:scripts/apply-patches.ps1`) instead of the working tree, and it refuses with exit 2 - printing both SHAs and the fix - when the checkout's HEAD is not that commit; `--allow-worktree-mismatch` overrides the refusal for tests and exotic checkouts. An empty `--ref` is refused too - it was the one value that slipped past both halves of the fix, because it would read the file list from the checkout and the module bytes from the git index (`git show :path`). Measured before the fix: a checkout behind `origin/main` planned 251 shipped files and "to add: none" while the ref carried 253, hiding the two modules #790 had added; the dry run stops after that plan, and an `--apply` from the same checkout still ended `RESULT: PASS`.
- **The prompt coach's "Coach this" bar appears with the coach switched on (2026-10-24).** The bar's gate was inverted: it drew nothing while the PC said the coach was ON, and drew the button while it was OFF - where tapping it could only produce the PC's "the prompt coach is switched off" refusal. One rule now decides it (`PromptCoach.barDrawn`): nothing before the PC answers, nothing while the coach is simply off, and the existing "this PC's Jarvis has no prompt coach" line still shown on an older PC so the owner is told rather than left with a silent gap. No new read, no write, no setting change. The bar's look and TalkBack order stay a field-test row, not a pass.
- **The flight price radar can read the live page now, and its 300-second floor is really a floor (2026-10-24, F1 fix after a live test).** Google Flights' public page is about 2.3 MB today (310 KB gzipped), not the 100-250 KB the first build's cap assumed, so the request now asks for gzip and the page cap is 8 MB, with a 2 MB wire cap and a bounded inflate that refuses a gzip bomb. The poll jitter now only ever delays, so no check can come sooner than 300 s. The live test also recorded the first real measurement (11 itineraries, lowest $856.00, SEA to NRT). The radar is still OFF, backend-only, and not a booking tool.
- **Six guards are written before the three waves that need them (2026-10-24, Doc 2 section 7).** The document workspace, GitHub-write and publishing waves are not built yet, so their safety rules arrive first, as new files only: a bounded zip/XML-member reader, a phone-upload name cleaner and safe join, a GitHub tree payload that refuses to delete everything with no base_tree, a project walk that refuses Windows junctions, and an EPUB/print XHTML allow-list. Nothing is wired into the product and no CI file changes; the 56-check adversarial suite is a desk gate (`tools/prebuild_guards/test_guards.py`, including a real junction probe).
- **The phone's Galaxy gets a read-only "at a glance" band (2026-10-24).** Offered three choices, the owner picked the light one: above the existing People and things list, the phone now shows how many names and facts it holds and the eight names that share facts with the most others, as plain labels that never announce as buttons. It is not a canvas and not a new page: the numbers come from the two reads the plate already makes, the shared-fact rule is the same one the rows use, nothing is tappable, nothing is written, and it is hidden while the list shows a past date. The signed "read-only list, zero canvas" ruling stands. Runtime look and TalkBack order are a field-test row, not a pass.
- **The phone finishes a backup on the PC with one tap (2026-10-24).** The owner upgraded his morning decision from "let the PC finish a backup the phone started" to one tap from the phone: after the code is shown and he ticks "I have written this code down somewhere safe", the phone's finish button makes the PC write the file - no step on the PC. The one-time code the phone holds IS the authorisation for the phone that minted the run; the write still happens on the PC's disk, by the PC's own backend. `POST /api/backup/commit` therefore no longer refuses a caller for not being the PC: a live run minted by `prepare`, its exact code and the 15-minute TTL are what authorise it, and a wrong or missing code (400) or a dead run (409) still writes nothing. This deliberately relaxes the 2026-10-13 rule "a phone may ask, only the PC may write"; the residual risk - the "I saved it" tick is a phone-side promise, so a modified or misused phone could make the PC write a backup whose code nobody kept and push the oldest of the five kept out - is recorded in `docs/DECISIONS.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 8, with the owner's acceptance. The PC's typed-code block in Settings, Backups stays as the fallback for a run the phone left waiting, on the same route: no new route, and no approval card. The run is claimed under the module's lock before the write, so two presses write ONE file, and the phone's screen now says "Nothing has been written yet" before the press and "Your PC wrote the backup" only after the PC reports a file (the dialog used to claim it the moment it opened). **Hardened the same day on Gemini's ruling:** the finish is BOUND to the phone that minted the run or to the PC - `prepare` records the asking device's existing identity (`handler._jarvis_device`; no new header or token) and another paired device holding the same code is refused 403, writes nothing and does not spend the run, while a run minted with no recorded identity falls back to the code (said plainly in the code and the API doc); and a commit that cannot write now always records `last_attempt.ok: false` - including the 409 when the folder has gone, which used to record nothing - so a failed run is never shown as the previous run's success, while a truncated `.jbak` is never counted as a valid backup. The phone's screen, live run and TalkBack wording are a field-test row, not a pass.
- **A ninth personality preset: Patient Advocate, for doctor visits, lab results, medicines and insurance letters (2026-10-24).** `contract/personalities.json` gains `advocate` ("Patient Advocate", badge "Calm clinical translator", humour 1 / length thorough / formality warm / first name, Kokoro `af_heart:0.65+af_bella:0.35` at 0.94x), regenerated into `backend/jarvis_manner.py` and its byte-identical `jarvis-backend/` twin by `tools/gen_personalities.py`, and carried in the desktop's own copy (`jarvis-desktop/src/personalities.js`) and both `plain-error-cases.json` fixtures. Its prompt asks for plain-English medical and billing words, facts kept apart from guesses, three questions for the care team, and never a diagnosis or advice to stop a medicine. The assembled persona block measures **726 characters** with the longest word on every dial plus the spoken-live note, inside the 750 cap, and `tools/check_personalities.py` now measures that worst case for every preset. The crisis block still replaces all persona text for all nine. Unproven: the wording on a real device, the blended voice (Hear Sample still plays the base voice), and TalkBack.
- **A pairing card can no longer outlive its session, and a retried approval key keeps the waiter that stores it (2026-10-24, audit B-1/B-2).** Cancelling a pairing, starting a new one or letting the 600 seconds run out now takes the waiting `pair_device` card off `/api/pending` on both apps, so pressing Approve on it later is refused instead of reporting success while minting no key; the approval-key close-out withdraws the gate's real card id, and a retry of the same phone key joins the one card already waiting, so the owner's single Windows Hello yes still stores the key.
- **A pairing code stays hidden from screen capture even after an app-lock unlock (2026-10-24, desktop).** The pairing panel's capture guard is now a reason inside the one lock rule instead of a second writer, so the unlock sweep can no longer clear it while the code is still valid. While a code is shown, the Jarvis windows stay out of any screen share or recorder.
- **The medical record gets its own encrypted file, a 30-minute local-only latch, a place in your backups and a local MedGemma second reader - and says out loud what it cannot do (2026-10-24).** `backend/jarvis_medical_advocate.py` (shipped whole, no route, no screen) holds four record kinds in `~/.openjarvis/medical/health.db`, its own folder and file rather than another name in `memory.db`; every read and write sets `jarvis_gate.latch_taint(why="health_data")` *before* opening anything and fails closed with `MedicalStoreUnavailable` if it cannot, so a medical read pins the chat to local models for the gate's 30 minutes and no longer. The owner answered the open question the same day - "I wanted it encrypted as well and part of that file" - so every record field (label, dates, value, unit, source, note) is now sealed as one JSON payload per row with AES-256-GCM under a fresh nonce, with the kind and a random row label as associated data, and the 32-byte key lives in Windows Credential Manager under its own entry, `Jarvis Backend/medical key`, made on first use through the same `jarvis_chat_log.CredentialKey` helper chat history and the review decks use; a missing, short, broken or replaced key refuses in plain words before anything is read or written, a file from the earlier unencrypted version is refused rather than read or overwritten, and the tests write a distinctive secret then scan the raw database bytes (and any journal beside it) to prove no record word reaches the disk in plain text. What still sits in plain sight is stated: the file's name and folder, its row numbers, a random per-row label, when each row was made, and the audit rows' action, kind and counts - never a record's words. `health.db` is in `jarvis_backup.SOURCE_DBS` (not `NOT_BACKED_UP`) and travels under `db/medical/health.db`, its own folder, with its key inside the encrypted archive, so a restore on a new PC whose Credential Manager is empty opens the record; a second round-trip proves the harder case - the new PC had already minted its own medical key, the archive's key replaces it and the record still opens, with a stale -wal beside the restored file removed - and the backup suite's pinned counts moved from 17 + 8 to 18 + 7. `erase_all()` deletes only that file's rows (secure delete, a truncated write-ahead log, then a vacuum) and does NOT need the key (a lost or replaced key must never stop the owner wiping the record), a test proves a neighbouring `memory.db` is byte-for-byte untouched, a sealed payload moved between rows is refused rather than decoded, and the store is still NOT covered by a non-expiring latch. The lane client is the one HTTP path already in `jarvis_second_card` (a new `model=`/`keep_alive=` argument, default request byte-identical), probes `127.0.0.1:11435` in 0.20 s, refuses any other host, strips MedGemma's leaked `<unused94> ... <unused95>` thinking markers, and on any failure returns a calm fallback naming the main local model (:11436); its request timeout is 120 s, not the 25 s it first shipped with, because a measured answer took 28.35 s and 25 s cut it off. `validate_bundle()` checks a named subset of FHIR R4 (32 checks: Bundle, required elements and required-bound status values, id/instant shapes, reference syntax and in-bundle resolution, Observation value/unit/range/flag/component contradictions, duplicate rows and a magnitude heuristic, and code SHAPE) plus `summarise()`; it reports `no_errors_found` / `errors_found` / `not_checked` - never "valid" - always lists code meanings under `unverified`, and an optional local model is a second reader with `authority: "none"` whose junky, advice-giving or invented-code answers are dropped and can never change the structural report. `docs/MEDICAL-STORE.md` is the beginner's page (the lane's one-line start command, the honest limits, and the one decision still open); 95 hermetic medical tests and the backup suite's real new-PC round-trip hold all of it, and no federal API, outbound de-identifier, backup toggle, biometric lock screen or phone/desktop surface was built.
- **The phone's persistent Live stream now has its PC half: the transport and one whole turn, switched OFF (2026-10-24, Slice G/G2+G2b of `docs/DESIGN-PHONE-LIVE-STREAM.md`).** The frozen route and switch were already there; this adds the smallest RFC 6455 WebSocket server the design needs (no new dependency - the backend is standard-library only and the lock file cannot be regenerated), with token and origin checked BEFORE the upgrade, 64 ms binary audio frames, a random epoch and a monotonic sequence that refuse replays, and one whole turn: the ring's WAV through the SAME one-shot Live check, the PC's own `/api/chat` over loopback with the message marked live, then `say()`'s PCM back as the design's frames. The speech hand-off is whole-WAV for now (first-fragment streaming is a later phase). **The switch ships OFF and nothing enables it**; the phone half and every real measurement are still to come, so latency, battery and recovery are NOT-MEASURED.
- **The tools Jarvis is built with move to their current releases (2026-10-24).** The GitHub Actions that build and test the project move to their newest major versions (checkout 7, setup-python 7, setup-node 7, setup-java 6, upload-artifact 7, download-artifact 8), and the desktop app's Tauri stack moves to 2.12.5 with its build helper 2.7.4. Nothing you can see changes; this only keeps the build tools current. The upload/download changes are first exercised by the next full release build, not by this pull request's own checks.
- **A claim can no longer read as built when its test skipped the thing (2026-10-24, tools).** `tools/check_claims.py` proved a `test:` row from the exit code alone, and `backend/test_data_backup.py` exits 0 after printing a skip when the guard it would test is absent - so removing the NordLocker guard left claim F11 printed as built/holds. The checker now reads the skip lines a suite prints and withholds proof from any row naming a suite that skipped a check about the code rather than this machine. The environment skips the shipped suites really print (the Windows ACL fixture, a missing `cryptography` package, a module not importable here) stay proven, so the register is still green: 146 claims, 131 built. Its honest limit: a substantive skip in a suite withholds proof from every row naming that suite, and a new environmental skip with a new wording makes its row red until its reason is added to the allow-list - loud, never silent.
- **Past approvals show when they were decided, on both apps (2026-10-24).** The live `/api/pending` history rows carry `created`/`decided` - measured at 13:52 PT as exactly `id,action,tier,state,created,decided,decided_by`, a decided row's `decided` being a number like `1792875072.03678` - while the desktop (`brain.js` `activityWhen`) and the phone (`GateHistory.kt`) both read `decided_at`, which no row carried: a decided card showed the raise time as its decision time, or no time at all. Both readers now take `decided_at` first and fall back to `decided` (and `created_at`, then `created`, for the raise time), which is the whole fix against the measured row - no backend or patch change was needed or made, because the published base is never copied over the live install and both apps are the only readers of those names. `jarvis-desktop/tests/activity-when.mjs` lifts the real reader and pins an old-shape row and a row carrying both names; `PastApprovalsTest.kt` does the same for the Kotlin reader.
- **A slow approval click no longer gives up before the PC does (2026-10-24, desktop).** When a card does not say how long is left, the desktop waited 180 seconds for the answer, but the PC's own Windows Hello prompt is bounded at 900 seconds for the four cards that never expire (pairing a device, a phone's approval key, restoring a backup) - so a slow click was abandoned around 185 seconds while the PC was still waiting. The desktop now waits the PC's own bound plus the few seconds for the answer to come back (905 seconds), and its ceiling matches. Nothing is approved sooner or automatically; a denial is still a denial.
- **Ten small fixes from the phone audit's second pass (2026-10-24).** None changes a word the owner reads of his own, and none touches a setting: a Resume/Retry/Cancel asked for on a stale link is now refused with the existing link sentence instead of being sent; a briefing notification writes its restart point before it rings, so a crash inside two seconds can no longer replay it; the swipe on an approval card now feels the same gates its buttons show, so a heavy card's swipe approves after its two-second gate instead of silently springing back, and a stale-link swipe no longer asks for a fingerprint; the sandbox switch keeps reading while a card waits, so a denied or timed-out card stops reading as "waiting"; "Brief me now" keeps the setups and the senders switch the plate had already read instead of claiming no briefing exists; with the memory lists hidden, Goals no longer shows or speaks the PC's lock/reached/follows lines; a failed gallery read shows the PC's own sentence instead of "run apply-patches.ps1"; a share no longer doubles on a rotation and a sent photo does not come back; the phone's screen-capture guard now outlasts the screen-change fade instead of clearing with it; a limits or attention change and a model switch/install now wait as long as the approval card they raise (up to 20 minutes) instead of reporting "took too long" after 15 seconds; and a face whose shader will not compile costs that face, not the app - the default face is drawn instead of crashing at every launch, and one per-frame brush that was rebuilt 60 times a second is now built once. Runtime behaviour stays NOT-TESTABLE here (no emulator): every desk test is a JUnit or source pin, never a phone pass.
- **The phone stops blaming the pairing key for PC-only cards, and stops a recording it was told to cancel (2026-10-24).** Four small fixes from tonight's full audit, plus one data-rule fix: a card that can only be approved on the PC now says so in its own words instead of "your pairing key was refused" and a trip to the pairing screen, and all eight PC-only actions are known in one place; closing "Add a voice" (or picking a file) now stops a recording already running, instead of letting the take reappear in the next attempt; "Train my voice" and "hey Jarvis" refuse to open a second microphone beside Jarvis Live, so Live's own spoken answer can no longer land in the voice-print clip; a dead microphone now stops Live with the reason instead of spinning at full CPU while holding it open; and Send waits for a photo that is still being prepared, so the photo cannot ride the next question. No route, no new read, no write. Runtime behaviour is NOT-TESTABLE here (no emulator) and stays a field-test row, never a pass.
- **"People and things" past dates no longer mix, and the phone's Galaxy counts each fact once (2026-10-24).** Two bugs from tonight's full audit, both fixed in the phone's read-only code: tapping a different "as of" date now drops the previous date's facts immediately, so the heading and the list always describe the same day - even while the new read is running, and after a read that fails; and the Galaxy band's "N facts" line now counts each saved fact once instead of once per name, which is what its own promise said. No new read, no write, and nothing changes on screen when one fact names two people. Runtime look and TalkBack order remain a field-test row, not a pass.
- **A changelog entry is now one small file per pull request, rolled into the big list when it is safe (2026-10-23).** Every pull request used to append its entry at the top of `CHANGELOG.md` - one shared line, so two pull requests always conflicted and every merge to `main` dirtied the rest; a conflicted pull request gets no CI, and re-clearing one costs a fresh ~71-minute `frontend` run (one night lost six). Now a pull request writes ONE bullet file under `changelog.d/` (`YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md`) and never touches `CHANGELOG.md`; on a quiet `main`, `py -3 tools/roll_changelog.py` inserts the bullets newest first under "Not in a numbered version yet" and removes the fragment files, in ONE pull request with nothing else in it. `--check` validates a fragment's name and shape on every PR and never demands that it has already been rolled (that would fail the very pull requests the fragments exist for); `--dry-run` shows the insertion and writes nothing. The roll is deliberate, never automatic, and idempotent. Proven by `backend/test_roll_changelog.py` (20 checks: the good shape, every bad shape named one by one, newest-first insertion, existing entries kept, fragments removed only after the text is read back, a second roll is a no-op, dry-run writes nothing, the real exit codes).
- **Four approval cards now wait for you instead of expiring; every other card keeps its 15 minutes (2026-10-23, rescue #4 narrowed to the owner's ruling Q1).** `rescue/card-expiry-never-expires` (2026-10-13) made **23** gate actions' cards never expire; the owner's ruling refused that scope, so `backend/no-expiry-cards.patch` exempts only the three interactive ceremonies that need you at the PC: **pairing a device** (`pair_device`, `unretire_shared_key`), **registering a phone's approval key** (`register_approval_key`) and **restoring a backup** (`restore_backup`). Every other card - including "forget a time frame" and every switch that loosens a rule (web search, reading tools, notifications, learning, limits, the voice bar) - keeps `#378`'s 15-minute window. There is **no way to ask for no deadline**: the decision is `timeout_for(action)` keyed on the gate's OWN action ids, there is no flow argument, and `None`, `0`, a negative number, a malformed name or an unknown flow name all get the ordinary 900 seconds. `pending()` leaves `expires_in` OFF for those four (never `0`, which both apps read as "expired now"), the start-up sweep no longer ages them out, and a new `jarvis_gate.withdraw()` takes a cancelled or replaced pairing/approval-key card off the list; `jarvis_owner_check.py` asks the gate before treating an absent field as "the shipped timeout", so Windows Hello no longer answers 409 GONE for a card the gate is still waiting on. **Waiting is still not approving:** nothing runs until a person answers, a denial is still a denial, and the re-read after the Windows Hello prompt still refuses a card that is no longer the live one. The two shipped-module halves are carried to `jarvis-backend/` byte-for-byte. **Honest limits:** a restore card whose waiting thread died (an app restart) stays pending until denied or re-raised - the one case where a card is answerable but nothing is listening; that is the cost of "no clock" and it is said in `backend/README.md`. Tests: `backend/test_gate_no_expiry.py` (10 tests, 170 checks: the four required proofs plus approval/denial/withdrawal, the sweep, `expires_in`, and the Windows Hello half); `backend/test_gate_no_expiry.py` applies the patch to the published base with `git apply` and imports the result. Docs: `docs/JARVIS-API.md` (`expires_in`), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2, `backend/README.md`.
- **DeepSeek's own Search API is a sixth web-search provider, opt-in and key-only, and SearXNG is still the default (2026-10-23).** Jarvis already offered five searches (SearXNG, DuckDuckGo, Exa, Tavily, Brave); this adds `deepseek`, off until the owner chooses it in **Settings, Web search** on the PC (or says "use DeepSeek for web search"). It calls `POST https://api.deepseek.com/v1/tools/search` with `Authorization: Bearer <key>` and only `{"query", "count": 5, "need_content": false}` - the guide's optional conversation question and address field are deliberately never sent - and turns the answer's `summary` (written for a model) into the 300-character snippet, with `snippet` as the fallback; a null `date` is harmless and other fields are unused. Its **key lives in Windows Credential Manager** as `Jarvis Backend/DeepSeek Search key`, written on the PC only by the desktop's Settings or `py -3 jarvis_search.py key deepseek`, never over HTTP, never logged, sent only to `api.deepseek.com`. The **owner's costs and limits**, from DeepSeek's own guide: **$0.75 per 1,000 successful searches** (failed searches and searches that find nothing are not billed), a **1 to 128 character** query (a longer search is refused in `plan()` before any card or socket), and **100 searches a second** per account. A 401 says the key is wrong or cancelled, 402 says the account is out of balance and where to add credit, 429 says too many searches, and 408/5xx say DeepSeek had a problem and to try again; **no retry is sent automatically**, the same as the other five. Both apps list it last in the PC's order and both carry the same words; the phone still has **no key box**. Setup, "What Jarvis can reach", "tell me when a search shows something new", "which search should I use?" and the feature guide all name it. Tests: `backend/test_web_search.py` (398 checks: the no-key refusal with no socket, the recorded two-result answer with a null date and an empty `results` list, every documented error code, the 128-character bound, a redirect carrying no key, and the oversized-answer cap) plus the vault, reach, setup, feature and twin/base checkers. **Honest limits:** no request reached the real API - the owner's key is not in this container and must not be, so the network and every error path are **NOT-TESTABLE** and stubbed; the request/answer shapes and the price come from DeepSeek's guide (read 2026-10-23), not from a live call.
- **Settings -> "How Jarvis talks" now has the eight personality presets, four tuning dials and "My personalities" (2026-10-23, Personality Presets SLICE 2a).** The Settings window draws a grid of the eight built-in presets - Warm Collaborator (the default), Classic British Butler, Tactical Mission Control, Dry & Deadpan, Sharp Tech Partner, Socratic Coach, No-Nonsense Critic, Calm & Grounded - each with its badge and title, one-line description and sample line, the paired voice badge (e.g. "British Baritone Blend · 1.02x"), a **Hear Sample** button and **Use This Personality**, which shows "In use" on the active one. Below the grid sit the four dials as pill selectors: **Wit & Humor** (None / Subtle / Dry & Sharp / Playful), **Response Length** (Concise / Balanced / Thorough), **Formality** (Casual / Warm Peer / Formal Butler / Tactical) and **How Jarvis Addresses You** (First Name / "Sir" / "Ma'am" / Boss / Captain / No Title), plus the **"Switch Jarvis's spoken voice to match the chosen personality"** switch. **"My personalities"** saves the owner's own mixes: a name, one line about how it should sound, one example exchange, and a base preset to start from (the 240-character cap on each line, and Delete asks first and sends the explicit `confirm: true`). Every change is ONE field on the existing `/api/manner` route through the existing Settings-only `set_manner` command - no new route, no new permission, no new approval card, held on a stale link like every other change sent to the PC. Hear Sample asks the PC for the sound through the existing `hear_voice_sample` command and the PC's `/api/voice/voices/sample` route; the page never makes a sound itself. **The sample plays each preset's base voice for now:** the PC's sample route accepts exactly `{"voice"}` and refuses any other field, so the request carries the base voice only - the character's blend and speed stay stored and shown in its badge, a blended-voice preview arrives with the Kokoro/Live voice slice, and the page says so under the grid rather than promising a sound it cannot play. A custom preset that was saved by another client (the PC can mint a `custom-…` id) keeps the PC's own id, so Use and Delete reach it instead of missing it. **What is deferred:** the canonical values live in the backend lane's `contract/personalities.json` and its extended `/api/manner` answer - until that lands, the page draws its own transcript of the blueprint's eight presets and keeps the new controls visible but disabled with the plain "run apply-patches.ps1" sentence, while the older Warm/Plain choices keep working exactly as before; a custom preset's voice comes from the base preset it starts from (a separate voice-blend picker is deferred). The HUD quick-switch pill is NOT in this change (it is the separate HUD-page commit). Tests: `jarvis-desktop/tests/personalities.mjs` (19 checks: the eight ids/order, voice blends summing to 1, the four dials, the PC's answer winning, the local fallback, 240/60 limits, the explicit delete confirm, a server-shaped custom id surviving verbatim for Use/Delete, and the sample request carrying exactly the base voice), `jarvis-desktop/tests/personality-settings.mjs` (14 checks in a headless browser: the eight cards and the active one, Hear Sample's one-field request and the page's base-voice note, Use sends the id alone, a custom preset saved elsewhere keeping the PC's id through Use and Delete, a dial sends the four dials, sync sends `voice_sync`, custom save/delete with the owner's confirm, per-row accessible names, the older-PC fallback and that the two old choices still send exactly `{manner}`), and the Rust unit `plain_errors::tests::personality_change_bodies`; the shipped control test `jarvis-desktop/tests/custom-voices.mjs` also pins the sample body to exactly `json!({ "voice": voice })` in `hear_voice_sample`. **Honest limits:** no real WebView2 window was driven (headless Playwright with a stubbed bridge), the look is not visually reviewed yet, and no real backend has answered the new fields at this desk.
- **The Brain window's Galaxy now has three flat layouts beside the constellation (2026-10-23, Galaxy rework Phase G0).** A new row of choices in the Galaxy bar - and the keys **6, 7, 8** while the picture has focus - switch the map between the **constellation** that ships today, a **Star map** (every name on one flat plane, rings at R = 34/68/108 and seven faint spokes), a **Clean tree** (four stationary front-facing rows, read top to bottom) and a **Zen ring** (the best-known name in the centre, the rest on one R = 108 halo with inward curves). Pressing a layout's key again returns to the constellation. The five rich 3D presets the blueprint lists after these are shown in the same row as **disabled "not built yet" choices** - never hidden, never faked, and their keys say so in plain words. The layouts only move the names the picture already draws: no new dot, line, label rule or node budget is added (the existing 240-name ceiling is unchanged and pinned by a test), and nothing goes to the PC - no route, no server write, no new dependency, no 3D, no remote file. The choice is remembered **on this device** (a stored preference in the window); a corrupt or old value falls back to the constellation, and the same is true if a layout ever fails its own safety check. The constellation's own **Fit** button and `0` key now keep the wider margin a flat layout needs, so the outer names are never clipped after a switch or a window resize. **Also fixed in passing:** the constellation's labels were half a stage to the left of their dots (`margin-left: -50%` resolved against the stage's width instead of the label's own), so the default view's names now sit under their dots where they were always meant to; that was a bug that shipped before G0 and it visibly changes the view that ships today. The five rich presets, the same three layouts in the big HUD window, the icon packs and the phone version are later phases. **Honest limits:** how the guide drawing and the labels look on the real WebView2 window and monitor is NOT-TESTABLE here - the browser checks measure boxes and positions, not the owner's screen, and that is a field-test row, never a pass. Docs: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §8, `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-23). Tests: `jarvis-desktop/tests/galaxy-presets.mjs` (determinism, the three shapes, the outward growth, the 240 cap, no overlap at every size, the safe fallback, a corrupt stored value, the real window's choices/hotkeys/reload, and the real 32-name graph's readable labels) plus the existing Galaxy suites.
- **The phone now has the same "All features and abilities" directory as the desktop (2026-10-23, feature-controls B-side, #749).** Settings on the Android app gains ONE read-only, searchable row listing all 139 features in the PC's own five labels (40 Switchable/Adjustable, 2 Settings-Only, 16 PC-Only, 69 Command-Only, 12 Always-On), each with its name, what it is for, its real example phrase, its screen path and - where the PC really has a switch - the switch it names. It is a DIRECTORY, not a control panel: it draws no toggle, changes nothing and calls no route; every word comes from `contract/feature-controls.json`, generated into `FeatureDirectory.kt` by `jarvis-client/tools/gen_feature_directory.py` (never hand-typed), so the phone cannot drift from the desktop's copy. Saying "open all features and abilities" on the phone lands on it (a phone-only registry Section - the desktop's own card has no section id of its own). The feature-controls contract's phone evidence is now live: the directory rows no longer say " (planned)" on the phone half, `check_feature_controls.py` refuses a re-added marker on either half (with a bite), and the `topics` row's phone evidence records that the phone's Settings search now names it (its status stays planned - the typed setter is still pending). Docs: the README's "desktop-only" limit, ARCHITECTURE section 8's one-sided row and SETTINGS-BY-CONVERSATION are corrected. Tests: `backend/test_feature_controls.py` (15 bite cases including the new "(planned)" refusal), `backend/test_settings_registry.py` (52/52 sections and the phone row's search guard), `FeatureDirectoryTest.kt` (139 rows, the five labels with the corrected 40/2/16/69/12 counts, no fake control, every row findable), `SettingsSearchTest`/`SettingsJumpTest`/`OpenPlaceTest` (the row is searched, jumped to and opened), and `jarvis-desktop/tests/feature-directory.mjs` (both halves live). **Honest limits:** the runtime screen was NOT-TESTABLE here (no local Android build; CI compiles and runs the JVM tests only), so the first real look on a phone is a field-test row; the directory says where a control is and never offers one, and 30 of the 40 switchable rows are still planned.
- **A note saved from web text is now remembered as such, so reading it back is treated as outside text (2026-10-23, Web-Gap 4).** When a note is saved in a turn that read outside text (already carded and approved), a small PC-side registry keeps only the note's **reference** and the time - never a copy of any note - in `notes-taint.json` beside the settings. A later notes search marks each matching result `source_taint: outside_web`, wraps its snippet in `<untrusted_web_derived_note>` and latches the same process-wide taint every other outside read sets, so the model is told plainly that this is data, never instructions. The owner's Obsidian/Joplin/Logseq store is **never edited** and no save is refused; the registry is bounded (200 references) and pruned (90 days), and a missing or damaged registry is safely empty. Tests: `backend/test_note_multihop_taint.py` (30 checks: normalisation, TTL/cap, damaged-file safety, all three writers' own reference, the vault and Joplin read paths, wrapping once, the agent's latch, and the outside-shaped-only write record; four mutation bites proven). **Honest limit:** the generic "this came from a tool" label already existed for every search result - this adds the specific provenance and marker, it does not replace that.
- **Backups no longer carry a voice profile that could not work, and a restore refuses unsafe or corrupt archives (2026-10-23, security/backup hardening).** Two changes, both fail-closed. **(1) The voice profile is left out of `.jbak`.** It is locked to this PC with a Windows Credential Manager key that a bare-metal restore does not have, so carrying it restored files the owner believed were back while they were dead; the manifest now says, in plain words, that the voice is not carried and to enrol it again on a new PC. An older archive that still contains sealed voice files is checked on this PC: a file this PC cannot open is **not written** and the restore reports re-enrolment instead of counting it as restored; a file this PC can open still restores normally. **(2) A restore refuses a whole archive before touching a single live file** when it holds a link, an absolute or drive path, a `..` in any position, an empty/NUL name, or a member outside the folders a backup carries - and it checks every staged database's SQLite header plus `PRAGMA quick_check` and `integrity_check` before the first live file is replaced. Any failure leaves every current file exactly as it was. Tests: `backend/test_backup.py` (traversal checks strengthened to whole-archive refusal) and `backend/test_web_rogue_and_backup_hardening.py` (45 checks, including three restore refusals that prove the live files are untouched, and the voice not-written/re-enrol cases). **Honest limits:** a real bare-metal restore on a second Windows PC cannot be tested here; that is why the voice is not carried rather than carried unusably.
- **The big window's Galaxy map now stops drawing when nobody is looking (2026-10-23, the idle-power review).** Once the Brain map had been opened, the animated picture kept being redrawn for as long as the window was open - even after switching back to the Conversation view, where the map is not on screen at all, and with no check for a hidden or covered window. Now one guard decides: the map draws only while the Brain map is the view on screen **and** the window is visible. Leaving the map or hiding the window stops it; coming back starts it again and re-measures the picture once (so a resize while hidden cannot leave it at the old size). A hidden window also pauses the page's two endless animations - the blinking answer caret and the pulsing push-to-talk button - so nothing keeps being drawn for a window nobody can see. **Nothing live is paused:** the event stream and its reconnect, the approval reads, speech and the animated face keep running; the face was already unloaded when the Talk view ends, and the Brain window was already clean (its picture settles in about a second and then stops) and is now pinned by a test. No new route, permission, card, dependency or deploy step of its own is added - the page rides the normal shipped-file copy. **Honest limit:** what a real minimized or covered window does on this PC, and the power saving itself, are owner-assisted measurements - NOT-TESTABLE here; the automated proof is that the page asks for no drawing steps while the map is hidden or off screen and starts again when it is back. Tests: `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud.mjs` and `jarvis-desktop/tests/galaxy-dots.mjs`; the three HUD copies stay byte-identical.
- **The Daily Pulse now has real money, study and PC-health lines, read on this PC (2026-10-23, P3 seam B5-c).** Wave D-1's Pulse had a money / study / PC-health half that only a caller could fill, so in the live briefing it always said those sources were "not checked". A new shipped module, `jarvis_pulse_sources.py`, supplies them through `jarvis_briefing.live_deps()`: a **count** of bank statements whose layout is waiting (`jarvis_spending`), the decks' own "3 cards ready" line and the fading-topic **count** (`jarvis_decks`), the Pillar 28 silent-failure **titles** (`jarvis_pc_help`), and - only when the owner turned the radar on and its store exists - one "a watched price changed" line with no route, airline or amount. Reading nothing new: no socket, no model, no route, no card, no permission or grant; every call reads a store this PC already keeps. **What can never reach the Pulse:** an amount, a bank file name or path, a deck name, a song/route/airline or a finding's long paragraph - counts and titles only. App lock / "Hide memory lists and chat history" withholds the whole block, and an unreadable lock state counts as locked. Each source is bounded and fail-soft, so one broken source cannot stop the briefing - and the briefing then says it was not checked instead of showing 0; a source that WAS read with nothing to say is no longer called "not checked" either. Tests: `backend/test_pulse_sources.py` (45 checks: the extras shape, one source at a time, a raising and a slow source, fail-closed lock, the no-leak rule on fake stores stuffed with private strings, and the `pulse_from`/briefing integration). Docs: `docs/JARVIS-API.md` §22, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. **Honest limits:** the live PC gets it on the next backend deploy (shipped-file sync, no desktop/phone rebuild); the quiet-thread list and the prep people/notes are still caller-supplied in v1; the radar is off by default, so its line is absent until the owner turns it on.
- **A safety latch that survives a restart can now be read, read-only, and it shows on the HUD (2026-10-23, Seam A4).** `/api/status` carries two new fields, **`taint_latched`** and **`rush_latched`**: the two persistent session latches the gate already keeps on this PC (the taint latch is a row in its SQLite database; the rush latch is a small JSON state file), so a screen can stay honest across a backend restart instead of going quiet with no waiting card. They are **true / false / null**, where `null` means "the PC has not said" (never "not latched") and a taint read that fails answers `true`, matching the gate's own fail-closed rule. **Reading them does nothing else:** nothing is cleared, approved or decided, no route, permission, card or grant is added, `/api/status` is already on the HUD's fixed allowlist, and there is no control that clears either latch. A missing module answers `null`. **The HUD's fixed `#hud-safety` strip draws them now**, in its own fourth chip and plain words ("This session is staying on your PC." / "Something Jarvis read tried to rush you recently. Approvals ask for one more look until it clears."), fed from the `/api/status` reads the page already makes - no new fetch, permission, command, card or control, and the stale-link Approve/Deny rule is untouched. Docs: `docs/JARVIS-API.md` `/api/status`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (H4's persistent-latch honest limit replaced). Tests: `backend/test_status_latches.py` (19 checks: both latches survive a module reload - the restart proof - the route's own lifted block carries them, a second read clears neither, no clearing/approving call is made (checked with `ast`), the honest `null`/fail-closed answers, and the warm-cache route makes no probe), `backend/test_status_cache.py` (payload keys), `backend/test_reconcile_live_backend.py` (224 checks: the chained `hud-status-latches` literal delta lands after the cached-route delta, is idempotent, and refuses a half-applied live file), `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud-safety.mjs` (words matrix, only `true` draws, `null` is never read as "not latched", chip order, no jargon) and the H4/A4 block in `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud.mjs` (both latches armed show one chip; a `null`/false answer stays silent; no control appears; a stale link still holds only Approve); `tests/csp-inline.mjs` count unchanged and the three HUD copies stay byte-identical. **Honest limits:** the live PC reaches all of it only when the A4 backend deploy runs (the HUD literal-delta path in `tools/hud-deltas/` + `reconcile_live_backend.py`; that deploy is not this wave), the installed-WebView2 look is not tested here (Playwright/source-level only), and the pre-existing full-`run_suites.py` hang seen at a desk is environmental, not this change.
- **The HUD now has a fixed Stop everything control that no layout can hide or move (2026-10-22, owner-approved).** The big window's stage bar carries a native **Stop** button (`#hud-stop`) outside every card, region and the saved layout document, so Cockpit/Studio/Ambient/Quiet/Telemetry/Second Brain/Minimal, an imported layout and Edit HUD all show it and none can remove it. It calls the SAME Rust command as the **Alt+Shift+X** hotkey, the tray and the widget (`screen_work::stop_everything` -> `commands::stop_everything_now`): Jarvis's speech and the running turn stop at once, `/api/stop_all` is asked with the token Rust already holds, and nothing is approved or started - there is no approval card and no confirmation step, matching the existing stop controls. It is deliberately never held: a stale link and App lock do not disable it. The only permission added is the least-privilege HUD set **`hud-stop`** (`allow-stop-everything`); the HUD page still holds no pairing token, and the broad `task-control` set is NOT granted. H4 may later move the button into the fixed safety rail, with no second control. Tests: `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud.mjs` (click sends exactly one `stop_everything`, no approval card; visible/enabled in all six shells and seven presets, in Edit HUD and after a real Import-dialog import; a stale link does not disable it), the HUD source pin and the ACL/checker battery; the three HUD copies stay byte-identical and the inline-script count stays 4. **Honest limits:** the HUD window can still be hidden by App lock (the hotkey/tray/widget remain), and real WebView2/touch behaviour is NOT-TESTABLE here.
- **Read-only background steps no longer wait for a card, and outbound drafts get two tiers of protection (2026-10-22, Duet Wave D-2).** `jarvis_jobs.create_plan()` runs a plan's read-only, independent steps while another step waits on an approval card, queues everything behind that card, halts on Stop at a step boundary, and finishes with `review_status: "ready_for_review"` so only you mark it reviewed; it never approves anything. `jarvis_scrub.classify_outbound_sensitivity()` refuses card numbers, government ids, vault handles, API keys and pairing tokens even when asked (Tier 1), and health or personal-finance figures unless the turn is untainted and your own prompt explicitly asks for that category (Tier 2); `jarvis_email_draft.plan()` runs it before a card is built, and `jarvis_browser_control` refuses brokerage trades, inter-account transfers and third-party account logins outright, before any card. Spending and document answers now carry `source_provenance` (`From: <file>`) in the summaries the model reads, withheld when the private lists are hidden or the app is locked. `jarvis_live` gains `stage_walk_brain_dump()` and `build_evening_report()`: a spoken walk brain-dump splits into prep items and items needing a card, and the evening bundle keeps the details hidden under the lock. Tests: `backend/test_duet_d2.py` (45 checks); docs: `docs/JARVIS-API.md` section 155.
- **Choosing a built-in HUD layout in Settings no longer deletes your saved layouts, and Ambient keeps its whole left side reachable (2026-10-22, H3 post-merge audit fixes).** A Settings preset used to save a fresh document with an empty `user_presets`, so one click silently wiped every layout in **My layouts**; the store now keeps the current library (and a Settings-page test proves a built-in pick, a saved-layout pick and Reset all keep it). Ambient (`center-core`) hid its left rail with no way to open it, so its cards - and the protected Jarvis-bar card in edit mode - were unreachable; it now has the same **[Navigation]** drawer the new shells use, and entering **Edit HUD** auto-reveals every hidden rail so the protected controls the HUD draws stay visible/reachable while editing (the layout document keeps all four protected ids forced visible; the HUD's fixed Stop everything control is its own entry above). The H3 page tests the PR claimed now exist: every preset's shell/density/opening tab, the imported `two-column-right` drawer, first-paint preview fallback, DPR/reduced motion at 900/1024/1280 and the Ambient drawer in edit mode; the stale H1/H2 comments and the over-broad "drawer for every rail" docs sentence are corrected.
- **Three safety facts now live in a fixed strip no layout can hide (2026-10-22, HUD H4: the safety rail).** The HUD's editable layouts can no longer hide or move the truth about **App lock** ("App lock is on. This window hides when you step away, and approvals wait until you're back."), **hidden memory lists**, the **queue-scoped rush/outside-text evidence** ("Something Jarvis read tried to rush you on the card that is waiting… Take your time — this can never be hidden.", disappearing with the card) or **catching up with the PC** ("…Approvals wait for the link; **Deny still works**."). `#hud-safety` is one fixed strip owned by the shell, outside every editable region: not a card, not in `CARD_REGISTRY`, no layout-document entry — so no preset, import, hand-edited settings file or edit-mode action can hide it (a hostile import is dropped, and a non-default preset and edit mode keep it, and it says "Always visible — no layout can hide this" while editing). App lock / hidden lists are read through the ALREADY-GRANTED `get_lock_flags` (two booleans, no token); the rush banner is derived from the pending queue's `raised` object the page already polls, and it never claims the PC-only persistent latch is armed (that read is a later follow-up). While the link is stale the card's **Approve is disabled** with the words tied to it and **Deny stays enabled**, matching Rust's own rule that only an approved decision is held; an offline link shows Rust's own sentence. **No new command, permission, capability or deploy**; one new external module (`hud-safety.js`), three byte-identical HUD copies, inline-script count still 4, CSP untouched. Honest limits: the lock flags are polled (boot/focus/visibility/a 20 s beat) so the chip can lag by that beat; the real locked-window state is the shell hiding the HUD, so no in-page locked overlay is shown by design; the real Windows Hello/lock-screen experience is a field-test row (NOT-TESTABLE in CI — never a pass). Tests: `tests/hud-safety.mjs` plus the H4 block in `tests/hud.mjs` (catching up + Deny reaches `decide_approval`, offline, App-lock/hidden chips with no card raised, escaped rush quote with no DOM injection, edit mode cannot hide it, geometry at 1280×820 and 1024×768 at DPR 1 and 900×600 at DPR 1.5 with click-through checks on the bar launcher, quickbar, composer and the approvals buttons, healthy edit mode keeping the strip, and reduced motion).
- **Reminders can now be edited, moved and reordered in the HUD and the Brain, as the desktop half of the scheduler's Phase 2 (2026-10-22, HUD Reminders Phase 2 UI).** The HUD's Reminders panel and the Brain's Coming up gain ONE `Edit` button on an editable row (timer, alarm, reminder, to-do - active or paused), and `Move up`/`Move down` on a to-do inside its own list. Edit opens an inline editor: a timer's words and minutes -> `seconds`; a one-off alarm/reminder's words and date/time (cardless, like pause); a repeating alarm/reminder's words and repeat rule, which is the ONE `schedule_repeat` 202 card - the asking sentence appears before the answer, the old rule stays on screen and running until the owner answers, and nothing is ever claimed early; a to-do's words and list, where a list-only save is the backend's `move` verb and a list riding with words is the one `edit`. Up/Down send the PC's own rank +/-1 and are disabled at the ends of the list. Nothing new was granted: the same `hud_schedule_act`/`brain_schedule_act` commands take an optional typed `change` (`deny_unknown_fields`, Rust caps mirroring the PC's), the stale-link hold is unchanged, the HUD still holds no pairing token, the App-lock redaction still blanks the words (the new `rank` is a number and `phase2` a boolean), the three HUD copies are byte-identical and the inline-script count stays 4. Every control is drawn ONLY when the PC's own read says `"phase2": true`, so an older backend shows exactly the Phase 1 panel - never a fake control. A repeat edit's wait gets 1,200 s (the card is the answer; the same shape as the focus-preset apply) while every other schedule call keeps 20 s. **Honest limits:** no edit history or undo in v1, no drag/copy-paste, no new-list creation from the UI (the list picker offers the to-do list and the lists that exist), module-managed kinds (tellme/standby/briefing/today/nexttime/review) are still changed where they were set up, the phone stays read-only for now, and the real WebView2 window and the real card decision need the rebuilt app and the owner. Tests: the Phase 2 block in `jarvis-desktop/tests/hud.mjs` (marker gating, the three edits and their bodies, the 202 asking sentence, move-vs-edit, reorder and disabled ends, Escape/no-op) and in `tests/coming-up.mjs` (shared gate/rank, the Brain's editor sending ONE `change`, no controls on an older backend), plus Rust unit tests for `change_body`/`change_timeout` and the redaction test keeping the new numeric fields; docs: JARVIS-API section 21 and ARCHITECTURE sections 3 and 8.
- **The scheduler can now edit, move and reorder its to-dos, and the HUD Reminders backend half is ready for its UI (2026-10-22, HUD Reminders Phase 2 backend).** The shipped `jarvis_schedule.py` gains three single-job verbs on the existing `POST /api/schedule/act` - no new route, no patch-carried file changed, no live write by the author. **`edit`** changes one job all-or-nothing: the words, a one-off's own date/time (`when`/`at`/`date`+`time`) or a timer's length (`seconds`) are **cardless** because they move when one already-authorised thing happens; a **repeating** job's next time is refused 409 (change its repeat instead); a **`repeat` change** raises exactly ONE `schedule_repeat` 202 card and the OLD rule keeps running until the owner says yes (denied/timed-out changes nothing); module-managed kinds (tellme/standby/briefing/today) refuse edit because their module owns the body. **`move`** puts a to-do on another named list (or the to-do list, `null`) through the same `list_key()`/`MAX_LISTS` rules, appends it to the end and renumbers both lists. **`reorder`** takes an absolute integer `rank` (or `before`/`after`) within the current list and rewrites every open item's `rank` to 0..n-1 in one transaction; the identical request twice writes nothing.
